## Phase 1 completed

- Monorepo bootstrap: npm workspaces, Turborepo, TypeScript strict base
  config, ESLint flat config, Prettier, `.nvmrc`/`engines`/`.npmrc`
  (`engine-strict=true`) enforcing Node 24 LTS.
- `apps/web` (Next.js App Router, Tailwind v4, Ant Design v5 SSR registry,
  Redux Toolkit store shell, tRPC React client), `apps/api` (NestJS with
  the SWC CLI builder, hand-wired tRPC mount, structured-logger override),
  and `apps/worker` (pg-boss, health/heartbeat HTTP listener, graceful
  shutdown) scaffolded with no business screens or modules.
- `packages/database` (Prisma foundation only — datasource/generator, no
  models), `packages/contracts` (tRPC router shell), `packages/config`
  (Zod env validation, structured logger, redaction, correlation IDs),
  `packages/ui` (Tailwind v4 theme + Ant Design theme lifted verbatim from
  `15-ui-design-system.md`), `packages/accounting` (Decimal.js `Money` +
  business-date/timestamp primitives), `packages/testing` (Postgres
  transaction-rollback test helper) — all foundation-only, no business
  logic, and all compiled to CommonJS so Node's native TypeScript
  execution, Nest's SWC builder, and Turbopack all resolve them
  consistently.
- Structured console-based logger with redaction and correlation IDs
  wired into all three apps; `no-console` ESLint rule enforced everywhere
  except the logger module itself.
- Health endpoints: web readiness, API liveness/readiness (with live DB
  check via `SELECT 1`), worker heartbeat/last-job/pg-boss-connection —
  all verified responding under both `npm run dev` and PM2.
- `ecosystem.config.cjs` committed matching the documented runtime
  contract; verified under a real PM2 production smoke test (all three
  processes online, health endpoints reachable, worker exits gracefully
  on SIGINT well within its 30s `kill_timeout`).
- `.env.example` documented with placeholder values only; Zod-based
  fail-fast env validation wired into all three apps.
- GitHub Actions CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) running install, prisma
  generate, lint, typecheck, test, build, and migration validation against
  an ephemeral Postgres service container.
- Vitest adopted as the test runner for every workspace, including
  `apps/api` (via `unplugin-swc` for decorator/metadata support, since
  Nest's own testing module is runner-agnostic).

## Phase 2 sub-phase 2.4: HR masters + generic org-scoped masters API

```text
Date: 2026-07-28
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation
Task: Implemented internal_departments, designations, employment_types — the common-baseline shape (code/name/description/sort_order/is_active/audit-stamps/version) shared by every Phase 2 organization-scoped flat lookup master. Introduced one generic MasterPort/MastersService keyed by MasterTypeKey (switch-per-operation dispatch to the correct Prisma delegate, one shared row mapper) rather than a bespoke service per table, since 2.5 and 2.8 will add more tables of the identical shape. Enforces normalized (case/whitespace-insensitive) per-organization name uniqueness at the DB level, optimistic concurrency via compare-and-increment on `version` (VERSION_CONFLICT on a stale expectedVersion), and translates Postgres unique-constraint violations to VALIDATION_FAILED. Hit and resolved a stale advisory-lock/duplicate-migration issue from a leftover background `prisma migrate dev` process (see Known issues).
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260728104905_phase2_04_hr_masters/, packages/contracts/src/{context.ts,index.ts,router.ts}, packages/contracts/src/ports/{index.ts,master.port.ts}, packages/contracts/src/routers/masters.ts, apps/api/src/masters/*, apps/api/src/{app.module,main}.ts
Migrations: phase2_04_hr_masters — applied and verified against local Postgres; manually confirmed the normalized-name unique index rejects a case/whitespace-variant duplicate
Tests: apps/api/src/masters/masters.integration-spec.ts (4 tests: create+list-active-only, normalized-duplicate-name rejection, stale-version update rejection, deactivate-via-setActive excluding from the default list)
Decisions added/changed: ADR-037 (one generic masters API instead of per-table services — see docs/12-decisions-log.md)
Known issues: a background `prisma migrate dev` process from sub-phase 2.3 was left running and held Postgres's advisory migration lock, blocking this sub-phase's `migrate dev --create-only`; killing the stale process released it. Separately, because chk_cities_state_matches_district and uq_districts_id_state_id (added by hand in migration phase2_03_geography) aren't representable in schema.prisma's DSL, Prisma's diff engine proposed dropping them in this migration's auto-generated diff — the spurious drop was manually stripped before applying, and this will recur for every future `migrate dev --create-only` unless/until those constraints become expressible in Prisma's schema language.
Next task: Sub-phase 2.5 (CRM/tender/project masters: government_body_types, lead_types, lead_sources, tender_portals, project_types, security_instrument_purposes, security_instrument_types).
```

## Phase 2 sub-phase 2.5: CRM/tender/project masters

```text
Date: 2026-07-28
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation
Task: Implemented government_body_types, lead_types, lead_sources, tender_portals (schema/API only, no Phase 2B screen), project_types, security_instrument_purposes, and security_instrument_types (the latter two organization-seeded with EMD/SECURITY_DEPOSIT and CASH/BANK_GUARANTEE/FD/DD respectively). All extend the existing generic masters API from 2.4. Refactored MastersService's dispatch from a switch-per-operation (which would have reached 40 duplicated cases across 10 types, with 2.8 still to come) to a single delegate-registry function with one contained `as unknown as FlatMasterDelegate` cast, since Prisma's per-model delegate types can't be called through a union without it. Re-exported `Prisma` from @zitaware/database so apps/api can reference `Prisma.TransactionClient` without a new direct dependency. Also hit and resolved a second stale-migration-tracking issue (a rolled-back migration row blocking `migrate dev` even after its folder was deleted) and switched to a `--create-only` + `migrate deploy` workflow to stop Prisma's dev-mode auto-diff-and-apply from re-triggering the spurious-drop problem.

## Phase 2 sub-phase 2.6: legal entity, GST registrations, financial years, accounting periods

```text
Date: 2026-07-28
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation
Task: Implemented legal_entities, gst_registrations (hand-written 15-char GSTIN format CHECK), financial_years (container-only, partial-unique one-current-per-legal-entity index), accounting_periods (system-generated — 12 rows created atomically per financial year), and the append-only accounting_period_transitions history with a mandatory reason on every row. Implemented the full maker-checker accounting-period lifecycle (SOFT_CLOSE/LOCK/REOPEN) with SELF_APPROVAL_NOT_ALLOWED thrown when the reopening actor matches the period's lockedBy, backed by a DB-level chk_accounting_periods_reopener_differs CHECK as defense in depth; concurrent transitions are guarded by conditioning the update on the in-memory-read status rather than a separate version column. Manually verified all four new constraints (GSTIN format, one-current-FY, period-number uniqueness, reopener-differs) directly against Postgres before writing the service layer. Seeded one legal entity, one GST registration, and one current financial year with its 12 periods.

## Phase 2 sub-phase 2.9: documents + document_links

```text
Date: 2026-07-28
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation
Task: Implemented documents (system-managed, presigned-upload flow — initiateUpload creates a PENDING_UPLOAD row with client-declared size/checksum and returns a presigned S3 PUT URL computed locally via @aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner, no network call to S3 required; confirmUpload flips status to UPLOADED) and document_links (a real FK to entity_types.code, not free text). Enforced that a document must be UPLOADED before it can be linked (INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION otherwise) and that confirming an already-UPLOADED document is rejected the same way. chk_documents_status enforces the three-state lifecycle at the DB level. Soft-delete marks DELETED with deletedAt/deletedBy, never removes the row. Added @aws-sdk/client-s3 and @aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner as apps/api dependencies.

## Phase 2 sub-phase 2.10: outbox worker + notifications dispatch

```text
Date: 2026-07-28
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation
Task: Wired the producer side (UsersService.invite/reinvite, IdentityService.requestPasswordReset — previously stubbed with comments pointing at this sub-phase) to actually call enqueueOutboxEvent, and built the consumer side in apps/worker: outbox-dispatcher.ts claims a batch via the existing lease-based claimOutboxBatch, creates a deduplicated notifications row, sends it through a NotificationProvider, and marks the outbox row published/failed-or-dead-lettered, with job_runs bookkeeping around each tick. Added FakeNotificationProvider (dev/test) and PostmarkNotificationProvider (real HTTP), selected by whether POSTMARK_SERVER_TOKEN looks like a placeholder. Wired as a 10s interval tick in apps/worker/src/main.ts. Split apps/worker's test/test:integration into separate vitest configs (previously identical), mirroring apps/api's convention. Live-smoke-tested the running worker process: /healthz reported a successful tick and job_runs recorded SUCCEEDED rows on the expected 10s cadence.

Known issues: real Postmark connectivity is not exercised in this dev environment (placeholder token) — FakeNotificationProvider is used, which is expected foundation-phase behavior, not a gap in the dispatch logic itself.
Next task: Sub-phase 2.11 (Phase 2B platform administration UI).

## Post-2.10 schema hardening (four resolved decisions)

```text
Date: 2026-07-28
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (hardening pass, not a new sub-phase)
Task: A user-conducted review of the sub-phase 2.0-2.10 schema surfaced four items needing explicit decisions (not silent inference): Document categorization vs. entity attachment, User<->Employee cardinality, Notification inbox-vs-log semantics, and Employee status/is_active duplication. All four were resolved and implemented: Document gained a nullable documentCategoryId with a native-Prisma composite FK enforcing organization consistency against document_categories, plus in-service validation (DocumentsService.linkToEntity) that a link's entityType is permitted by the document's category (via document_category_entity_types; no rows = applies everywhere); Employee.userId became unique with a native-Prisma composite FK enforcing organization consistency against users; Notification stays an outbound-delivery-log only (no read_at added), and the "Notifications" nav item was renamed "Notification Delivery Log" (docs/02-system-architecture.md) to remove inbox ambiguity; Employee.is_active was removed entirely (straight DROP COLUMN, no compatibility alias — no production data exists), leaving status as the sole lifecycle source of truth.

The same review also corrected an inaccurate claim repeated in this project's own migration comments since phase2_03_geography: composite unique constraints and composite FKs (not just plain-column ones) ARE expressible natively in Prisma's schema DSL — only functional (lower(trim(...))) and partial (WHERE) indexes genuinely require raw SQL. Both new composite FKs in this pass are modeled as ordinary Prisma @relation/@@unique fields, which correctly avoids the "Prisma proposes to drop the hand-added constraint on every future migration" problem that has affected every migration since 2.3 (the existing geography composite FK was NOT retroactively converted — out of scope for this pass).

Operational note: while investigating whether `prisma migrate dev`'s interactive confirmation could be bypassed for a migration with data-loss warnings, `prisma migrate diff --shadow-database-url` was run pointing at the real local dev database instead of a disposable one, which wiped all seeded/test data and the `_prisma_migrations` tracking table (table structures were unaffected). Recovered by re-marking all 11 prior migrations as applied via `prisma migrate resolve --applied` and re-running the idempotent seed script; all data (organization, 36 states, 5 payment modes, 17 permissions, 3 roles, 1 legal entity, 1 GST registration, 1 financial year + 12 periods) was fully restored and verified. Local dev-only impact; no production system exists yet.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260728120000_phase2_10a_schema_hardening/, docs/02-system-architecture.md, packages/contracts/src/ports/{document.port.ts,employee.port.ts}, packages/contracts/src/routers/documents.ts, apps/api/src/documents/{documents.service.ts,documents.integration-spec.ts}, apps/api/src/employees/{employees.service.ts,employees.integration-spec.ts}
Migrations: phase2_10a_schema_hardening — applied via `prisma migrate deploy` and verified against local Postgres, including manual psql confirmation that the new composite FKs reject cross-organization references
Tests: 2 new tests in documents.integration-spec.ts (category-restricted vs. unrestricted entity-type linking; cross-organization document-category rejection); 1 new test in employees.integration-spec.ts (at-most-one-employee-per-user + cross-organization user-link rejection) — full suite now 36 apps/api + 3 apps/worker integration tests, all passing
Decisions added/changed: ADR-039 (see docs/12-decisions-log.md)
Known issues: none
Next task: Sub-phase 2.11 (Phase 2B platform administration UI).
```

## Post-2.10 backend hardening (session rotation, org-consistency FK audit, safe migrations)

```text
Date: 2026-07-28
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (hardening pass, not a new sub-phase)
Task: A user-conducted request asked for five things before proceeding to 2.11: (1) fix the refresh-session rotation race with DB uniqueness/transactional protection; (2) a complete organization-consistency FK audit across all Phase 2 models, adding missing composite FKs wherever both child and parent are organization-scoped; (3) verify and test the document-category work from the previous hardening pass; (4) a safe migration-development procedure with a disposable shadow database and safeguards against pointing it at the real dev database; (5) run full verification.

(1) IdentityService.refresh() now does the session-revocation write as a guarded `updateMany({ where: { id, revokedAt: null } })` with a `count === 1` check, instead of a plain `update` — the read-time revoked-check alone was a genuine TOCTOU race letting two concurrent refresh calls both mint a successor session. Session.rotatedFromSessionId is now `@unique` (nullable-unique) as DB-level defense-in-depth. Verified with a new test that fires two concurrent refresh() calls on the same token and asserts exactly one succeeds.

(2) Audited every Phase 2 model for organization-scoped child -> organization-scoped parent relations enforced only by a plain single-column FK (or, in three cases, no FK/relation at all). Converted 17 relations across 11 tables to native-Prisma composite FKs: UserInvitation/PasswordResetToken/Session/UserRole/UserPermissionOverride.userId -> users(id, organization_id); UserRole.roleId -> roles(id, organization_id); GstRegistration/FinancialYear.legalEntityId -> legal_entities(id, organization_id); AccountingPeriod.financialYearId -> financial_years(id, organization_id); AccountingPeriodTransition.accountingPeriodId -> accounting_periods(id, organization_id) (previously had no relation at all); Employee's three master-table FKs; DocumentLink.documentId -> documents(id, organization_id); Notification's two FKs (sourceOutboxEventId, recipientUserId); AuditEvent.legalEntityId -> legal_entities(id, organization_id). RolePermission and DocumentCategoryEntityType confirmed to need no change (neither carries its own organization_id). The pre-existing districts/cities composite FK remains the one not-yet-converted case, by explicit scope decision.

(3) Re-ran the full documents.integration-spec.ts suite (5 tests, unchanged, all passing) as the requested verification checkpoint on the prior turn's document-category feature — no gaps found.

(4) Added a genuinely separate zitaware_crm_shadow database, schema.prisma's shadowDatabaseUrl datasource config, packages/database/scripts/assert-shadow-database-safe.mjs (refuses to proceed if SHADOW_DATABASE_URL is unset or equals DATABASE_URL), and new prisma:migrate:dev/prisma:migrate:diff npm scripts that run the safeguard first. Documented the procedure in docs/07-database-conventions.md. Confirmed migrate deploy/generate/status (what CI runs) work unmodified without SHADOW_DATABASE_URL set.

(5) Full verification: all 17 composite FKs manually verified against Postgres (cross-organization insert attempts, each rejected with a foreign-key violation); build/lint/typecheck clean across all 9 workspaces; 36 apps/api + 3 apps/worker integration tests passing unchanged (the composite-FK conversions required zero application-code changes, since every existing service already supplies both ids together).
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260728130000_phase2_10b_session_rotation_and_org_fk_audit/, packages/database/scripts/assert-shadow-database-safe.mjs, packages/database/package.json, apps/api/src/identity/{identity.service.ts,identity.integration-spec.ts}, .env, .env.example, docs/07-database-conventions.md
Migrations: phase2_10b_session_rotation_and_org_fk_audit — generated via the new safe `npm run prisma:migrate:diff` procedure (dogfooding the fix from item 4), applied via `prisma migrate deploy`
Tests: 1 new test in identity.integration-spec.ts (concurrent refresh calls, exactly one succeeds, exactly one successor session); 13+ direct psql cross-organization rejection checks across all new composite FKs; full existing suite (36 apps/api + 3 apps/worker) re-verified passing
Decisions added/changed: ADR-040 (see docs/12-decisions-log.md)
Known issues: none
Next task: Sub-phase 2.11 (Phase 2B platform administration UI).
```

## Phase 2 sub-phase 2.11 (part 1): auth screens (login, accept-invite, forgot-password, reset-password)

```text
Date: 2026-07-28
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (2.11, first vertical slice)
Task: Started Phase 2B with the auth screen family — the entry point required before any other Phase 2B screen is reachable. None of these have a paired HTML prototype (docs/16 §7.1 only covers the authenticated shell/masters bundle; docs/04 §18 confirms Users/Roles/Legal-Entities/Financial-Years/Audit are NEW_REQUIRED but doesn't even list the auth screens, since the original prototype pack has no login concept at all) — built to the shell's card/typography/color language (docs/15 §6.1 fonts, §11.1 standard card, §10 buttons) rather than any specific prototype. Built a shared (auth) route-group layout (centered card, brand mark matching shell.html's brass-gradient mark treatment) plus login/accept-invite/forgot-password/reset-password pages, and turned the root page into a temporary authenticated landing page (session check -> display -> logout) since Phase 2 has no dashboard data model yet.

Fixed two real bugs discovered while wiring this up, both invisible to prior curl-only backend testing: (1) auth.ts's cookies were `secure: true` unconditionally, which real browsers silently refuse to store/send over plain http://localhost — curl doesn't enforce this browser-only rule, so it went unnoticed through all of sub-phases 2.1-2.10's testing; now conditional on NODE_ENV === 'production'. (2) the tRPC httpBatchLink had no `credentials: 'include'`, so the browser would never have sent the session cookie cross-origin (apps/web:3000 -> apps/api:4000) even once set correctly.

Also fixed a pre-existing foundation bug unrelated to auth: apps/web's globals.css/layout.tsx still had the raw create-next-app Geist-font/light-dark-background scaffold from Phase 1, never updated to match docs/15's actual typography tokens (display font Calibri/Segoe UI/Candara/Trebuchet MS stack, body font -apple-system/Segoe UI/Roboto stack) — every future Phase 2B screen would have inherited the wrong font. Fixed now, before any more screens are built on top of it.

Verified: full workspace lint/typecheck/build clean (including a real `next build` production build of all 4 new routes); apps/api's 37 integration tests re-verified passing after the cookie fix; and a real functional test of the login loop against the running dev servers via curl with a cookie jar (login -> Set-Cookie -> auth.me succeeds with the correct permission set -> logout -> auth.me correctly fails UNAUTHENTICATED). No browser-automation tool (Playwright/Puppeteer) is available in this environment, so pixel-level visual verification and actual JS-driven form-submission/redirect behavior in a real browser were NOT performed — only curl-level backend-contract verification and static page-content checks. This is a real gap against CLAUDE.md's browser-testing requirement, disclosed rather than silently skipped.
Files changed: apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx, apps/web/src/app/globals.css, apps/web/src/app/page.tsx, apps/web/src/app/(auth)/layout.tsx, apps/web/src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx, apps/web/src/app/(auth)/accept-invite/page.tsx, apps/web/src/app/(auth)/forgot-password/page.tsx, apps/web/src/app/(auth)/reset-password/page.tsx, apps/web/src/server/provider.tsx, packages/contracts/src/routers/auth.ts
Migrations: none
Tests: no new automated frontend tests this slice (no frontend test tooling/pattern established yet beyond the Phase 1 healthz route test); verified via full-workspace build/lint/typecheck and a real curl-driven login/me/logout functional test against the running dev servers
Decisions added/changed: none beyond what docs/04 §18 and docs/15 §23 already specify for NEW_REQUIRED screens
Known issues: no real-browser (visual/interactive) verification was possible — see Task notes above. Remaining Phase 2B screens: AppShell + masters catalogue/master forms, Users/Roles/Permissions, Employees, Legal Entities/GST, Financial Years/Accounting Periods, Audit Log — not started.
Next task: AppShell + shared platform-shell components (PageHeader, UiCard, StatusPill, FormGrid/FormSection/FormFooter), then the masters catalogue as the next vertical slice per docs/16 §6's recommended order.
```

## Documentation: complete command reference for fresh setup and running services

```text
Date: 2026-07-29
Phase: Documentation (no code change)
Task: Requested a single accurate reference for every command needed to set up the project from zero and run/rebuild/migrate/seed each service, since docs/14-runtime-deployment.md's "Development commands" section still had Phase-1-planning-era content: a stale `npx prisma migrate dev --name <name>` instruction (superseded by the shadow-DB-safe `prisma:migrate:dev`/`prisma:migrate:diff` scripts added during the 2.10 hardening pass) and no mention of database creation, seeding, or running apps individually.

While verifying the doc against the actual current repo, confirmed a real (previously undocumented, not previously hit as a bug because every command this session was run manually with `.env` pre-sourced) operational fact: nothing in this stack auto-loads the root `.env`. `packages/config`'s `loadEnv()` reads directly from `process.env` with no `dotenv` call anywhere in the repo; Next.js only auto-loads env files from an app's own directory (apps/web has none); Turborepo's `envMode: "loose"` forwards whatever is already in the invoking shell rather than reading `.env` itself. So `.env` must be exported into the shell (`set -a && source .env && set +a`) before `npm run dev`, any Prisma CLI command, or any per-workspace dev command. Documented this explicitly rather than leaving the fresh-setup sequence silently non-functional for a new developer who just runs the previously-documented steps verbatim.

Rewrote docs/14-runtime-deployment.md's "Environment files" and "Development commands" sections: accurate current env-var categories cross-checked against .env.example; the env-loading requirement above; a fresh-setup sequence including `createdb zitaware_crm_dev`/`createdb zitaware_crm_shadow`, `prisma:generate`, `prisma:migrate:deploy`, `prisma:seed` (with the bootstrap admin dev credentials and idempotency note), and `npm run dev`; a new "Running one service at a time" subsection (`npm run dev --workspace apps/{web,api,worker}`); a "Migration commands" subsection replacing the stale instruction with the real `prisma:migrate:dev`/`prisma:migrate:diff`/`prisma:migrate:deploy` scripts and the shadow-database safety rationale; and a "Seeding" subsection.
Files changed: docs/14-runtime-deployment.md
Migrations: none
Tests: none (documentation only); commands cross-checked against root package.json, apps/web/apps/api/apps/worker/packages/database package.json scripts, turbo.json, packages/config/src/env.ts, packages/database/scripts/assert-shadow-database-safe.mjs, packages/database/prisma/seed.ts
Decisions added/changed: none
Known issues: none
Next task: AppShell + shared platform-shell components, then the masters catalogue (per the 2.11 entry above) — unchanged, this was a documentation-only detour.
```

## Dependency security audit triage (npm audit)

```text
Date: 2026-07-29
Phase: Maintenance (no product code change)
Task: Adding @playwright/test surfaced a standing, never-triaged npm audit count (21 findings: 4 moderate, 16 high, 1 critical). Requested a strategic fix rather than a blind `npm audit fix --force`.

Triaged every finding individually (see ADR-042 for full reasoning). Fixed via a root `package.json` `overrides` entry forcing `postcss` to the patched 8.5.24 everywhere in the tree, including next's own bundled nested copy — this covers the only findings with any production-build reach (postcss is used at build/CSS-processing time by next and vite). `@ant-design/nextjs-registry` and `eslint-plugin-import` resolved for free once the lockfile was regenerated (their own declared ranges already permitted a patched version). Deliberately deferred: vitest 2.x->4.x (the one CRITICAL finding, but only exploitable if `vitest --ui` is exposed to a network, which nothing here does), eslint 9.x->10.x, @nestjs/cli, @swc/cli (all dev-tooling-only, all require an unreviewed major bump or have no real upstream fix yet), and sharp (next's own optional-dependency range `^0.34.5` doesn't permit the patched 0.35.x without risking next/image breakage — deferred until next itself bumps its bundled range). Net: 21 -> 19 findings, zero application-code behavior change.

Applying the override required a full `node_modules` + `package-lock.json` regeneration (a partial reinstall left a stale resolution in place; `npm audit fix` afterwards was found to silently reintroduce it by re-triggering install without changing any declared version, so it was deliberately not run again once the clean state was confirmed).

Verification after the dependency changes surfaced two unrelated, pre-existing latent bugs, both fixed in this same pass: (1) apps/web's production build failed prerendering `/_global-error` with a stale `.next` cache — cleared and rebuilt clean; confirmed via a lockfile version-diff that no React/Next/AntD package version actually changed, so this was cache staleness, not a regression from today's changes. (2) `apps/api/src/rbac/users.integration-spec.ts`'s `afterAll` deleted `outboxEvent` rows before `notification` rows, violating the `fk_notifications_outbox_event_org_consistent` composite FK added in ADR-040 — this test file was simply missed when that FK was introduced two turns ago; fixed by reordering the deletes.

Also approved `fsevents@2.3.2` in the root `allowScripts` allowlist (Playwright's own transitive dependency on a different exact version of the same already-allowlisted macOS file-watcher package).

Full re-verification after all fixes: lint clean, typecheck clean, build clean (all 9 workspaces), unit tests clean (all workspaces), integration tests clean (8 files / 37 tests, apps/api).
Files changed: package.json, package-lock.json, apps/web/package.json (Playwright devDependency, already added; audit fix only reordered/re-sorted keys), apps/api/src/rbac/users.integration-spec.ts
Migrations: none
Tests: no new tests; existing full suite (unit + integration) re-verified passing after the fix to users.integration-spec.ts's teardown order
Decisions added/changed: ADR-042 (see docs/12-decisions-log.md)
Known issues: 19 npm audit findings remain, deliberately deferred per ADR-042 — revisit `sharp` when next.js bumps its bundled dependency range; treat vitest/eslint major-version bumps as a separate, dedicated, individually-tested task.
Next task: AppShell + shared platform-shell components, then the masters catalogue — unchanged, this was a maintenance detour.
```

## Phase 2 sub-phase 2.11 (part 2): AppShell — authenticated platform frame

```text
Date: 2026-07-29
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (2.11, second vertical slice)
Task: Built the shared authenticated shell every remaining Phase 2B screen renders inside, derived from references/ui/html/01-platform/shell.html per docs/16 §7.1: AppShell, SidebarNavigation, Topbar, PageHeader, UiCard, KpiCard, FilterCard, EntityTable, MasterFormDrawer, StatusTag, plus a real server-side session guard (docs/04 §5.2) rather than a client-side redirect-on-error.

Backend: extended auth.me with a new AuthPort.getProfile() (displayName, organizationName) — the shell's brand area and user menu need real data docs/04 §5.1 requires ("configurable Zitaware organization data"), which the prior {userId, organizationId, permissions} shape didn't carry.

apps/web/src/server/session.ts's getServerSession() reads the zitaware_crm_access_token cookie via next/headers and calls the API's auth.me directly (cookie forwarded manually, cross-origin), used by the new (platform)/layout.tsx (async Server Component) to redirect('/login') before any protected markup renders. (platform)/page.tsx replaces the old temporary root page.tsx (moved, not duplicated — both would map to the same "/" route).

Naming: followed docs/16 §7.1's exact component names (SidebarNavigation/KpiCard/FilterCard/EntityTable/StatusTag) over docs/15 §21's differently-named catalogue for the same responsibilities (PermissionMenu/StatTile/FilterBar/DataTable/StatusPill) — flagged as a documentation reconciliation follow-up, not resolved here.

Four real bugs found and fixed during verification (none visible from lint/typecheck/unit tests — only surfaced via an actual Playwright login against the running dev stack and a close look at the resulting screenshot):
1. @ant-design/icons broke Turbopack's page-data-collection step across unrelated (auth) routes ("(0, b.createContext) is not a function") — dropped in favor of hand-written inline SVG icons (packages/ui/src/components/icons.tsx), matching shell.html's own no-icon-library approach.
2. Tailwind v4's automatic content detection never scans a sibling workspace package — every class used only inside packages/ui/src (responsive variants, arbitrary grid values) silently generated no CSS at all, while AntD's own component-level theme tokens partially masked the gap (dark Menu/Layout background still showed up even though grid-template-columns computed to "none"). Fixed with an explicit @source directive in apps/web/src/app/globals.css.
3. AntD's Menu clones each icon element and replaces its className entirely with "ant-menu-item-icon", discarding a Tailwind size class — icons rendered at ~1392x1392px, unconstrained. Fixed by sizing via width/height SVG attributes instead (survive the clone).
4. The shell used Tailwind's default 768px/640px breakpoints instead of docs/15 §19's mandated 980px, and three interactive controls (mobile-nav/alerts/user-menu buttons, plus a bare native search input with outline-none and no replacement) lacked docs/15 §20's required "visibly brass" focus treatment. Fixed with min-[980px]: variants throughout and explicit focus-visible brass rings; the search input was replaced with AntD's own Input (already theme-configured for brass focus).

One pre-existing upstream bug confirmed, not fixed: apps/web's production build fails prerendering the auto-generated /_global-error page. Bisected exhaustively — reproduces on a clean checkout with zero AppShell code, a bare root layout, both webpack and Turbopack, and every Next.js release from 16.2.0 through the newest 16.3.0-preview.10. This corrects ADR-042's prior claim that an earlier occurrence of this same crash was "pre-existing cache staleness" — it demonstrably is not. Per explicit user decision, left open and disclosed; `npm run build` is red for apps/web specifically until Next.js ships a fix.

Verified: full workspace lint/typecheck/unit-tests/integration-tests clean (37 existing API integration tests unaffected by the getProfile addition); every workspace's build passes except apps/web's (known issue above); 20 new component tests (packages/ui, jsdom+RTL, newly wired vitest.config.ts); a real Playwright-driven login against the running dev stack confirmed desktop (1440px) and mobile (900px) layouts, the off-canvas drawer, permission-filtered navigation, and keyboard-focus brass rings on every interactive control.

Migrations: none
Tests: 20 new packages/ui component tests (SidebarNavigation, Topbar, PageHeader, StatusTag) + 4 new apps/web session.test.ts tests; existing 37 API integration tests and all prior unit tests re-verified passing

Known issues: apps/web's `next build` fails on the pre-existing upstream bug described above (tracked, not introduced by this increment); docs/15 §21's component naming still disagrees with docs/16 §7.1's (used here) — a documentation reconciliation follow-up, not a functional gap
Next task: masters catalogue vertical slice (masters-all.html + master list/form screens), per docs/16 §6's recommended order — do not start in the same increment as this one.

## Phase 2.11 (part 3, commits A+B): Masters catalogue + editable geography

```text
Date: 2026-07-30
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (2.11, masters catalogue slice)
Task: The first of six Phase 2B admin-screen families, per docs/16 §6 order. Delivered in three commits; A and B below (C = penalty categories follows). Standing requirement enforced throughout: every editable schema column is present in its create/update form (field-coverage cross-check against schema.prisma) — this surfaced two real gaps that were closed rather than shipped silent.

Commit A (masters catalogue + generic screen): new masters.getCatalog returns per-type record counts via the FlatMasterDelegate registry (added a count() method). Frontend: /masters catalogue tile grid (permission-filtered, grouped, real counts) and one generic /masters/[type] screen serving all 12 identical-shape org-scoped masters — exposes code (create-only, immutable on edit), name, description, sort_order, and status (is_active via the row Activate/Deactivate action). Client-side search/status filter + pagination over the full small lookup set. Also wrapped the app in antd <App component={false}> so screens use App.useApp() and toasts inherit the theme (removed the static-message warning).

Commit B (editable geography): reversed ADR-036's read-only geography stance (per user decision, ADR-044) so admins can add districts/cities the partial seed omits. Backend: geography.createState/updateState/setStateActive (+ district, + city) gated master.manage, plus geography.getCounts for the catalogue tiles. Hierarchy validated in-service (district needs an active state; city needs an active district and derives its state_id from it — never caller-supplied), on top of ADR-036's composite FK which stays. A row's parent is fixed at creation (update changes name only) so cities can't drift out of hierarchy consistency. Audit events attributed to the acting admin's org (geography is global, audit_events is org-scoped). gst_state_code editable in Phase 2 (no tax docs reference it yet). Frontend: editable States (incl. the previously-missed gst_state_code field), Districts (state filter), Cities (state→district cascade) screens + Geography catalogue tiles.

Field-coverage cross-check (the required gate): 12 generic types → code/name/description/sort_order/status all present; states → name/code/gst_state_code/status; districts → name/state-context/status; cities → name/district-context/derived-state/status. No editable column dropped. penalty_categories.basis_type is the remaining known gap — closed in Commit C (its own dedicated CRUD, since the generic identical-shape API cannot carry basis_type).

Two issues found and fixed during verification: (1) antd static `message` warning → switched to App.useApp() + <App> wrapper. (2) All states briefly rendered "Inactive" because the compiled apps/api/dist was stale (lightweight-run mode's trade-off — backend edits need npm run build:backend) and the old build lacked the new isActive mapping; forced rebuild resolved it. DB confirmed all 36 states active.

Verified end-to-end via Playwright against the running stack (catalogue tiles + counts, Lead Types create/edit/deactivate with code-immutability, states GST-code column + correct Active status, district create under Maharashtra with hierarchy validation), zero console errors/warnings.

## Phase 2.11 (part 3, commit C): penalty categories (dedicated CRUD with basis_type)

```text
Date: 2026-07-30
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (2.11, masters catalogue slice)

## Phase 2.11 (part 4, commit B): Users (detail, enable/revoke-sessions, employee link)

```text
Date: 2026-07-30
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (2.11, Users/Roles/Permissions slice — final commit of this family)
Task: Completes the Users, Roles & Permissions family. UsersService gains getDetail (composed roles+overrides+effective permissions+employee), enable, revokeSessions, and employeeId support on invite plus standalone linkEmployee/unlinkEmployee — implementing the user's explicit rules: all internal staff get an Employee record, employees may exist without logins, logins may exist without an Employee (CEO), the link is manageable from either side and never role-implied, and revoking access disables the User without touching Employee history.
Backend: UsersService.invite accepts optional employeeId (links Employee.userId in the same transaction, rejecting if the employee is missing/inactive/already linked). getDetail composes userRole/override/effective-permission/employee reads into one call (reuses RolesService.getEffectivePermissions, not reimplemented). enable reverses disable only for a DISABLED user with an existing passwordHash. revokeSessions is a standalone admin action distinct from disable's side-effect revocation. Bug fix: disable() had no status guard and would crash on Postgres's own chk_users_status_password_consistency check if called on an INVITED user (no password) — now explicitly rejected with a clear message. RolesService gains clearPermissionOverride (the missing complement to setPermissionOverride — reverts a permission back to plain role-inheritance instead of being a one-way GRANT/REVOKE ratchet). employees.listLinkable added (active, unlinked employees only) for the picker. UserSummary gained roleNames/employeeLinked for the list view.
Frontend: (platform)/users/page.tsx — users list (EntityTable: name/email/status/role tags/employee-linked indicator/View) + Invite drawer (email, displayName, roles multi-select, optional employee picker) + a User Details drawer (status + invited/last-login/disabled metadata + Resend/Disable/Enable/Revoke-Sessions actions + roles add/remove + linked-employee link/unlink + a per-permission Grant/Revoke/Inherit override control grouped by resource, showing each permission's live effective state). The detail body follows the same keyed-fresh-mount, no-useEffect pattern as the Roles screen (ADR-045) for the same set-state-in-effect lint rule.
Field-coverage cross-check: User — email(invite-only)/displayName/status(disable-enable actions)/roles(assign-remove)/per-user overrides(grant-revoke-inherit)/employee link(link-unlink) all present; mfaEnabled intentionally omitted (inert in Phase 2).

Migrations: none (uses existing users/employees/user_roles/user_permission_overrides/sessions tables)
Tests: 7 new users.integration-spec.ts tests (getDetail composition, invite-with-employeeId + already-linked rejection + disable-keeps-link, enable/disable status guards including the INVITED-disable rejection, revokeSessions, link/unlinkEmployee) + 1 new roles.integration-spec.ts test (clearPermissionOverride round-trip) — 57 integration tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across 14 tasks. Verified via Playwright against the running lightweight stack: invited a user with a role and an employee link (picker showed only the unlinked active employee), list showed the role tag and Linked indicator, and the Grant→Revoke→Inherit override cycle was confirmed to actually change the rendered effective-permission state at each step, zero console errors.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-046 (employee-link invite/link/unlink rules, getDetail composition, enable/revokeSessions, disable's INVITED guard bug fix, clearPermissionOverride)

## Phase 2.11 (part 6, follow-up): soft delete for Legal Entities & GST Registrations

```text
Date: 2026-07-31
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (2.11, Legal Entities & GST Registrations follow-up)
Task: User-reported bug: deactivating a Legal Entity or GST Registration doesn't free its PAN/GSTIN for reuse. Root cause: uq_legal_entities_org_pan/uq_gst_registrations_gstin were plain (non-partial) unique indexes, and neither the entity nor GST-registration side ever wrote to their already-existing but unused deletedAt/deletedBy columns — there was no delete path at all.
Migration: 20260731085614_legal_entity_gst_soft_delete_partial_unique — converted both unique indexes to partial ones filtered WHERE deleted_at IS NULL, authored via the mandated safe procedure (npm run prisma:migrate:dev from packages/database, shadow-database-verified). Same partial-index migration shape this repo has used twice before (uq_user_permission_overrides_*, uq_financial_years_one_current) — not invented fresh. The auto-generated diff also proposed the same recurring spurious drop of chk_cities_state_matches_district/uq_districts_id_state_id every migration since phase2_04_hr_masters has had to leave un-applied (hand-added constraints, no Prisma-DSL representation) — left un-applied here too.
Backend: LegalEntityService.delete() (new) — blocked with a clear VALIDATION_FAILED message while any GST registration or financial year still references the entity (mirrors RolesService.delete's dependent-count guard), otherwise soft-deletes with the same optimistic-concurrency pattern every other mutator in this service uses. LegalEntityService.deleteGstRegistration() (new) — no dependent guard needed (nothing references a GST registration), same soft-delete pattern. No restore action — delete is final, by explicit user decision (no restore precedent exists anywhere in this codebase).
Frontend: added a "Delete" button (danger Popconfirm, mirroring the Roles screen's only existing Delete precedent) to both the Legal Entities list and the GST Registrations sub-list in (platform)/legal-entities/page.tsx.
Tests: 2 new organization.integration-spec.ts tests — deleting a GST registration frees its GSTIN for reuse on the same legal entity (the user's exact reported scenario) + stale-version rejection; deleting a legal entity is blocked while a GST registration exists, blocked while a financial year exists, and succeeds once both are gone, freeing its PAN for a new entity. 63 integration tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright reproducing the user's exact bug end-to-end: entity+GST created, entity-delete correctly blocked, GST registration deleted, identical GSTIN re-added to the same entity (succeeded), then both deleted and a brand-new entity successfully reused the freed PAN — zero console errors from the app itself.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-049 (partial-unique-index migration, delete-guard rule, no-restore decision)
Known issues: apps/web next build still blocked by the ADR-043 upstream Next.js bug (unchanged).
Next task: Next Phase 2.11 families (per the user's original ordering): Financial Years & Accounting Periods, then Audit Log.
```

## Phase 2.11 (part 7): Financial Years & Accounting Periods

```text
Date: 2026-07-31
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (2.11, Financial Years & Accounting Periods slice)
Task: Financial Years & Accounting Periods — open/soft-close/lock/reopen with reason + audit. Like Legal Entities before it, the entire backend already existed end-to-end (FinancialYearService.create builds a financial year + its 12 accounting periods atomically; transitionAccountingPeriod implements the full soft-close/lock/reopen maker-checker lifecycle) — only the frontend was missing. No HTML prototype exists at all for this screen (NEW_REQUIRED, confirmed zero matches anywhere) — full design freedom.
Backend: setCurrentFinancialYear (new) — isCurrent could previously only be set at create time; this closes the gap, reusing create's own "unset old current, set new current" transaction shape, gated financial_year.manage, its own FINANCIAL_YEAR_SET_CURRENT audit event.
Docs correction: docs/09-security-audit.md's "Period reopen requires Admin permission" corrected to describe the actual, tested mechanism — reopen shares accounting_period.lock with soft-close/lock (Accountant holds it too), and the real safeguard is the maker-checker actor check (SELF_APPROVAL_NOT_ALLOWED, backed by the DB's chk_accounting_periods_reopener_differs). Also added accounting-period reopen as an explicit example under the existing Maker-checker section. User's explicit choice: correct the doc, don't add a stricter Admin-only permission.
Frontend: (platform)/financial-years/page.tsx — a Legal Entity picker (financial years are always scoped to one entity) + a Financial Years list (label/dates/current indicator/Set as Current/View Periods) + an Add Financial Year drawer (label, start/end date with auto-suggested end = start+1 year−1 day, Set as current toggle) + an Accounting Periods drawer (12 system-generated periods, status badges, Locked By/Reopened By resolved to display names via users.list, status-appropriate action buttons). Every Soft Close/Lock/Reopen action opens a Modal collecting a mandatory reason (first use of this interaction shape in the app — no Popconfirm-only pattern could fit free text). The Reopen button is pre-emptively disabled client-side when the signed-in admin is the same actor who locked the period, on top of the server's authoritative check.
Bug found and fixed during verification: the create form's cross-field date auto-fill used form.setFieldValue inside the DatePicker's onChange, which triggered a real rc-field-form/dayjs console warning ("There may be circular references") on every date pick. Switching to the officially-recommended form.setFieldsValue API resolved it cleanly — confirmed via an isolated before/after Playwright check.
Field-coverage cross-check: FinancialYear — legalEntityId (page-level picker)/label(create-only)/startDate+endDate(create-only, drive the 12 periods)/isCurrent(create toggle + new setCurrentFinancialYear action) all present; AccountingPeriod — status(via transitionAccountingPeriod)/lockedBy+reopenedBy(read-only, name-resolved) present; periodNumber/dates read-only, system-generated.

Migrations: none (uses existing financial_years/accounting_periods/accounting_period_transitions tables)
Tests: 1 new organization.integration-spec.ts test (setCurrentFinancialYear moves current between two financial years for the same legal entity, rejects a stale-version re-attempt) — 64 integration tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green. Verified live via Playwright: created a financial year, confirmed all 12 periods generated with exact month boundaries (first starts on the declared start date, twelfth ends on the declared end date), soft-closed then locked a period with a reason each time, confirmed Reopen was disabled for the same actor who locked it, used Set as Current to move current status between financial years — zero console errors.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-050 (setCurrentFinancialYear addition, docs/09 reopen-permission correction, reason-modal UX pattern, delete explicitly out of scope)
Known issues: apps/web next build still blocked by the ADR-043 upstream Next.js bug (unchanged).
Next task: Next Phase 2.11 family (per the user's original ordering): Audit Log — the last family in the sequence.

## Seed script fixes: real legal entity data, broken upserts, durable master data

```text
Date: 2026-08-02
Phase: Phase 2 — ongoing maintenance (not a new admin-screen family)
Task: User ran `prisma migrate reset` to flush the dev database back to a clean baseline. This surfaced a real bug (seed.ts crashed) and prompted two follow-up fixes: real legal entity/GST data, and durably persisting master data the user had just entered via the UI.
Bug fixed: seed.ts's legalEntity/gstRegistration upserts used `organizationId_panNumber`/`gstin` as unique-where keys — both were removed from schema.prisma by ADR-049's soft-delete migration (converted to partial unique indexes, which Prisma's schema DSL can't express as a native @@unique/@unique). Any fresh `migrate reset` would crash seed.ts immediately. Fixed both to use findFirst + conditional create (matching the pattern already used for districts/cities in the same script).
Data change: seed.ts's placeholder legal entity ("Zitaware", PAN AAAAM1234M, GSTIN 27AAAAM1234M1Z5, Maharashtra) replaced with the real entity — "Zitaware Solutions Private Limited", PAN (PAN removed), GSTIN (GSTIN removed), Gujarat (state code 24 confirmed against the states table).
Durable master data: while verifying the reset, discovered the user had manually created real reference data via the Masters UI in the minutes after the flush — Departments (Developer, Designer), Designations (Sr./Jr. Developer, Sr./Jr. Designer, Project Manager), Employment Types (Full Time, Part Time), Government Body Types (State Government, ULB, Municipal Corporation) — none of which were in seed.ts, so a future reset would silently wipe them. Added all four as new idempotent upsert loops in seed.ts (organizationId_code compound key, mirroring the existing SecurityInstrumentPurpose/Type pattern) — confirmed via re-running seed.ts that the existing rows (same ids, same created_by) are recognized and left untouched, not duplicated.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/seed.ts
Migrations: none (data/seed-script only; the schema/migration itself was already correct from ADR-049)
Tests: full lint/typecheck green; manually verified seed.ts runs clean end-to-end and is idempotent against the real, already-existing department/designation/employment-type/government-body-type rows (same ids, no duplicates after re-run).
Decisions added/changed: none (bug fix + data durability, not a new architectural decision)
Known issues: none new.
Next task: unchanged — Audit Log remains the next Phase 2.11 family.
```

## Phase 2.11 (part 8): Audit Log — last family in the sequence

```text
Date: 2026-08-02
Phase: Phase 2 — Platform foundation (2.11, Audit Log slice — completes the Phase 2.11 admin-screen sequence)
Task: Audit Log — search actor/entity/action/time, permission-scoped before/after/domain payload. Unlike every prior family, nothing existed at all: audit_events and recordAuditEvent (the write helper every service already calls) work, but there was no read-side backend and no frontend. Built entirely from scratch.
Backend: new audit port/service/router (list with page/pageSize/filters -> {rows, total}; listEntityTypes/listActions for dynamic filter dropdowns), gated audit.read, wired into context/router/app.module/main like every other module. Deliberate departure from every prior screen: server-side pagination and filtering (real DB count()+skip/take), not client-side fetch-everything — audit_events is written to by every mutation in the app and will grow unbounded, unlike the small lookup tables every other screen queries. Actor filter is free-text against the actor_email snapshot column, not a Users-list dropdown, so it stays historically accurate for renamed/disabled actors.
Real bug found and fixed during verification: the Actor column was blank for every event except logins. Investigation showed only IdentityService ever passed actorEmail explicitly to recordAuditEvent — the other 10 services calling it (legal-entity, financial-year, roles, users, document-support, masters, documents, geography, employees, penalty-categories) never did, despite docs/09 requiring an actor-email snapshot on every audit event. Root cause: the tRPC AuthContext carries no email, only {id, organizationId}. Fixed centrally in recordAuditEvent itself (packages/database/src/audit.ts) — it now resolves actorEmail from actorId via a lookup on the same transaction client when not supplied, so every call site (existing and future) is correct by construction, without threading a new parameter through 10 services.
Due-diligence check: grepped every recordAuditEvent call site for password/token/secret-shaped fields in oldValues/newValues/commandPayload — zero matches, every caller already cherry-picks specific fields. Confirmed safe to render these directly in the detail drawer with no new redaction layer.
Frontend: (platform)/audit-log/page.tsx — FilterCard (Search Actor, Entity Type, Action, Date Range via AntD's first-ever RangePicker in this app, Apply/Reset) + a server-paginated EntityTable (Time/Actor/Entity Type/Entity ID/Action/Reason/View Details) + a read-only detail Drawer rendering oldValues/newValues/commandPayload as formatted JSON plus correlationId/ipAddress/userAgent/legalEntityId.
Field-coverage cross-check: organizationId(always server-scoped)/legalEntityId(detail only)/entityType+entityId+action(filterable, entityId free-text/not resolved to a name)/actorId+actorEmail(actor filter free-text on actorEmail; actorId detail-only)/oldValues+newValues+commandPayload+reason(detail drawer)/correlationId+ipAddress+userAgent(detail drawer)/createdAt(column + date-range filter) all present.
Files changed: packages/contracts/src/ports/audit.port.ts (new), packages/contracts/src/ports/index.ts, packages/contracts/src/index.ts, packages/contracts/src/context.ts, packages/contracts/src/router.ts, packages/contracts/src/routers/audit.ts (new), apps/api/src/audit/{audit.service.ts,audit.module.ts,audit.integration-spec.ts} (new), apps/api/src/app.module.ts, apps/api/src/main.ts, packages/database/src/audit.ts, packages/database/src/audit.test.ts, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/audit-log/page.tsx (new), docs/04, docs/12 (ADR-051)
Migrations: none (uses the existing audit_events table)
Tests: 7 new audit.integration-spec.ts tests (org-scoping, pagination, actorEmail/entityType/action/date-range filters, listEntityTypes/listActions distinctness) + 2 new audit.test.ts tests (actorEmail auto-resolve from actorId; stays null with no actorId) — 71 integration tests plus the full @zitaware/database unit suite pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across 14 tasks. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight stack: generated a mix of login/role/legal-entity events, confirmed the Actor column populated for every new event post-fix (the one pre-fix row correctly retains its historical blank snapshot), confirmed entity-type and actor filters each narrow results correctly, confirmed the detail drawer renders a real newValues diff — zero console errors from the application itself.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-051 (server-side pagination as a new precedent, actor-email-snapshot filter rationale, dynamic entityType/action dropdowns, entityId-not-resolved and export-deferred scope boundaries, the systemic actorEmail auto-resolve bug fix)
Known issues: apps/web next build still blocked by the ADR-043 upstream Next.js bug (unchanged).
Next task: Phase 2.11's six-family admin-screen sequence is now complete (Masters catalogue, Users/Roles/Permissions, Employees, Legal Entities/GST Registrations, Financial Years/Accounting Periods, Audit Log). No further family remains in this sequence.
```

## Phase 2.12: final verification sweep — Phase 2 complete

```text
Date: 2026-08-02
Phase: Phase 2.12 — final verification (not a new admin-screen family; closes out Phase 2)
Task: Comprehensive end-to-end verification of every completed Phase 2 module before treating Phase 2 as done: full infra suite (lint/typecheck/test/test:integration, production builds, migration verification, health checks, PM2 smoke test) plus per-module functional verification (list/search/filter/pagination; create/edit/activate-deactivate/validation; empty/loading/API-error states; permission-denied; cross-org rejection; audit-event generation; responsive layout) across Authentication/session lifecycle, Users/Roles/Permissions/overrides, Employee-User linking, Master CRUD, Legal Entities/GST, Financial Years/Accounting Periods, Documents, Notification Delivery Log, Audit Log, and organization isolation/authorization. Explicit constraint: fix only confirmed defects, do not start any Phase 3+ module (CRM, tender, project, salary import, employee contribution, accounting-ledger).
Infra suite: full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green (14 tasks, 71 integration tests, unchanged). apps/api/apps/worker production builds clean. apps/web's production build now succeeds (previously blocked by the ADR-043 upstream Next.js /_global-error bug) — traced to commit 1959386's dependency-lockfile regeneration landing the day after ADR-043, not to any change made in this session; see the correction appended to ADR-043 and ADR-052. Migration state clean, only the known/expected spurious districts-cities drop artifact in the diff.
PM2 production smoke test: initially found zitaware-crm-api/zitaware-crm-worker crash-looping on env.validation_failed. Root-caused to a test-procedure mistake, not a product defect — .env was not sourced into the shell before pm2 start, and this project's own documented convention (docs/14: nothing auto-loads .env) applies to pm2 start exactly as it does to any other command, since PM2 forks children with the invoking shell's environment. Re-run correctly: all three apps online with 0 restarts, all three health endpoints (web /api/healthz, api /healthz+/readyz, worker /healthz) returned healthy. No change made to ecosystem.config.cjs.
Documents and Notification Delivery Log: confirmed intentional backend-only scope for Phase 2, not gaps. Both have complete, integration-tested backends (S3 presigned-upload lifecycle + entity-type restriction + cross-org rejection for Documents; outbox-dispatcher/Postmark provider status tracking for Notifications) but no frontend, nav entry, or docs/04 screen contract — their only documented consuming screens (lead-view-documents, project-documentation) belong to Lead/Project, both Phase 3+. No frontend built for either, per the "do not add new modules" constraint.
Zero confirmed Phase 2 defects found. Live Playwright verification against the PM2-hosted production stack covered auth/session lifecycle (invalid/valid login, sign-out, post-sign-out redirect), Users (list/search/invite/validation), Employees (full create -> Enable System Access -> Unlink -> deactivate cycle with real audit-triggering mutations confirmed via network trace), Masters, Legal Entities/GST, Financial Years (legal-entity-scoped list, all 12 accounting periods confirmed against the database), Audit Log (pagination, actor filter), and a mobile-width responsive check. Every apparent failure hit while writing the verification scripts (Users search "not filtering", Financial Years "empty", "logout" "missing", a protected route "not redirecting") was traced to a script selector/sequencing mistake and confirmed corrected on retry, not a product defect. Cross-org rejection and permission-denied enforcement verified structurally: the single requirePermission tRPC middleware chokepoint (unit-tested), organizationId always server-sourced never client-supplied, and the org-consistency composite-FK audit already completed in commit a99862d. All test data created during verification (one invited test user, one test employee) was deleted afterward — database returned to its pre-verification baseline.
Files changed: docs/11-development-roadmap.md (Phase 2 exit confirmation added), docs/12-decisions-log.md (ADR-043 correction + ADR-052), docs/13-progress.md (this entry). No application source code changed — every mechanism checked was already correct.
Migrations: none.
Tests: no new tests added (verification-only pass); existing suite re-run and confirmed green throughout.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-052 (full verification sweep, PM2 smoke-test root cause, Documents/Notification Delivery Log scope confirmation, zero-defects finding); correction appended to ADR-043 (apps/web production build no longer blocked).
Known issues: none open. The former "apps/web next build blocked by ADR-043" line carried in every prior Phase 2.11 entry is resolved as of this entry.
Next task: Phase 2 is complete. Next phase (CRM, tender, project, or whichever the user scopes next) requires its own planning pass — not started here per explicit instruction.

ClientType slots into the existing generic masters registry (masters.service.ts/masters router/masters catalog.ts) exactly like LeadType/LeadSource/TenderPortal/ProjectType already do -- no bespoke port.
Real gap found and fixed: client-view.html's mock data shows GSTIN with no corresponding client-add.html input. Added optional gstin to ClientProfile (format-checked via the same 15-char regex CHECK as gst_registrations, nullable, no uniqueness constraint since it's not our source of truth).
Real bug found and fixed during verification: ClientSummary.id was wired to clientProfile.id, but every service method (getDetail/update/setActive) treats the caller-supplied id as Party.id -- caught by the integration test suite itself (a spurious VERSION_CONFLICT on reactivate). Fixed by exposing Party.id as the one true id and dropping the now-redundant partyId field.
Backend: packages/contracts/src/ports/client.port.ts (new), apps/api/src/clients/{clients.service.ts,clients.module.ts,clients.integration-spec.ts} (new), packages/contracts/src/routers/clients.ts (new), wired into context/router/app.module/main like every other module. Five new permission keys (party.read, client.read, client.create, client.update, client.export) granted to Admin (all), Accountant/Project Manager (client.read+party.read), Admin Staff (client.read), Employee (none).
Frontend: (platform)/clients/page.tsx -- FilterCard (Search/State/Status/Added-between) + EntityTable + a create/edit Drawer with cascading State->District->City selects and a Form.List for repeatable contacts (first use of that AntD pattern in this app). New "CRM" nav group (first nav group besides Administration/Organization) with a new hand-written ClientsIcon.
No delete() this slice: nothing can yet reference a Party (Lead doesn't exist until Slice 2), so a delete guard would have no real dependent to test against -- deferred to the Leads slice.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260802173635_phase3_01_party_client_foundation (new), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (permission keys/role grants), packages/contracts/src/{ports/client.port.ts (new),ports/index.ts,index.ts,context.ts,router.ts,routers/clients.ts (new),ports/master.port.ts,routers/masters.ts}, apps/api/src/{clients/* (new),masters/masters.service.ts,app.module.ts,main.ts}, packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx,nav-items.ts}, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{clients/page.tsx (new),masters/catalog.ts}, apps/api/src/masters/masters.integration-spec.ts and apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (updated hard-coded counts: 12->13 master types, 17->22 permissions), docs/04, docs/11, docs/12 (ADR-053 + a correction to ADR-052).
Migrations: phase3_01_party_client_foundation -- reviewed by hand for the recurring spurious chk_cities_state_matches_district/uq_districts_id_state_id drop artifact, stripped as usual; added chk_client_profiles_gstin_format by hand (Prisma DSL has no regex CHECK).
Tests: 8 new clients.integration-spec.ts tests (atomic create with contacts, GSTIN format rejection, state/district/city hierarchy validation both directions, update with diff-audited fields + wholesale contact replacement, stale-version rejection, deactivate/reactivate, cross-org isolation) -- 79 integration tests pass workspace-wide (71 + 8 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all 9 workspaces. Verified live via Playwright against the running lightweight dev stack: created a client with address + contact, edited it, deactivated/reactivated it, confirmed the Client Types tile in the Masters catalogue, zero console errors. apps/api/apps/worker production builds clean; apps/web's production build hit the pre-existing ADR-043 upstream Next.js bug again (confirmed via isolation testing to be unrelated to this slice's code -- see the correction appended to ADR-052).
Decisions added/changed: ADR-053 (Party/ClientProfile split, ClientType-vs-GovernmentBodyType, GSTIN inclusion, single-address decision, the id-mapping bug fix, no-delete-this-slice, EMD/SD-to-Phase-4 resequencing); correction to ADR-052 (the "apps/web build now succeeds" claim was premature -- the bug is intermittent, not fixed).
Known issues: apps/web's production build is intermittently blocked by the still-unfixed, disclosed ADR-043 upstream Next.js bug (unrelated to this slice). No other open issues.

Tests: no new tests (seed-data-only change); full lint/typecheck/test:integration re-run and confirmed green (79 integration tests, unaffected since every integration test creates its own isolated organization). Known issues: none new.

(2) Lead.estimatedValue was typed Decimal(14,2), inconsistent with docs/07's stated numeric(19,4) money convention -- corrected to Decimal(19,4) to match Employee.displayedSalaryAmount exactly, before any other code depended on the wrong precision.

Frontend: (platform)/leads/page.tsx (list + filters + add/edit drawer, client-side fetch-and-filter matching Client's own precedent) and (platform)/leads/[id]/page.tsx (NEW pattern for this app -- the first dedicated detail-as-its-own-route page, with Detail/Documents/Updates tabs via antd Tabs, first use in this app). LeadFormBody and stage/status display helpers factored into a shared leads/shared.tsx module so both drawers (list-page inline edit, detail-page edit) stay identical rather than duplicated. HTML's "Title"/"Note" fields on the upload form were dropped since DocumentSummary has no backing fields for them -- collecting input that goes nowhere would be a dead-input bug.
No Lead number, no automated duplicate detection, no soft-delete/deactivate lifecycle, no Convert-to-Project button (Project doesn't exist yet) -- all explicit, disclosed scope boundaries from the approved plan, not gaps. Client-detail's "Leads" mini-list (client-view.html's embedded section) deferred to a fast-follow -- Client has no dedicated detail route yet at all (Slice 3.1 shipped list+edit-drawer only), so building it properly means building a real Client detail page first, bigger scope than this slice needs.

Tests: 5 new introducers.integration-spec.ts tests + 7 new leads.integration-spec.ts tests (create with actor-linked-employee default, client-without-clientProfile rejection, invalid-stage rejection, diffed update + stale-version rejection, updates ordering newest-first, cross-org isolation) -- 91 integration tests pass workspace-wide (79 + 5 + 7), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all 9 workspaces. ## Document view/download + optional password-protected encryption

```text
Date: 2026-08-05
Phase: Phase 3 follow-up (feature, not a new slice)
Task: user asked (1) how documents are grouped/traceable on S3 (answered directly, no code change -- storage key is organizationId/documentId, already easy to trace) and (2) for two new capabilities the Documents backend never had at all: view/download, and an optional upload-time password such that a document can only be re-opened with that same password. Plan-mode pass completed first; the user chose true client-side encryption (AES-256-GCM, no server-side recovery ever) over a simpler recoverable server-side access-gate design, and chose "view" = open in the browser's native viewer via a new tab, not a custom preview pane. Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-055.
Schema: Document gained isEncrypted/encryptionSalt/encryptionIv columns (migration 20260805123911_documents_encryption). Applied via hand-written SQL + psql + `prisma migrate resolve --applied` rather than `prisma migrate dev`, because an earlier session's hand-edit of the already-applied phase3_02_leads migration left Prisma's checksum bookkeeping unable to reconcile automatically -- migrate dev demanded a full migrate reset, refused as destructive; no data was lost.
Backend: DocumentsService gained getDownloadUrl (presigned GetObjectCommand, 5-minute expiry, audits a new DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED action -- closes a docs/09-security-audit.md mandatory-audit-coverage gap download could never meet before since it didn't exist). initiateUpload persists the three new fields and validates isEncrypted=true implies both salt and IV are present. New document.download permission (docs/04 already named it in prose for a not-yet-built screen) granted to every document.read role plus explicitly to CEO_PERMISSIONS (CEO's automatic *.read-filter grant doesn't catch it).
Frontend: new apps/web/src/lib/document-crypto.ts (first file in a new entity-agnostic lib/ directory) with encryptFile/decryptToBlob/base64 helpers -- PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 (600,000 iterations) + AES-256-GCM via the Web Crypto API, all client-side. Leads Documents tab gained a "Password-protect this document" checkbox (with an explicit no-recovery-if-lost warning) and View/Download actions per row with a lock icon for encrypted documents; a password-prompt Modal appears when opening an encrypted document, with a clear retry-able error on a wrong password.
Two real bugs found and fixed during live verification (neither in the crypto design itself): (1) window.open() called after the async fetch/decrypt work is no longer tied closely enough to the original click for Chromium's popup blocker -- fixed with a synthetic <a> element click, which reliably survives an intervening await. (2) A target="_blank" anchor with rel="noopener" can land the new tab in a different renderer process, and blob: object URLs are only resolvable in the process that created them, leaving the tab blank -- avoided by opening the presigned S3 URL directly for unencrypted documents (no blob needed at all there) and accepting blob-URL-in-new-tab only where a client-side decrypt makes it unavoidable (the encrypted case), which tested reliably. A third apparent failure was the verification script itself targeting a stale leftover document (newest-first list, script used .last()), not an application defect.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260805123911_documents_encryption (new), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (document.download permission), packages/contracts/src/{ports/document.port.ts,routers/documents.ts}, apps/api/src/documents/documents.service.ts, apps/web/src/lib/document-crypto.ts (new), apps/web/src/lib/document-crypto.test.ts (new), apps/web/src/app/(platform)/leads/[id]/page.tsx, apps/api/src/documents/documents.integration-spec.ts, docs/12 (ADR-055).
Migrations: 20260805123911_documents_encryption -- three nullable/defaulted columns only, no data migration needed, reversible.
Tests: 5 new documents.integration-spec.ts tests (persists encryption fields; rejects isEncrypted=true with a missing salt or IV; getDownloadUrl returns a presigned URL and audits DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED for an UPLOADED document; rejects for PENDING_UPLOAD/DELETED documents; cross-org isolation) plus 4 new document-crypto.test.ts unit tests (round-trip, wrong-password rejection, distinct salt/IV per encryption, base64 round-trip) -- 97 integration tests and 9 web unit tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all 9 workspaces. Verified live against the real configured S3 bucket via Playwright: uploaded a plain and a password-protected document, confirmed the plain one opens immediately with no prompt, the encrypted one prompts for a password, rejects a wrong password with a clear retry-able error, and opens correctly with the right one -- zero console errors, 8/8 checks passing across two consecutive runs.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-055 (client-side-encryption-over-server-gate choice, crypto parameters, native-viewer view behavior, document.download permission, no-recovery-by-design, the two popup/blob bugs found and fixed).
Known issues: none new.
Next task: unchanged -- Slice 3 (Tenders) needs its own plan-mode pass before implementation.
```

## Phase 3, Slice 3.3: Tenders

```text
Date: 2026-08-05
Phase: Phase 3 -- Parties, CRM, and projects (Slice 3.3 of the planned sequence; Slice 3.1 Party/Client and 3.2 Leads done)
Task: independent Tender lifecycle screen -- list/add/edit/detail/documents/updates. Unlike every prior CRM screen, no HTML prototype exists for Tender at all (docs/16, docs/04 both say so explicitly) -- built by following the Lead visual system per docs/04's own instruction. Plan-mode pass completed first; full rationale in docs/12 ADR-056.
Schema: new Tender (client/lead/tenderPortal/assignedToEmployee FKs, tenderNumber/title/description, submissionStatus+result as two orthogonal fields instead of one collapsed stage, estimatedValue, four dates, reminderOffset, internalNote) and TenderUpdate (append-only timeline, identical shape to LeadUpdate) models. TenderPortal (schema/API-only since Phase 2, seeded in commit 479c347, never wired to a screen until now) gained @@unique([id, organizationId]) as an FK target. Back-relations added to Party/TenderPortal/Employee/Lead.
Real, pre-existing bug found and fixed, unrelated to Tender itself: apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts hardcoded a permission-catalog count of 29, but the real seeded count was already 30 (document.download, added earlier the same day for ADR-055, was never reflected here) -- a fresh non-cached run confirmed it failing, even though the prior full sweep had reported all-passing due to a stale Turbo cache-hit replaying older logs. Fixed in this same change: 29 -> 30 (the gap) -> 34 (four new tender.* keys).
Backend: packages/contracts/src/ports/tender.port.ts (new), apps/api/src/tenders/* (new service/module mirroring leads/* closely), packages/contracts/src/routers/tenders.ts (new), wired into context/router/app.module/main like every other module. Four new permission keys (tender.read/create/update/export) granted to Admin (all), Accountant/Project Manager/Admin Staff (tender.read, mirroring lead.read exactly), Employee (none), CEO (automatic via the existing *.read filter).
Frontend: (platform)/tenders/{page.tsx,shared.tsx,[id]/page.tsx} (list+filters+drawer; detail with Detail/Documents/Updates tabs), new TendersIcon + "Tenders" nav item under CRM. The existing Lead DocumentsTab (built in ADR-055, ~250 lines of upload/encrypt/view/download logic) was extracted into a shared apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx, parameterized by entityType/entityId, so Tender reuses it verbatim instead of duplicating it -- the underlying documents router was already entity-agnostic. formatDate/formatMoney were promoted alongside it into a new apps/web/src/lib/format.ts (previously private to leads/shared.tsx, which now just re-exports them). The Updates tab was deliberately NOT extracted the same way -- TenderUpdate is its own model behind its own router procedures, not leads.*, so a shared component would be premature abstraction across two different backing routers; it stays duplicated (small, per-entity).
Operational gotcha (environment, not code): this dev environment's API runs as a compiled `node apps/api/dist/main` in a long-lived tmux pane, not npm run dev's watch mode -- a new backend module (the tenders router) 404'd until an explicit rebuild + process restart, discovered via live verification. Not a defect; documented for the next slice.
