## Bugfix: Documents/Updates tabs showed "0 items" instead of an error on fetch failure

```text
Date: 2026-08-06
Phase: Phase 3 follow-up (bugfix, not a new slice)
Task: user reported uploaded documents "disappearing" after each server restart, showing 0 documents in the UI. Investigation found the data was never lost -- documents.integration confirmed all rows and their document_links stayed intact in Postgres throughout. The real bug: DocumentsTab (apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx) and both Lead/Tender detail pages' UpdatesTab only ever checked query.isLoading, never query.isError -- a failed fetch (e.g. the API briefly unreachable during this dev environment's manual restart, per ADR-056's documented gotcha) rendered identically to a genuinely empty list ("No documents yet." / "No updates yet."), with no indication anything had gone wrong.
Fix: all three components now check isError before falling through to the empty-state branch, showing "Could not load documents/updates. Please retry." plus a Retry button (calling the query's own refetch()) instead of the misleading empty state. Matches the existing errorMessage pattern already used by EntityTable elsewhere in the app (Lead/Tender list pages), just applied to these two hand-rolled (non-EntityTable) list displays that had been missing it.
Files changed: apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/leads/[id]/page.tsx, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/tenders/[id]/page.tsx.
Migrations: none.
Tests: no new automated tests (defensive UI state, not a business rule). Verified live via Playwright using a route-level fault injection (parses the batched tRPC response and replaces just the listLinksForEntity/listUpdates procedure results with a simulated 500, leaving leads.getDetail/tenders.getDetail intact in the same batch) -- confirmed the page shell still loads, both tabs show the new error state with a visible Retry button instead of the misleading empty state, and clicking Retry after removing the fault injection recovers with real data. 6/6 checks passed. Full lint/typecheck green (forced fresh, no Turbo cache).
Decisions added/changed: none (defensive UI fix, no new business rule).
Known issues: none new.
Next task: unchanged -- Slice 3.4 (Projects core) needs its own plan-mode pass before implementation.
```

## Phase 3, Slice 3.4: Projects core

```text
Date: 2026-08-06
Phase: Phase 3 -- Parties, CRM, and projects (Slice 3.4 of the planned sequence; Slices 3.1-3.3 done)
Task: first slice where a Project exists at all, and the first where a Project may reference a Lead, a Tender, or both (named Phase 3 exit criterion). Unlike Tender, the stage/status model was not invented -- docs/05-workflows-state-machines.md already specifies it: stage is a six-value forward pipeline starting at WON (a project only ever exists after something was won), operationalStatus is a separate field (ACTIVE/ON_HOLD/CANCELLED/CLOSED). Plan-mode pass completed first; full rationale in docs/12 ADR-057.
Schema: new Project model (client/legalEntity required; lead/tender/projectManager/introducer/projectType all independently nullable; name/description/stage/operationalStatus/value/budget/gstApplicable/workOrderNumber/workOrderDate). legalEntityId is Project's first real non-admin consumer of LegalEntity. ProjectType (schema/API-only since Phase 2) gets its first real consuming screen and gained @@unique([id, organizationId]) as an FK target, same fix every newly-activated master has needed.
Backend: ProjectsService is the first service in this codebase with dedicated state-transition commands (changeStage/changeStatus) entirely separate from update -- stage/operationalStatus are not even present in the update input type, so they can only change via the explicit commands. Transition rules: forward stage moves only to the immediate next stage (no skipping), backward moves to any earlier stage require and record a reason; status toggles freely between ACTIVE/ON_HOLD, cancelling requires a reason from either, CLOSED is only reachable once stage is SUCCESSFULLY_COMPLETED; once CANCELLED/CLOSED, both commands reject any further change (no reversal path this slice). Six new permission keys (project.read/create/update/export/change_stage/change_status) -- the first role permission set in this codebase shaped around a role's own namesake domain rather than uniform read/write: Project Manager gets read+change_stage+change_status but not create/update (PMs operate on assigned projects, they don't originate them).
Frontend: (platform)/projects/{page.tsx,shared.tsx,[id]/page.tsx} -- list+filters+drawer; detail page's "Stage & Status Actions" card offers only the single valid forward button plus a "Move Backward..." action opening a reason-required Modal (same UX shape as Financial Years' reopen-reason modal), so the UI itself never exposes a skip-ahead control. New "Projects" nav group (not folded into "CRM" -- docs/16's prototype itself separates 02-crm/ from 03-projects/ as distinct folders) with a new ProjectsIcon.
Deliberately deferred, disclosed not hidden: no Documents/Progress tab (Slice 3.6's own deliverable), no "Convert to Project" auto-populate flow from Lead/Tender detail pages (plain manual origin dropdowns instead, same as Tender's own optional Lead origin), no Project Manager scope enforcement (Slice 3.5's own named exit criterion -- project.read currently returns organization-wide results for every permitted role), no delete/deactivate (retirement via CANCELLED/CLOSED terminal status instead, mirroring Lead/Tender).
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260806064500_phase3_04_projects_core (new), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (permission keys + role grants), packages/contracts/src/{ports/project.port.ts (new),ports/index.ts,index.ts,context.ts,router.ts,routers/projects.ts (new)}, apps/api/src/{projects/* (new),app.module.ts,main.ts,rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (permission count fix)}, packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx,nav-items.ts}, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/{page.tsx,shared.tsx,[id]/page.tsx} (all new), docs/12 (ADR-057).
Migrations: phase3_04_projects_core -- hand-written and applied via psql + prisma migrate resolve --applied rather than prisma migrate dev; the same phase3_02_leads checksum-drift block recurred a third time (also hit in Slices 3.3 and via ADR-055), resolved the same non-destructive way, no data lost.
Tests: 14 new projects.integration-spec.ts tests (create defaulting to WON/ACTIVE; create with optional Lead+Tender origin; client-without-clientProfile and nonexistent-legal-entity rejection; diffed update excluding stage/status + stale-version rejection; cross-org isolation; forward stage move; rejected skip-ahead; backward move requiring/recording a reason; version-conflicted stage change; free ACTIVE<->ON_HOLD toggle; cancellation requiring a reason from both statuses; full lockout once cancelled; CLOSED only reachable from SUCCESSFULLY_COMPLETED) -- 119 integration tests pass workspace-wide (105 + 14), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green, run with Turbo's cache forced off per the ADR-056 cache-replay lesson. The API was proactively rebuilt and restarted before live verification this time, per ADR-056's own documented gotcha -- avoided a repeat of the Tender slice's mid-verification surprise. Verified live via Playwright: created a project, confirmed WON/ACTIVE defaults, walked a forward stage transition, confirmed no skip-ahead control exists in the UI, moved backward with a required reason, toggled hold/resume freely, cancelled with a required reason, confirmed the terminal state disables every further action -- zero console errors, 12/13 checks passing (the one miss was the verification script's own nav-timing flake, confirmed passing on retest, not an app defect).
Decisions added/changed: ADR-057 (stage/status as dedicated commands separate from update, transition rules, field set and LegalEntity/ProjectType activation, deferred Documents/Convert-to-Project/PM-scoping/delete, the domain-shaped Project Manager permission set, the new Projects nav group).
Known issues: none new.
Next task: Slice 3.5 (Project team assignments) needs its own plan-mode pass before implementation, per CLAUDE.md's one-slice-at-a-time rule -- it's what actually implements the Project Manager scope enforcement deferred in decision (7) above.
```

## Phase 3, Slice 3.5: Project team assignments

```text
Date: 2026-08-06
Phase: Phase 3 -- Parties, CRM, and projects (Slice 3.5 of the planned sequence; Slices 3.1-3.4 done)
Task: implements Phase 3's own named exit criterion, "Project Manager scope is enforced", deferred explicitly when Projects core shipped (ADR-057 decision 7). The first place in this codebase where authorization depends on which rows an actor is tied to, not just a yes/no permission gate. Plan-mode pass completed first, including two clarifying questions resolved with the user (scoping mechanism, team-management ownership) before designing further; full rationale in docs/12 ADR-058.
Schema: new ProjectTeamMember model (project-scoped roster, additive to Project.projectManagerId, not a replacement) -- shape mirrors UserRole's own precedent (simple grant/revoke join row, hard delete on removal, no soft-delete/version). "Assigned to a project" = projectManagerId match OR a ProjectTeamMember row, uniformly.
Backend: new project.read_all permission -- Admin/Accountant/Admin Staff/CEO get it (unscoped), Project Manager doesn't (scoped to assigned projects only) -- a permission-key check, not role-name string matching, consistent with every other authorization decision in this codebase. Scoping applies to reads AND commands (list/getDetail/update/changeStage/changeStatus/listTeamMembers), per docs/04's explicit "queries, commands, file access and exports" text. Out-of-scope single-record access returns NOT_FOUND (not FORBIDDEN), matching this codebase's existing cross-org-isolation philosophy of "act like it doesn't exist." New listTeamMembers/addTeamMember/removeTeamMember, gated by project.update (Admin-only, per the user's explicit choice -- PMs execute stage/status but don't configure the team roster) and blocked once a project is cancelled/closed.
Frontend: (platform)/projects/[id]/page.tsx restructured to use Tabs (Detail/Team) for the first time -- previously a flat single-section layout, now matches Lead/Tender's own tabbed pattern. New Team tab: roster list + inline add form (Employee select + optional free-text role), gated by project.update.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260806072600_phase3_05_project_team_members (new), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (project.read_all permission + grants), packages/contracts/src/{ports/project.port.ts,routers/projects.ts}, apps/api/src/{projects/projects.service.ts,rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (permission count fix)}, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/page.tsx, docs/12 (ADR-058).
Migrations: phase3_05_project_team_members -- hand-written and applied via psql + prisma migrate resolve --applied; the same phase3_02_leads checksum-drift block recurred a fourth time (also hit in Slices 3.3 and 3.4), resolved the same non-destructive way, no data lost.
Tests: 9 new projects.integration-spec.ts tests (scoped actor sees only projectManagerId-matched or ProjectTeamMember-matched projects; getDetail on an out-of-scope project rejects NOT_FOUND; team-member-only assignment also grants scope; an unscoped canSeeAll actor sees everything; an actor with no linked Employee gets an empty list and NOT_FOUND; changeStage rejects out-of-scope and succeeds in-scope; team add/list/duplicate-rejection; employee-must-exist-in-org rejection; remove + re-remove NOT_FOUND; team add/remove blocked once cancelled) -- 128 integration tests pass workspace-wide (119 + 9), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green, forced fresh. The API was rebuilt and restarted before live verification, continuing the established habit from ADR-056/057. Verified live via Playwright with a real second login: created a real Project-Manager-role user (linked Employee, real argon2-hashed password), created two projects as Admin, assigned the new user to one via the Team tab, logged in as that user and confirmed they saw only the assigned project in /projects (not the unassigned one), and confirmed they could successfully execute a stage change on their own project -- 6/6 checks passing, zero console errors.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-058 (ProjectTeamMember shape, uniform assignment definition, project.read_all permission mechanism, scoping applied to reads and commands alike, NOT_FOUND for out-of-scope access, Team tab UX and Admin-only management, projectManagerId left untouched).
Known issues: none new.
Next task: Slice 3.6 (Project documents/progress) needs its own plan-mode pass before implementation, per CLAUDE.md's one-slice-at-a-time rule -- the third real consumer of the Documents backend, reusing the shared DocumentsTab component from ADR-056.
```

## Cross-cutting fix: deactivated lookup records in forms vs. filters

```text
Date: 2026-08-06
Phase: cross-cutting bugfix, not tied to a specific roadmap slice
Task: user's explicit policy directive -- deactivated records must remain visible in filters and read-only views, but must never be selectable when creating or editing a record. Audit found clients.list/legalEntities.list/introducers.list had no active/inactive filtering at all (every create/edit form's Client/Legal-Entity/Introducer dropdown offered deactivated records), and, in the opposite direction, four masters.list call sites used in filter rows (Leads/Tenders/Projects type filters, Employees Department/Designation filters) wrongly excluded inactive records from a filter context. Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-060.
Backend: ClientPort/LegalEntityPort/IntroducerPort.list() extended with includeInactive: boolean | undefined, matching the existing masters/geography/employees convention (omitted defaults to active-only). apps/api/src/{clients/clients.service.ts, organization/legal-entity.service.ts, introducers/introducers.service.ts} and their routers (packages/contracts/src/routers/{clients,legal-entities,introducers}.ts) updated accordingly.
Frontend: 15 call sites across leads/, tenders/, projects/, financial-years/, clients/, legal-entities/, introducers/, employees/ classified individually as form (includeInactive: false) or filter/entity-own-list-page (includeInactive: true). Two screens (financial-years' Legal Entity selector, employees' Department/Designation) serve both a filter/table-lookup role and a create-form role from what was a single query -- split into separate "all" and "active" queries so neither behavior regresses the other.
Files changed: packages/contracts/src/{ports/{client,legal-entity,introducer}.port.ts, routers/{clients,legal-entities,introducers}.ts}, apps/api/src/{clients/clients.service.ts, organization/legal-entity.service.ts, introducers/introducers.service.ts}, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{leads,tenders,projects,financial-years,clients,legal-entities,introducers,employees}/{page.tsx,shared.tsx}, docs/12 (ADR-060).
Tests: three new dedicated assertions (clients/introducers/legalEntities integration specs) confirming list defaults to active-only after deactivation and includeInactive: true still surfaces the inactive row; existing deactivate-then-list assertions in the same three specs fixed to pass includeInactive: true so they keep exercising the path they always tested. 128 integration tests pass workspace-wide, full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green, forced fresh. API rebuilt and restarted. Live-verified directly against the running API (created a real Introducer, deactivated it, confirmed the default list excludes it and includeInactive: true includes it with isActive: false, then cleaned up).

Next task: none specific -- return to Slice 3.6 (Project documents/progress) per the prior entry's plan.

## Phase 3, Slice 3.6: Project documents and physical progress

```text
Date: 2026-08-06
Phase: Phase 3 -- Parties, CRM, and projects (Slice 3.6 of 6, the last unstarted slice; Slices 3.1-3.5 done). Phase 3 is now fully complete.
Task: implements the two remaining docs/04 §10 screens -- project-physical.html (projectProgress.list/create) and project-documentation.html/project-document-upload.html (document.list/upload/download). Plan-mode pass completed first; full rationale in docs/12 ADR-061.
Schema: new ProjectProgress model -- append-only physical-progress timeline shaped like LeadUpdate/TenderUpdate plus a percentage column. No denormalized "current %" on Project -- derived from the latest (highest-percentage) row, same read-model-from-append-only-table precedent as LeadUpdate/TenderUpdate.
Backend: percentage must be an integer 0-100 and strictly greater than the current latest (0 baseline) -- "each update can only increase %" -- enforced in ProjectsService, not the database; rejected once the project is cancelled/closed, the same terminal-status guard team management uses. New project_progress.create permission, granted to Admin + Project Manager (same execution-domain reasoning as change_stage/change_status). listProgress/addProgress reuse getDetail's own Project-Manager scope check, same pattern listTeamMembers already established. The documents router (packages/contracts/src/routers/documents.ts) gained a targeted PROJECT-only scope guard on listLinksForEntity/linkToEntity, reusing ProjectsService.getDetail's existing NOT_FOUND-on-out-of-scope check rather than making the entity-agnostic DocumentsService aware of Projects -- closes docs/04's "file access" scoping requirement for the one entity type that has row-level scoping.
Also fixed in this slice, at the user's explicit request after being offered the choice: a pre-existing bug where document.manage (required by linkToEntity to complete any upload) was granted to Admin only, so despite Accountant/Admin Staff/Employee/Project Manager all holding document.upload, none of them could actually finish an upload on any entity -- including the already-shipped Lead/Tender Documents tabs. Now granted everywhere document.upload already is; Project Manager also gained document.upload itself (it had neither permission before).
Frontend: apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx's entityType widened to 'LEAD' | 'TENDER' | 'PROJECT' (the component's own comment already anticipated this). (platform)/projects/[id]/page.tsx gained two new tabs: Documentation (one-line DocumentsTab reuse, no category/search filters or stat tiles -- same simplification already shipped for Lead/Tender) and Physical Progress (new ProgressTab component -- timeline card, current-progress summary card, Add Progress Update form with an antd Slider bounded [currentPercent + 1, 100], gated by project_progress.create, hidden once terminal or at 100%; omits the mockup's "vs X% financial" comparison since no financial/milestone backend exists yet).

No dedicated test for the documents.ts router-level PROJECT scope guard -- this codebase has never unit-tested tRPC router logic directly, so it was verified live instead. The API was rebuilt and restarted before live verification. No browser-automation tool is available this session, so live verification was done directly against the running API via curl with real session cookies: created a real Project-Manager-role test user (real argon2-hashed password, linked Employee) unassigned to an existing real project, confirmed listProgress and documents.listLinksForEntity/linkToEntity both reject NOT_FOUND for that project; added a progress update as Admin, confirmed a lower/equal percentage is rejected; assigned the test user to the project via addTeamMember, confirmed listProgress/addProgress then succeed for that same actor; performed a complete upload-confirm-link cycle against the real configured S3 bucket as the non-Admin test user to confirm the document.manage bugfix actually unblocks upload end-to-end, and confirmed the document appears in listLinksForEntity -- all test rows cleaned up afterward, confirmed the project's one pre-existing (unrelated) team member was left untouched.

## Phase 4, Slice 4.1: Chart of Accounts

```text
Date: 2026-08-07
Phase: Phase 4 -- Milestones and accounting core (Slice 4.1 of many; the first Phase 4 slice, Phase 3 fully complete)
Task: Chart of Accounts -- the root of the entire accounting subtree (docs/03-domain-model.md: chart_of_accounts -> account_groups + ledger_accounts), a NEW_REQUIRED screen per docs/04-screen-contracts.md line 565. A first plan draft was rejected and substantially revised after detailed user feedback demanding a durable accounting foundation (structurally sound classification, every future journal-line dimension attachable later, audit-complete, optimistic-concurrency safe, no physical deletion) and a simple daily UI (one page, two plain tables, derived fields read-only, no trees or nested routes). Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-062.
Schema: new AccountGroup (code/name/description, accountType + normalBalance stored explicitly on every row and inherited by custom groups from their parent at creation, parentGroupId self-referencing, isSystemDefined, isActive, version, audit columns) and LedgerAccount (code/name/description, accountGroupId FK, allowManualPosting, isActive, version, audit columns -- no accountType/normalBalance of its own, always derived via the group join) models plus AccountType/NormalBalance enums.
Backend: ChartOfAccountsService (not a monolithic AccountingPort) enforces: code normalization (trim+uppercase) before every write, with a hand-written expression-index defense-in-depth at the database level independent of the application write path; accountType/normalBalance inheritance and pairing; every custom group requires an active parent; system-defined roots are permanently fixed (code/type/normalBalance/null-parent immutable, never deactivatable, name/description still editable); direct and indirect re-parent cycle rejection (walks the candidate parent's chain); re-parenting/account-moving only permitted to a classification-compatible active target; group deactivation blocked while active dependents exist, with a specific message. New chart_of_account.read/manage permissions -- Admin automatic, Accountant both explicitly, CEO read automatic via the *.read filter.
Frontend: new (platform)/chart-of-accounts/page.tsx under a new "Finance" nav group -- one page, Account Groups and Ledger Accounts sections, one drawer each, Account Type/Normal Balance always shown read-only and derived from the selected parent/group, never editable; every manage control entirely absent (not disabled) for a read-only viewer.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260807060000_phase4_01_chart_of_accounts (new, hand-written from the start for the expression indexes), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (two permissions + five seeded root groups), packages/contracts/src/{ports/chart-of-accounts.port.ts (new), ports/index.ts, index.ts, context.ts, router.ts, routers/chart-of-accounts.ts (new)}, apps/api/src/accounting/{accounting.module.ts,chart-of-accounts.service.ts} (new), apps/api/src/{app.module.ts,main.ts}, apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (permission count fix), packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx,nav-items.ts}, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/chart-of-accounts/page.tsx (new), docs/12 (ADR-062).
Migrations: phase4_01_chart_of_accounts -- hand-written from the start (needed two Postgres expression indexes Prisma's schema DSL can't express), applied via psql + prisma migrate resolve --applied; the recurring phase3_02_leads checksum-drift block was also hit when migrate dev --create-only was first attempted.
Tests: 18 new chart-of-accounts.integration-spec.ts tests covering seeded-root correctness, classification inheritance, missing/inactive-parent rejection, normalized duplicate-code rejection, system-root immutability with allowed name/description edits, direct/indirect cycle rejection, incompatible/inactive re-parent and account-move rejection, dependent-blocked deactivation, deactivate/reactivate lifecycles, stale-version rejection, cross-org isolation, and full audit old/new values on a move -- 151 integration tests pass workspace-wide (133 + 18), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration/production-build green across all workspaces, forced fresh. roles.integration-spec.ts catalog count updated 42 -> 44. A genuinely fresh database replayed the full 22-migration history end-to-end via prisma migrate deploy with zero errors, confirmed via prisma migrate diff to introduce zero drift beyond a pre-existing, unrelated cities/districts drift. The API was rebuilt and restarted before live verification. No browser-automation tool is available this session, so live verification was done directly against the running API via curl: confirmed the five roots and their protections, created a real custom group and ledger account, confirmed normalized-duplicate rejection and a stale-version 409, and confirmed a real Project-Manager-role test user is correctly FORBIDDEN from every chartOfAccounts procedure -- all test rows and the test user cleaned up afterward. The UI-specific requirements (derived fields read-only, hidden-not-disabled controls, responsive layout, zero console errors) could only be verified by static code review this session, not a live browser pass -- disclosed as an open gap, not claimed as done.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-062 (AccountGroup/LedgerAccount classification model, inheritance and immutability rules, cycle and dependent-deactivation guards, code normalization with database-level defense-in-depth, bounded ChartOfAccountsPort, permissions, Finance nav group, UI derived-field/hidden-control rules).
Known issues: UI frontend requirements not live-verified (no browser tool this session) -- recommend a real click-through before considering this slice's UI fully done.
Next task: remaining Phase 4 slices (CAPEX/OPEX milestones, payment alerts, journal entries, posting engine, idempotency registry, accounting period lock enforcement, reversal mechanism, EMD/SD) each need their own plan-mode pass before implementation, per CLAUDE.md's one-slice-at-a-time rule.
```

## Phase 4.1 verification-only pass and baseline freeze

```text
Date: 2026-08-07
Phase: Phase 4 -- Milestones and accounting core (verification pass on the completed Slice 4.1, no new slice work)
Task: user-requested verification-only pass on Chart of Accounts before starting Slice 4.2, covering CEO/read-only visibility, Accountant/Admin create-edit-deactivate workflows, derived-classification read-only rendering, drawer validation and dependent-block messages, responsive/keyboard/console behavior, and loading/empty/API-error states. No source changes were authorized unless the pass revealed a genuine defect.
Method: no browser-automation tool is available this session (same limitation disclosed in ADR-062) -- covered every checklist item that doesn't strictly require a rendered browser via (a) a full code-review pass of apps/web/src/app/(platform)/chart-of-accounts/page.tsx against each item, and (b) live API verification via curl against the running API (rebuilt from the frozen commit, restarted) using two new real test users -- a real Accountant-role and a real CEO-role user (real argon2-hashed passwords, via the same direct-Prisma test-user pattern used throughout this session -- cleaned up afterward).
Findings:
- CEO/read-only: confirmed via auth.me that the CEO test user holds chart_of_account.read only (no .manage) -- since every manage control in the page (Add Group/Add Account buttons, and the entire row-actions column for both tables) is gated by `canManage ? ... : null` (rendered absent, not disabled), a CEO sees zero manage controls. Confirmed via curl that every backend mutation (createGroup/setGroupActive/createAccount) independently rejects this user with FORBIDDEN regardless of UI state.
- Accountant/Admin workflows: confirmed via auth.me that the Accountant test user holds both chart_of_account.read and .manage. Live-drove the exact sequence the drawers perform: created a custom group under Assets, edited its name/description (classification fields correctly not part of the update payload), created a ledger account under it, attempted to deactivate the group while the account was still active (rejected with "Cannot deactivate — 1 active ledger account(s) depend on this group."), deactivated the account, then deactivated the group successfully -- full lifecycle round-trip confirmed working end-to-end for a real Accountant session, not just Admin.
- Derived classification read-only: confirmed by code review that the Account Type/Normal Balance display block in the group drawer is a plain read-only `<div>`, not wrapped in a `Form.Item` with a `name`, so it can never be part of a submitted payload regardless of what the parent selector shows.
- Drawer validation and dependent-block messages: confirmed by code review that every required field (Code, Name, Parent Group, Account Code, Account Name, Account Group) has a `required` rule with a specific message, and max-length rules match the backend's column limits. Confirmed live that the backend's specific, actionable error strings (dependent-block count, non-existent parent, normalized-duplicate code, VERSION_CONFLICT) are passed through verbatim to the user via each mutation's `onError: (error) => message.error(error.message)` -- the same wiring pattern already used on every other page in this app.
- Loading/empty/API-error states: confirmed by code review that both tables wire `loading`/`errorMessage`/`emptyTitle` to `EntityTable`, the same component and pattern used on every other list screen. One minor, non-functional inconsistency noted, not fixed as directed (no source changes without a genuine defect): the Ledger Accounts table shows an `emptyDescription` hint ("Use \"Add Account\" to create one.") for managers, but the Account Groups table has no equivalent hint -- cosmetic only, does not affect correctness.
- Not verifiable this session (no browser tool): responsive layout at specific breakpoints, keyboard/focus navigation, and zero-console-errors. Recommend a real click-through once a browser tool or the user is available to check.
No genuine defect was found. No source files were changed in this pass.
Decisions added/changed: none.
Known issues: same as noted at Slice 4.1 completion -- browser-only UI checks (responsive layout, keyboard/focus, console errors) remain unverified this session; the emptyDescription inconsistency on Account Groups noted above but left as-is pending user direction.
Baseline freeze: commit d84f3642d37a318e401c3ccc440d112c8b626df7 ("Phase 4.1: Chart of Accounts") is confirmed as the frozen Phase 4.1 baseline -- full lint/typecheck/unit/integration/production-build sweep re-run clean at this commit (151 integration tests passing), working tree clean, no changes made during this verification pass.
Next task: proceed to Slice 4.2 -- needs its own plan-mode pass before implementation, per CLAUDE.md's one-slice-at-a-time rule.

## Phase 4, Slice 4.2: CAPEX/OPEX Milestones -- implementation complete, UI verification pending

```text
Date: 2026-08-08
Status: Implementation complete, UI verification pending -- backend is fully implemented, tested, and live-verified via curl; the frontend has not yet had a real browser pass. Do not mark this slice's UI as done until a browser pass confirms: (1) CAPEX/OPEX drawer workflows and the live allocation balance; (2) the schedule preview and expandable installment rows; (3) role-based action visibility; (4) validation, responsive layout, and keyboard behaviour; (5) zero browser-console errors.
Phase: Phase 4 -- Milestones and accounting core (Slice 4.2 of many; Phase 4.1 Chart of Accounts frozen and verified)
Task: CAPEX/OPEX milestones and the installment schedule generator -- milestones and schedule generation only, no Payment Alerts/invoices/receipts/journals/postings this slice (docs/01, docs/05: "a schedule row creates an alert; it does not create an invoice or journal"). A first plan draft was rejected and comprehensively revised after a detailed 14-point user correction covering schema integrity, server-generated codes, an enforced CAPEX exact-allocation invariant, a missing updateEvent operation, a complete achievement lifecycle, independent service-period/invoice-date sequences, backend-authoritative money arithmetic, corrected summary cards, revised permissions, idempotent atomic creation, a simplified frontend, and full auditability. Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-063.
Schema: new ProjectMilestone (CAPEX/OPEX, code/name/approvedAmount, optional reference/approval-date/description, OPEX-only achievement/frequency/schedule fields CHECK-locked to type, lifecycle metadata), MilestoneEvent (CAPEX events, derived percentOfPool, own achievement lifecycle metadata), MilestoneInstallment (OPEX generated schedule rows), MilestoneCreationRequest (idempotency) -- with type-safe composite FKs (an event's FK triple can never match an OPEX-typed milestone, and vice versa) plus twelve hand-added CHECK constraints Prisma's DSL can't express.
Backend: MilestonesService enforces: retry-safe server-generated codes under concurrency; CAPEX event amounts sum to the pool's approvedAmount exactly on create and on updateEvent, with the precise shortfall/excess in the rejection message; percentOfPool always derived; a three-state achievement lifecycle (start/markAchieved/cancel, no skipping) for both CAPEX events and OPEX milestones, with lifecycle metadata stored on the row; OPEX milestone cancellation cascades every remaining PLANNED installment to CANCELLED in the same transaction, leaving already-SKIPPED rows untouched; one pure generateOpexSchedule function (Prisma.Decimal, calendar-safe end-of-month clamping, independent service-period/invoice-date sequences) shared by previewOpexSchedule, createOpex, and the tests; idempotent creation via a client-supplied key checked inside the retry loop. New milestone.read/create/update/mark_achieved/cancel/skip_installment permissions -- Admin automatic (all six), Accountant read/create/update/skip_installment (finance-maintained schedules), Project Manager read/mark_achieved/cancel (delivery execution), Admin Staff read, CEO read automatic.
Frontend: new MilestoneTab on the existing project detail page -- CAPEX/OPEX sections, server-computed summary totals (Contract Value from Project.value, no fabricated "Invoiced to Date" tile), a CAPEX Form.List drawer with a live running-allocated/remaining-balance display blocking Save until zero, an OPEX drawer whose schedule preview comes from previewOpexSchedule (never client-computed), achieve/cancel/skip modals, every action entirely absent (not disabled) when the actor lacks the permission.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260808070000_phase4_02_milestones (new, hand-written for the CHECK constraints), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (six permissions), apps/api/src/milestones/{milestone-schedule.util.ts,milestones.service.ts,milestones.module.ts} (new), packages/contracts/src/{ports/milestone.port.ts (new), ports/index.ts, index.ts, context.ts, router.ts, routers/milestones.ts (new)}, apps/api/src/{app.module.ts,main.ts}, apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (permission count fix), apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/{milestone-tab.tsx (new),page.tsx}, docs/12 (ADR-063).
Migrations: phase4_02_milestones -- generated via a plain prisma migrate dev first per the user's explicit no-pre-emptive-drift-assumption instruction, then hand-edited to append the twelve CHECK constraints; the recurring, already-understood phase3_02_leads checksum drift did reappear and was diagnosed (not assumed) as the same pre-existing artifact before falling back to the established psql + prisma migrate resolve --applied procedure.
Tests: 19 new milestones.integration-spec.ts tests (CAPEX exact-sum creation and audit, sum-mismatch rejection with the exact message, empty/non-positive rejection, concurrent-safe code generation, full event lifecycle including terminal-edit rejection, updateEvent re-validating the pool sum, idempotent-duplicate-returns-original, cross-org/out-of-scope rejection; OPEX schedule-matches-preview exactly, lifecycle with skip-ahead rejection, updateMilestone editable-fields-only, cancellation cascade leaving a pre-skipped installment untouched, skip-installment reason/already-skipped rejection, stale-version rejection; Project-Manager scoping; database CHECK-constraint rejections via raw SQL) plus 10 new milestone-schedule.util.spec.ts unit tests (calendar-safe end-of-month clamping including 31 Jan across leap/non-leap years, quarterly/yearly stepping, exact-sum and rounding-remainder-on-last-row schedule generation, consecutive/non-overlapping/inclusive periods, independent date sequences). A genuine bug was found and fixed during this slice's own test run: Prisma rejects a bare 'YYYY-MM-DD' string for @db.Date columns at runtime despite TypeScript accepting it -- fixed with a toDate() conversion helper applied at every date write site, caught by 8 of 19 integration tests failing identically on the first run. A separate test-authoring mistake (not a service defect) was also caught and corrected: a test wrongly expected a single CAPEX event's amount to change successfully while a sibling stayed fixed, which is mathematically impossible under the exact-sum invariant with only two events. 170 integration tests pass in apps/api (151 prior + 19 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all workspaces, forced fresh. roles.integration-spec.ts catalog count updated 44 -> 50. A genuinely fresh database replayed the full 23-migration history end-to-end via prisma migrate deploy with zero errors. apps/api/apps/worker production builds are clean; apps/web's production build hit the same pre-existing, non-deterministic upstream Next.js prerender bug already disclosed in ADR-043/052/053 (confirmed via a git-stash comparison against the unmodified baseline commit showing the identical failure) -- not a regression from this slice.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-063 (type-safe FKs and CHECK constraints, server-generated codes, CAPEX exact-allocation invariant, complete achievement lifecycle with OPEX cancellation cascade, independent service-period/invoice-date schedule generation, backend-authoritative money arithmetic, corrected summary cards, revised Accountant/PM permission split, idempotent atomic creation, simplified frontend, full auditability).
Known issues: UI-specific requirements (Form.List dynamic rows, live running-balance blocking Save, OPEX preview rendering, expandable installment rows, role-based control visibility, validation/error-state rendering, responsive layout, keyboard/focus behavior, zero console errors) could only be verified by static code review this session, not a live browser pass -- this slice's status stays "UI verification pending" (not "complete") until a real browser click-through confirms all five items in the Status line above. apps/web's production build hit the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js prerender bug already documented in ADR-043/052/053 (confirmed non-regression via a git-stash comparison against the unmodified baseline commit showing the identical failure) -- this remains a documented upstream baseline issue, not a Slice 4.2 regression, and does not block backend completion.
Next task: a browser pass on this slice's UI (see Status line) before it can be marked fully done; then remaining Phase 4 slices (payment alerts, journal entries, posting engine, idempotency registry, accounting period lock enforcement, reversal mechanism, EMD/SD) each need their own plan-mode pass before implementation, per CLAUDE.md's one-slice-at-a-time rule.
```

## Phase 4.1 follow-up: seed the working Chart of Accounts data

```text
Date: 2026-08-08
Phase: Phase 4 -- Milestones and accounting core (data-only follow-up on the frozen Slice 4.1 baseline; no schema/decision change)
Task: user request -- capture the Account Groups/Ledger Accounts already created live through the Chart of Accounts screen (during real use since the Slice 4.1 freeze) and add them to packages/database/prisma/seed.ts, so a fresh database or a reseed reproduces the same working chart instead of just the five bare system roots.
Method: read the live dev database directly (account_groups/ledger_accounts, joined to their parent/group) to get the ground truth rather than guessing -- found 7 active custom AccountGroups (all parented directly under one of the five roots: Bank Accounts/Client Receivables/Security Deposits/Tax Recoverable under Assets, Tax Payable under Liabilities, Project Revenue under Income, Administrative Expenses under Expenses) and 7 active LedgerAccounts (one per custom group). One stray row was deliberately excluded per the request's "active records" scope: a deactivated duplicate ledger account (code BANK-ACCOUNTS, a leftover under Assets directly, superseded by the correct HDFC_CURRENT_ACCOUNT under Bank Accounts) -- reproducing an inactive/superseded row would defeat the point of a clean seed.
Implementation: added ACCOUNT_GROUP_CUSTOM and LEDGER_ACCOUNT_CUSTOM arrays to seed.ts (same plain code/name/parent-or-group-code shape as every other lookup table in this file) and two new upsert loops immediately after the existing ACCOUNT_GROUP_ROOTS loop -- custom groups resolve their parent's id by code and inherit its accountType/normalBalance (mirroring ChartOfAccountsService's own inheritance rule, decision 3 of ADR-062, rather than hardcoding the resulting type/balance separately and risking drift from that rule); ledger accounts resolve their group's id by code. Both loops use the same organizationId_code upsert key as every other seeded table, so reseeding is idempotent -- reran on the live dev database and confirmed zero duplicates and zero row changes (group/account counts unchanged before and after).
Verification: full lint/typecheck clean for packages/database. Reseeded the live dev database directly -- idempotent, no changes. Additionally provisioned a genuinely fresh scratch database (zitaware_crm_seed_check, created and dropped for this check), replayed the full migration history via prisma migrate deploy, then ran the seed script from zero -- the resulting 12 account_groups and 7 ledger_accounts matched the live dev database's active records exactly (same codes, same parent/group linkage, same accountType/normalBalance).
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (ACCOUNT_GROUP_CUSTOM, LEDGER_ACCOUNT_CUSTOM, two new upsert loops).
Decisions added/changed: none -- this reproduces existing live data under the classification rules ADR-062 already established; it does not add a new group/account, workflow, or permission.
Known issues: none.
Next task: unchanged -- Slice 4.2's UI verification pass, then the remaining Phase 4 slices, per the prior entry.
```

## Phase 4, Slice 4.3: Payment Alerts -- implementation complete, UI verification pending

```text
Date: 2026-08-09
Status: Implementation complete, UI verification pending -- backend and the worker job are fully implemented, tested, and live-verified via curl plus a direct worker-function invocation; the frontend has not yet had a real browser pass. Do not mark this slice's UI as done until a browser pass confirms: (1) the Snooze modal workflow; (2) the disabled Raise Invoice tooltip and disabled state; (3) role-based control visibility (Snooze absent, not disabled, for a read-only viewer); (4) the four summary tiles and Overdue/Due/Snoozed badge rendering; (5) responsive layout, keyboard/focus behaviour, and zero console errors.
Phase: Phase 4 -- Milestones and accounting core (Slice 4.3 of many; Phase 4.2 Milestones implementation complete, UI verification still pending from the prior slice)
Task: Payment Alerts -- the workflow chain is Milestone/Installment -> Alert -> Invoice -> Receipt -> Deductions -> Journal -> Bank Match, and Milestones (4.2) is the only piece built so far. docs/04 section 11.3 and docs/05's OPEX-schedule state machine both describe this screen, but the screen's own contract also lists invoice.createDraftFromAlert -- Phase 5 territory per docs/11's own split. This slice's boundary is alerts and snooze only, the same split ADR-063 used to defer alerts out of Milestones. Two genuine business-rule ambiguities (CAPEX due-or-achieved OR-vs-AND trigger; who "Account Team" resolves to) were confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion before finalizing the plan, not guessed silently. Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-064.
Schema: new unified PaymentAlert model (sourceType discriminator, sourceEventId/sourceInstallmentId hard-unique nullable FKs, status OPEN|SNOOZED only this slice, snooze fields) plus two enums -- CHECK-enforced so exactly one source FK is set matching sourceType, and snoozed_until is set iff status is SNOOZED. No stored amount/due-date copy -- always read live via the source relation.
Backend: PaymentAlertsService (listForProject computes derived Overdue/Due/Snoozed labels and server-side totals; snooze validates a future date, optimistic concurrency, full audit event) plus an internal closeAlertsForSource helper. New apps/worker/src/payment-alert-opener.ts -- the first recurring-background-job-driven feature in this codebase, ticking every 15 minutes, reusing the existing outbox/notification pipeline with a new PAYMENT_ALERT_OPENED event type. Opens a CAPEX event's alert once past due or marked achieved (confirmed OR logic) and an OPEX installment's alert once past its reminder-offset window; reopens snoozed alerts past their window with a fresh notification; naturally idempotent via the schema's hard-unique source columns. Resolves ACCOUNT_TEAM to every active user whose effective permissions include payment_alert.snooze (role grants plus per-user overrides, not a hardcoded role) and PROJECT_MANAGER to the assigned employee's linked user email, silently skipping when unassigned/unlinked. MilestonesService (already shipped in 4.2) now injects PaymentAlertsService and deletes any live alert when its source is cancelled or skipped -- the one deliberate exception to this codebase's no-physical-deletion rule, since the row is a reminder for a billing event that will never happen. New payment_alert.read/snooze permissions -- Admin/Accountant get both, Project Manager/Admin Staff/CEO get read only.
Frontend: new PaymentAlertTab on the existing project detail page -- four server-computed summary tiles, Open Alerts/Snoozed sections, a Snooze modal (date + optional note), a disabled Raise Invoice button with a Phase-5-deferral tooltip (shown, not hidden, since every viewer should understand why it's inert), Snooze itself hidden for an actor lacking payment_alert.snooze.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260809080000_phase4_03_payment_alerts (new, hand-written for the two CHECK constraints), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (two permissions), apps/api/src/payment-alerts/{payment-alerts.service.ts,payment-alerts.module.ts} (new), apps/api/src/milestones/{milestones.service.ts,milestones.module.ts} (closeAlertsForSource integration), packages/contracts/src/{ports/payment-alert.port.ts (new), ports/index.ts, index.ts, context.ts, router.ts, routers/payment-alerts.ts (new)}, apps/api/src/{app.module.ts,main.ts}, apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (permission count fix), apps/worker/src/{payment-alert-opener.ts (new),outbox-dispatcher.ts,main.ts}, apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/{payment-alert-tab.tsx (new),page.tsx}, docs/12 (ADR-064).
Migrations: phase4_03_payment_alerts -- generated via a plain prisma migrate dev first, then hand-edited to append the two CHECK constraints; the recurring, already-understood phase3_02_leads checksum drift reappeared and was diagnosed (not assumed) before falling back to the established psql + prisma migrate resolve --applied procedure.
Tests: 6 new payment-alerts.integration-spec.ts tests (derived-field/totals correctness, future-date-required snooze validation, full snooze with audit values, stale-version rejection, cross-org rejection, Project-Manager project-scoping), 4 new cross-cutting tests appended to milestones.integration-spec.ts (cancel/skip each delete their live alert; cancelling a source with no alert yet is a no-op), and 11 new payment-alert-opener.integration-spec.ts tests in apps/worker (OPEX/CAPEX-due opens, CAPEX-achieved-early opens, CANCELLED/SKIPPED never open, idempotent re-run, snooze-expiry reopen with exactly one fresh notification, ACCOUNT_TEAM/PROJECT_MANAGER/BOTH recipient resolution including silently-skipped-unassigned-PM) plus one new outbox-dispatcher.integration-spec.ts assertion for the PAYMENT_ALERT_OPENED render case. 180 integration tests pass in apps/api (170 prior + 10 new) and 15 in apps/worker (4 prior + 11 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all workspaces, forced fresh. roles.integration-spec.ts catalog count updated 50 -> 52. A genuinely fresh database replayed the full 24-migration history end-to-end via prisma migrate deploy with zero errors. apps/api/apps/worker production builds are clean; apps/web's production build hit the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js prerender bug already documented in ADR-043/052/053/063 (confirmed non-regression via a git-stash comparison against the unmodified baseline commit showing the identical failure on /masters/cities) -- not a regression from this slice.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-064 (unified PaymentAlert model with CHECK-enforced source-type/FK matching, no stored amount/due-date copy, OPEN|SNOOZED-only status, confirmed CAPEX OR-trigger and Account Team resolution, the first background-job-driven feature reusing the existing outbox pipeline, physical alert deletion on cancel/skip as the sole no-physical-deletion exception, revised Accountant/PM permission split, disabled Raise Invoice placeholder).
Known issues: UI-specific requirements (Snooze modal, disabled Raise Invoice tooltip, role-based control visibility, summary-tile/badge rendering, responsive layout, keyboard/focus behavior, zero console errors) could only be verified by static code review this session, not a live browser pass -- this slice's status stays "UI verification pending" (not "complete") until a real browser click-through confirms all five items in the Status line above. apps/web's production build remains affected by the same pre-existing, disclosed upstream Next.js bug as every prior slice since ADR-043. Slice 4.2's own UI verification also remains outstanding from the prior entry.
Next task: a browser pass on both this slice's and Slice 4.2's UI (see Status lines) before either can be marked fully done; then remaining Phase 4 slices (journal entries, posting engine, idempotency registry, accounting period lock enforcement, reversal mechanism, EMD/SD) each need their own plan-mode pass before implementation, per CLAUDE.md's one-slice-at-a-time rule.

## Phase 4, Slice 4.4: Journal Entries, Posting Engine, Idempotency Registry, Period-Lock Enforcement -- implementation complete, UI verification pending

```text
Date: 2026-08-10
Status: Implementation complete, UI verification pending -- backend fully implemented, tested (16 new integration tests), and live-verified via a direct service-level script against real production data; the frontend has not yet had a real browser pass, and the HTTP/tRPC router layer specifically was not re-verified live this pass (see Known issues). Do not mark this slice's UI as done until a browser pass confirms: (1) the Journal Entries list/filter/drawer workflow; (2) the Ledger drawer's running-balance rendering and date-range filter; (3) responsive layout; (4) keyboard/focus behaviour; (5) zero console errors.
Phase: Phase 4 -- Milestones and accounting core (Slice 4.4 of many; Phase 4.2/4.3 UI verification still pending from prior slices)
Task: Journal Entries, Posting Engine, Idempotency Registry, and Period-Lock Enforcement, bundled per the user's own direction since a posting engine without journal models, period-lock enforcement, or duplicate-posting protection isn't a posting engine at all. docs/04 line 565 names the target screen ("Chart of Accounts and Ledger Viewer", NEW_REQUIRED) -- Slice 4.1 built only the Chart-of-Accounts half; this slice completes the Ledger Viewer half. docs/06-accounting-posting-rules.md is the authoritative, already-frozen spec implemented directly. Nothing in the codebase yet triggers a real posting (EMD/SD, Phase 4's own next deliverable, is the first planned caller) -- this slice is infrastructure, proven by tests and a direct service-level posting against real data. Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-065.
Self-caught correction during implementation: the plan's original design called for a new generic idempotency table. Mid-implementation, a generic IdempotencyKey model was discovered already in the schema (Phase 1 foundation, already used by UsersService.invite) with a materially better design (request-hash conflict detection). The plan's own design was discarded in favor of reusing this existing, proven mechanism.
Schema: new JournalEntry (legal entity, accounting date, a snapshot accountingPeriodId resolved at post time, source type/id, posting event, description, status DRAFT|POSTED|REVERSED with only POSTED reachable this slice, unique on (organizationId, sourceType, sourceId) as a belt-and-braces duplicate guard) and JournalLine (ledger account, debit/credit CHECK-enforced to exactly one being set, plus every dimension docs/06 names -- project/party/employee/gstRegistration/taxCode/narrative -- even though nothing populates most of them yet). Two purely-additive migration changes to already-shipped 4.1 models: LedgerAccount and GstRegistration each gained an (id, organizationId) composite unique so JournalLine's FKs to them are genuine org-consistent composite FKs.
Backend: PostingService.postJournal enforces every docs/06 mandatory posting control in one atomic transaction (zero lines, negative amounts, both-or-neither debit/credit, unbalanced, no matching period, LOCKED period rejected, SOFT_CLOSED still accepted -- confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion, inactive/non-postable ledger account, duplicate source event) before checking-and-recording the idempotency key (reusing the existing IdempotencyKey model, commandType 'journal.post', same request-hash-conflict-detection shape as UsersService.invite) and writing the header, lines, and audit event together. No tRPC procedure exposes postJournal -- it is internal-only, called by other backend services once one exists. Read-only query methods (listEntries paginated/filtered mirroring AuditPort, getEntryDetail, getLedgerActivity with a server-computed running balance signed per the account's own normal balance) are exposed, gated journal.read. New journal.read permission -- Admin/Accountant/CEO.
Frontend: new /journal-entries page (FilterCard + EntityTable, server-paginated, row-click drawer showing line-level drill-down) under a new "Journal Entries" Finance nav item. The existing Chart of Accounts page's Ledger Accounts table gained a "View Ledger" row action opening a drawer with that account's posted lines, opening/closing balance, and a date-range filter -- the one place this slice touches already-shipped 4.1 frontend code.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260810090000_phase4_04_journal_posting_engine (new, hand-written for the two CHECK constraints), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (one permission), apps/api/src/accounting/{posting.service.ts (new),accounting.module.ts,main.ts}, packages/contracts/src/{ports/journal.port.ts (new), ports/index.ts, index.ts, context.ts, router.ts, routers/journal.ts (new)}, apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (permission count fix), apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{journal-entries/page.tsx (new),chart-of-accounts/page.tsx}, packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx,nav-items.ts}, docs/12 (ADR-065).
Migrations: phase4_04_journal_posting_engine -- generated via a plain prisma migrate dev first, then hand-edited to append the two CHECK constraints; the recurring, already-understood phase3_02_leads checksum drift reappeared and was diagnosed (not assumed) before falling back to the established psql + prisma migrate resolve --applied procedure.
Tests: 16 new posting.integration-spec.ts tests (balanced posting succeeds and is retrievable with a full audit event; every mandatory-control rejection individually; SOFT_CLOSED accepts a posting while LOCKED rejects one; idempotent-repeat-returns-original and same-key-different-request throws IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT; duplicate-source-event rejection; cross-org isolation; getLedgerActivity running-balance arithmetic across a sequence of debits and credits). 196 integration tests pass in apps/api (180 prior + 16 new), full lint/typecheck/test/test:integration green across all workspaces, forced fresh. roles.integration-spec.ts catalog count updated 52 -> 53. A genuinely fresh database replayed the full 25-migration history end-to-end via prisma migrate deploy with zero errors. apps/api/apps/worker production builds are clean; apps/web's production build hit the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js prerender bug already documented in ADR-043/052/053/063/064 (confirmed non-regression via a git-stash comparison against the unmodified baseline commit showing the identical failure on /masters/cities) -- not a regression from this slice.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-065 (journal/posting-engine structure implementing docs/06 verbatim, idempotency-registry reuse correction, confirmed SOFT_CLOSED-still-posts rule, internal-only postJournal with no user-facing posting action, revised live-verification approach avoiding the user's active dev processes).
Known issues: UI-specific requirements (list/filter/drawer workflow, running-balance rendering, responsive layout, keyboard/focus behavior, zero console errors) could only be verified by static code review this session, not a live browser pass. Additionally, unlike every prior slice, the HTTP/tRPC router layer was not independently re-verified live via curl this pass: the user's own long-running dev processes (a tmux-managed session, not this session's PM2 instances) were found actively serving stale pre-slice code -- two prior slices this week mistakenly killed this same session's processes believing them abandoned, a real mistake already disclosed to the user, and this pass deliberately avoided repeating it rather than restarting those processes without being asked. Live verification instead used a direct service-level script that posted a real balanced entry against the actual production legal entity/financial-year/ledger-accounts already in the dev database, confirmed correct retrieval, and cleaned up precisely (scoped by exact IDs, never an unscoped DELETE). A period-lock test against a real production period was deliberately not attempted live (too risky against data the user depends on) -- covered instead by 2 of the 16 integration tests against disposable, isolated fixtures. An HTTP-level smoke test of the three query procedures remains outstanding until the user's API process is next restarted with this code. apps/web's production build remains affected by the same pre-existing, disclosed upstream Next.js bug as every prior slice since ADR-043. Slices 4.2 and 4.3's own UI verification also remain outstanding from prior entries.
Next task: restart the user's api process to pick up this slice's code, then a browser pass on this slice's and Slices 4.2/4.3's UI (see Status lines) before any can be marked fully done; then the remaining Phase 4 slices (reversal mechanism, EMD/SD) each need their own plan-mode pass before implementation, per CLAUDE.md's one-slice-at-a-time rule.

## Phase 4, Slice 4.5: EMD / SD (Security Instruments) -- implementation complete, UI verification pending

```text
Date: 2026-08-11
Status: Implementation complete, UI verification pending -- backend fully implemented and tested (15 new integration tests against the real dev database); the frontend has not yet had a real browser pass. Do not mark this slice's UI as done until a browser pass confirms: (1) the lodge-form workflow, including the funding-entity reveal and its Legal-Entity-or-Party mutual exclusivity, and the purpose/type-driven conditional bank-account field; (2) the list page's filters/tiles/drawer; (3) the Record Return modal, including the returned-to defaulting from funding info; (4) the project tab's embedding and pre-fill behavior; (5) responsive layout, keyboard/focus behaviour, and zero console errors.
Phase: Phase 4 -- Milestones and accounting core (Slice 4.5 of many; Phase 4.2/4.3/4.4 UI verification still pending from prior slices)
Task: EMD/SD (Security Instruments) -- deferred to Phase 4 by ADR-056 pending the Chart-of-Accounts/journal/posting engine, now satisfied by Slice 4.4. This slice gives PostingService.postJournal (built call-less in 4.4) its first real caller. Three HTML prototypes (emdsd-all.html, emdsd-add.html, project-emdsd.html) plus docs/04's exact permission-key names and docs/06's short, authoritative EMD/SD posting rules (cash lodged/returned Dr/Cr Recoverable-Bank, BG principal contingent) were the primary references. Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-066.
Mid-planning, the user asked a direct follow-up question not anticipated by the plan as drafted: what happens when an EMD/SD is funded by a different entity, and how does the eventual return route back to them? This reshaped the "Funded by Primary Company?" field from the prototype's plain yes/no toggle into a structured, entity-linked capture on both the lodge and return sides -- confirmed via AskUserQuestion (structured capture, informational only, no new accounting treatment invented).
Schema: new SecurityInstrument model (immutable after creation except through recordReturn) plus SecurityInstrumentCategory/SecurityInstrumentStatus enums. SecurityInstrumentPurpose gained a nullable category (EMD/SD) and a per-purpose default recoverable ledger account; SecurityInstrumentType gained requiresCashPosting plus a new cash row. A real field-coverage bug was caught and fixed before the migration was finalized: category was originally planned NOT NULL, but the already-shipped generic Masters catalogue screen can create new security_instrument_purposes rows through its own generic create()/update() path, which never sets a category -- would have crashed on the next admin-created purpose. Fixed by making the column nullable, excluding null-category purposes from the EMD/SD tile split rather than the tile split forcing every future purpose through this slice's own assumptions. A second issue was caught before any build ran: a schema doc comment containing a literal `*/` prematurely closed Prisma's generated JSDoc block and corrupted the entire generated .d.ts file -- root-caused to the comment text itself, not a tooling bug, and fixed by rewording it.
Backend: SecurityInstrumentsService.lodge() -- wrapped in the same generic IdempotencyKey registry postJournal already uses, full request-hash conflict detection. For cash-equivalent types, resolves the purpose's recoverable ledger account (rejects if unmapped) and calls postJournal (Dr Recoverable / Cr Bank); for guarantee types, no journal call at all -- principal tracked only as the instrument row (contingent exposure, docs/06). Not a single shared database transaction spanning both postJournal's own commit and the instrument row's commit (postJournal itself was left untouched, zero risk to 4.4's tests) -- the journal-level idempotency key is deterministically derived from the lodge's own key so a retry converges on exactly one journal entry rather than an orphaned duplicate, a disclosed, accepted tradeoff. recordReturn() mirrors the same pattern and only succeeds when the received amount equals the lodged amount exactly -- penalty-deducted (partial) returns are deferred, confirmed with the user, not silently accepted or partially posted. Four new permissions (security_instrument.read/create/post/export) mirroring the live milestone.*/payment_alert.* grant pattern exactly (confirmed against the real seed.ts role arrays): Accountant/Admin get all four, CEO gets read via the .read-suffix filter, Project Manager/Admin Staff get read only.
Frontend: new /emd-sd list page (five server-computed summary tiles, FilterCard + EntityTable, a lodge Drawer, a Record Return modal) under a new "EMD & SD" Finance nav item; a new "EMD / SD" project tab reusing the same shared Drawer/Modal/Tiles components via a shared apps/web/src/app/(platform)/emd-sd/shared.tsx module (mirroring the leads/shared.tsx precedent). @zitaware/ui and @zitaware/contracts were rebuilt explicitly before typechecking apps/web -- the exact gap that bit twice in 4.4 and was not repeated here.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260811100000_phase4_05_security_instruments (new, hand-written -- nullable category with a backfill-and-safety-net, not NOT NULL), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (cash type row, requiresCashPosting/category/ledger-account-mapping data, new SECURITY_DEPOSIT_RECOVERABLE ledger account, SECURITY_INSTRUMENT entity type, four permissions, role grants), apps/api/src/security-instruments/{security-instruments.service.ts,security-instruments.module.ts,security-instruments.integration-spec.ts} (new), packages/contracts/src/{ports/security-instrument.port.ts (new), ports/index.ts, index.ts, context.ts, router.ts, routers/security-instruments.ts (new)}, apps/api/src/{app.module.ts,main.ts}, apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (permission count fix), apps/web/src/app/(platform)/emd-sd/{page.tsx,shared.tsx} (new), apps/web/src/app/(platform)/projects/[id]/{security-instrument-tab.tsx (new),page.tsx}, packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx,nav-items.ts}, docs/11 (ADR-053-to-ADR-056 citation correction), docs/12 (ADR-066).
Migrations: phase4_05_security_instruments -- generated via prisma migrate diff against the live database (prisma migrate dev hit the recurring, already-understood phase3_02_leads checksum drift), hand-written with a nullable category column backfilled per seeded purpose code plus a safety-net default, then applied via psql + prisma migrate resolve --applied per the established procedure. A follow-up ALTER TABLE dropped a NOT NULL constraint applied in the first pass, once the Masters-catalogue field-coverage gap (see Schema above) was caught -- applied live and folded back into the migration file so it reflects the final, correct state.
Tests: 15 new security-instruments.integration-spec.ts tests (cash-equivalent lodging posts the correct Dr/Cr journal and is retrievable; guarantee-type lodging posts no journal; neither-project-nor-tender rejection; tender-only lodging accepted; unmapped-purpose rejection; missing-bank-account rejection; funding-entity mutual-exclusivity rejection; idempotent-repeat-returns-original and same-key-different-request throws IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT; full return posts the mirror journal and transitions to RETURNED with a full audit trail; mismatched-amount return rejection; already-returned rejection; guarantee-type return with no posting; cross-org isolation; tile-arithmetic sanity) -- all passing against the real dev database. roles.integration-spec.ts catalog count updated 53 -> 57. @zitaware/database/@zitaware/contracts/@zitaware/ui all rebuild cleanly; apps/api/apps/web both typecheck and lint cleanly.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-066 (EMD/SD structure implementing docs/06 verbatim, the nullable-category field-coverage correction, the user-reshaped funding-entity capture, sequential-not-shared-transaction posting with deterministic-derived-key retry safety, revised permission grants mirroring milestone.*/payment_alert.*).
Known issues: UI-specific requirements (lodge-form workflow, funding-entity reveal, list filters/tiles/drawer, Record Return modal, project-tab embedding, responsive layout, keyboard/focus behavior, zero console errors) could only be verified by static code review this session, not a live browser pass -- no browser-automation tool was available. Live HTTP/tRPC verification against the user's own running dev processes was not performed this pass either, following the same standing lesson as 4.4 (the user's tmux-managed dev session must not be touched without asking) -- correctness rests on the same requirePermission(key).query/mutation() shape already proven live in every prior Phase 4 slice, plus this slice's own full Services-interface type-conformance check passing. apps/web's production build was not re-verified this pass; it remains subject to the same pre-existing, disclosed, intermittent upstream Next.js prerender bug documented since ADR-043. Slices 4.2, 4.3, and 4.4's own UI verification also remain outstanding from prior entries.
Next task: restart the user's api/web processes to pick up this slice's code, then a browser pass on this slice's and Slices 4.2/4.3/4.4's UI (see Status lines) before any can be marked fully done; then the remaining Phase 4 slice (reversal mechanism) needs its own plan-mode pass before implementation, per CLAUDE.md's one-slice-at-a-time rule.

## Phase 4, Slice 4.6: Reversal Mechanism -- implementation complete, UI verification pending (final Phase 4 slice)

```text
Date: 2026-08-12
Status: Implementation complete, UI verification pending -- backend fully implemented and tested (9 new integration tests, 25 total in posting.integration-spec.ts, all passing against the real dev database); the frontend has not yet had a real browser pass. Do not mark this slice's UI as done until a browser pass confirms: (1) the Reverse action's visibility rules (POSTED-only, permission-gated) and modal workflow; (2) the detail drawer's bidirectional reversal-link rendering; (3) responsive layout; (4) keyboard/focus behaviour; (5) zero console errors.
Phase: Phase 4 -- Milestones and accounting core (Slice 4.6 of six -- the final slice). Phase 4.2/4.3/4.4/4.5 UI verification still pending from prior slices.
Task: Reversal Mechanism -- deferred by ADR-065 pending its own plan-mode pass, and the last remaining item in docs/11's Phase 4 list once EMD/SD (4.5) shipped. Makes the already-reserved JournalEntryStatus.REVERSED value reachable for the first time. docs/06's core principles ("Posted journal entries are immutable." / "Reversal entries reference the original entry." / "Create an opposite journal. Do not delete the receipt or original journal.") plus docs/01/04's "Correction actions must request a reason" and docs/09's "reverse" common-action vocabulary were the primary references -- no HTML prototype or screen contract exists for a reversal UI anywhere. Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-067.
Confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion before implementation: no maker-checker restriction -- any Accountant/Admin holding journal.reverse may reverse any posted entry, including their own, since this is the first slice where a human directly triggers a posting-adjacent action at all (postJournal itself has no UI).
Schema: JournalEntry gains four nullable columns (reversalOfJournalEntryId @unique single-column self-FK, reversalReason, reversedBy, reversedAt) and a JournalEntryReversal self-relation -- reversalOfJournalEntryId is set only on the new reversal entry; the original is found via Prisma's automatic back-relation (reversedByEntry), not a second physical column. No JournalLine schema change. No new version column -- the POSTED->REVERSED transition is inherently one-time-terminal, protected by an updateMany(where: status='POSTED') zero-count check instead.
Backend: PostingService.resolveAccountingPeriod extracted as a private helper, shared identically by postJournal and reverseJournal (postJournal's own public behavior and all its existing tests unaffected). reverseJournal: idempotency check (commandType 'journal.reverse') -> load original+lines -> reject if not POSTED -> reject empty/whitespace reason -> resolve the accounting period for the reversal's OWN supplied date (not the original's, which may since be LOCKED -- expected, you reverse into a fresh open period) -> build swapped lines (every original line's debit/credit exchanged, same ledger account/dimensions/narrative) -> one $transaction creating the new opposite-signed entry (sourceType 'journal_entry_reversal', sourceId = original's id, doubling the existing uq_journal_entries_org_source constraint as "at most one reversal per entry" for free) + flipping the original to REVERSED + two audit events + the idempotency-key row. Ledger-account active/allowManualPosting validation is deliberately NOT re-run for reversal lines -- the accounts were already valid when the original posted, and reversing a legitimate entry must remain possible even if an account was deactivated since. Reversing a reversal is allowed, not specially blocked. One new permission, journal.reverse (Accountant + Admin only, matching docs/09's "Manual journal posting is restricted to Accountant/Admin").
Frontend: the existing /journal-entries page gains a "Reverse" row action (visible only when status is POSTED and the actor holds journal.reverse) opening a ReverseJournalModal (required reason, accounting date defaulting to today); the entry-detail drawer gains bidirectional reversal-link rendering (a reversed original shows its reversal reason/date and a button to view the reversal entry; a reversal entry shows a button to view the original) via a shared onNavigateToEntry callback that swaps which entry the same drawer displays. StatusTag gets an explicit tone="neg" override for REVERSED. No new route.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260812110000_phase4_06_reversal_mechanism (new, purely additive, generated via prisma migrate diff after prisma migrate dev hit the recurring phase3_02_leads drift), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (one permission, journal.reverse, added to PERMISSION_KEYS and ACCOUNTANT_PERMISSIONS), apps/api/src/accounting/posting.service.ts (resolveAccountingPeriod extraction + reverseJournal), apps/api/src/accounting/posting.integration-spec.ts (9 new tests), packages/contracts/src/{ports/journal.port.ts, routers/journal.ts}, apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (permission count fix), apps/web/src/app/(platform)/journal-entries/page.tsx (Reverse action, ReverseJournalModal, drawer linkage), docs/11 (4.6 entry, Phase 4 marked fully implementation-complete), docs/12 (ADR-067).
Migrations: phase4_06_reversal_mechanism -- generated via prisma migrate diff against the live database (prisma migrate dev hit the recurring, already-understood phase3_02_leads checksum drift, plus a second expected drift on the already-hand-edited phase4_05_security_instruments migration file from the prior slice's own field-coverage fix), applied via psql + prisma migrate resolve --applied per the established procedure. Purely additive, no backfill needed (all four new columns nullable).
Tests: 9 new tests appended to the existing posting.integration-spec.ts (reversing a POSTED entry creates a correctly opposite-signed new entry referencing the original, flips the original to REVERSED with reason/actor/timestamp and a full audit trail on both sides, and confirms every line's debit/credit was exchanged; reversing an already-REVERSED entry is rejected; an empty/whitespace reason is rejected; reversing into a LOCKED period for the reversal's own date is rejected; idempotent-repeat-returns-original and same-key-different-request throws IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT; reversing an entry whose ORIGINAL period is now LOCKED still succeeds, confirming only the reversal's own date's period matters; a post-then-reverse round trip nets getLedgerActivity's closing balance back to its exact pre-posting value; reversing a reversal is itself accepted, creating a second valid reversal entry) -- all 25 tests in the file pass (16 prior unchanged, confirming the resolveAccountingPeriod extraction preserved postJournal's exact behavior, + 9 new). roles.integration-spec.ts catalog count updated 57 -> 58. Full monorepo lint/typecheck/test/test:integration/production-build sweep green, forced fresh, across all workspaces (211 prior apps/api integration tests + 9 new = 220). A genuinely fresh database replayed the full 27-migration history via prisma migrate deploy and reseeded cleanly, confirmed via direct SQL that journal.reverse is granted to exactly Accountant and Admin.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-067 (reversal creates a new opposite-signed entry referencing the original rather than mutating it, the nullable single-column self-relation FK design, the resolveAccountingPeriod extraction and its ledger-account-revalidation asymmetry, the confirmed no-maker-checker decision, the new journal.reverse permission and its Accountant/Admin-only grant, reversal as the first human-triggered posting-adjacent tRPC mutation in the app).
Known issues: UI-specific requirements (Reverse action visibility, modal workflow, bidirectional drawer linkage, responsive layout, keyboard/focus behavior, zero console errors) could only be verified by static code review this session, not a live browser pass -- no browser-automation tool was available. Live HTTP/tRPC verification against the user's own running dev processes was not performed this pass, following the same standing lesson as 4.4/4.5 (the user's tmux-managed dev session must not be touched without asking) -- correctness rests on the same requirePermission(key).query/mutation() shape already proven live in every prior Phase 4 slice, plus this slice's own full Services-interface type-conformance check passing. apps/web's production build was re-verified this pass and succeeded cleanly (the /masters/cities upstream Next.js bug did not reproduce this run -- it remains intermittent, not fixed). Slices 4.2, 4.3, 4.4, and 4.5's own UI verification also remain outstanding from prior entries.
Next task: a real browser pass across all of 4.2/4.3/4.4/4.5/4.6's UI is the only remaining work before Phase 4 as a whole can be called fully complete -- no further Phase 4 slices remain to plan. Phase 5 (Finance income vertical slice) is the next phase-level body of work whenever the user is ready to start it, and would need its own fresh plan-mode pass as a new, separate initiative rather than a continuation of Phase 4's slice sequence.
```
