## Phase 6, Slice 6.5: Vendor Payment (the final step that actually moves money)

```text
Date: 2026-08-23
Status: Implementation complete, UI verification pending -- backend fully built and tested (16 new tests in vendor-payments.integration-spec.ts, including 2 proving PaymentRequestsService.revoke()'s own activated check, all 16 passing on a clean first run), typecheck/lint clean across all 9 workspaces (23/23 tasks), production build clean for every workspace except apps/web (see below), a fresh-database migration replay confirmed the new migration applies cleanly from zero, and the full apps/api integration suite passed clean (29/29 files, 363/363 tests); the frontend has not yet had a real browser pass. apps/web's own production build hit the pre-existing, previously-disclosed non-deterministic upstream Next.js /_global-error//_not-found prerender crash (ADR-043, corrected by ADR-052/053) on 5 consecutive attempts this session -- web's own typecheck/lint and every compile/TypeScript step inside that same build passed clean every time, including all new vendor-payments routes, confirming this is not a regression from this slice. Do not mark this slice's UI as done until a browser pass confirms: (1) the New Vendor Payment page's ?paymentRequestId= seed, eligible-lines checklist, per-line Cash Paid + Deductions editor, and live Dr/Cr preview all behave correctly; (2) the Vendor Payment detail page's per-allocation deduction table and Reverse action show/hide correctly per permission and status; (3) the Payment Request detail page's new "Record Payment..." action shows only while APPROVED and a balance remains, and its "Vendor Payments" card lists payments correctly.
Phase: Phase 6 -- Procurement and expense (Slice 6.5 -- the final step of the procurement chain, renumbered from 6.4 once that slot was used by the Purchase Request/Vendor Bill merge).
Task: User asked to start Vendor Payment directly (AskUserQuestion, "Start Vendor Payment (Slice 6.5)"). Research confirmed ClientReceipt/ReceiptAllocation is a clean direct mirror with the debit/credit sides flipped and PaymentRequestLine standing in for Invoice. Three scope questions were confirmed via AskUserQuestion before planning: deduction typing limited to TDS/CGST_TDS/SGST_TDS/IGST_TDS only (no Hold/Retention/Penalty-on-payment); TDS as a free-entered amount with a display-only rate % convenience (no TDS section/rate master); batching AND partial payment both allowed (one payment may settle several APPROVED lines across separate Payment Requests sharing one vendor+legal entity, and one line may be paid across multiple payments over time). The plan was approved with no revisions.
Backend: New VendorPayment/VendorPaymentAllocation/VendorPaymentDeduction models mirror ClientReceipt/ReceiptAllocation/AllocationDeduction field-for-field, narrowed to the TDS/GST-TDS deduction subset, with no vendorId column on the header (implied by the allocations) and no maker-checker of its own (the authorization gate already happened at Payment Request's own approve()). New "available to pay" formula (line.requestedAmount minus sum(settledAmount) across every POSTED allocation on that line) mirrors Payment Request's own getAvailableForRequest, computed by one shared private helper reused by getEligiblePaymentRequestLines, getPaymentRequestAvailability, and computeVendorPaymentPlan alike. postVendorPayment mirrors postReceipt with debit/credit sides flipped: validates every allocation's line -> parent request is APPROVED, that every allocation shares one vendor and one legal entity matching the paying bank account's own, and that cashPaid+deductions never exceeds a line's live available balance; posts one Dr Accounts Payable -- Vendor line for the batch total (partyId = the shared vendor), one Cr line to the bank account's own ledger account, and one Cr line per distinct TDS/GST-TDS ledger account referenced. reverseVendorPayment mirrors reverseReceipt exactly. PaymentRequestsService.revoke() activates its own forward-flagged check from ADR-078/6.3: a direct VendorPaymentAllocation existence query added after the existing status/access checks, rejecting revoke once any payment already references one of the request's lines. Three new permissions (vendor_payment.{read,post,reverse}) mirror receipt.{read,post,reverse}'s exact role restriction -- Accountant only for post/reverse, PM/Admin Staff/CEO get read, Employee gets none.
Frontend: New /vendor-payments (list, filters), /vendor-payments/new (requires ?paymentRequestId= seed, resolves vendor+legal entity server-side, an eligible-lines checklist where each checked line expands into its own Cash Paid amount plus a Deductions editor -- Add Deduction, Type/Rate%/Amount/Note per row, rate/amount two-way sync against the line's own vendorBillTaxableAmount -- and a live Dr/Cr Summary panel powered by calculatePreview), /vendor-payments/[id] (payment details, one card per allocation with its own deductions table, a Reverse action gated by vendor_payment.reverse and POSTED status). Payment Request's own [id] detail page: the old "Payment fulfilment... is not yet available" placeholder note is replaced with a live remaining-balance readout plus a "Record Payment..." action (shown only while APPROVED and a balance remains, mirroring Vendor Bill's own "Raise Payment Request..." visibility rule) and a "Vendor Payments" card listing payments recorded against the request so far.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, new migration packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260823090000_phase6_05_vendor_payment, packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (3 permissions, role grants, 4 ledger accounts, one entity type). apps/api/src/vendor-payments/{vendor-payments.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts} (new), apps/api/src/payment-requests/payment-requests.service.ts (revoke() check), apps/api/src/{app.module.ts, main.ts}, apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (catalog count 86 -> 89). packages/contracts/src/{ports/vendor-payment.port.ts, routers/vendor-payments.ts} (new), wired into context.ts/router.ts/ports/index.ts/index.ts. apps/web/src/app/(platform)/vendor-payments/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx} (new), apps/web/src/app/(platform)/payment-requests/[id]/page.tsx (Record Payment action + Vendor Payments card), packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts} (new VendorPaymentIcon and nav item).
Migrations: phase6_05_vendor_payment -- hand-curated from `prisma migrate diff` (the same three unrelated pre-existing constraints/indexes always excluded), purely additive (two enums, three tables, no data migration), applied via prisma migrate deploy + prisma generate.
Tests: 16 new tests in vendor-payments.integration-spec.ts -- plain-TDS and split CGST/SGST/IGST-TDS journal shape; sum(cashPaid) != actualAmount rejected; batching two lines from separate Payment Requests sharing one vendor+legal entity; cross-vendor and cross-legal-entity batch rejection; a non-APPROVED line rejected; a genuine partial-payment sequence (first payment, updated available-to-pay, an over-allocation rejected, the exact remainder paid off cleanly, the line then absent from eligible lines and availability at 0.00); reversal reverses the journal, flips status, restores availability, and leaves allocation/deduction rows untouched; a stale-version reversal rejected; idempotent replay on post; project scoping (NOT_FOUND via getDetail and exclusion from list for an out-of-scope actor, confirmed visible for an in-scope one); cross-org isolation; and 2 tests proving PaymentRequestsService.revoke() now rejects once a VendorPaymentAllocation exists and still succeeds when none does. All 16 passing on a clean first run (no flaky reruns). roles.integration-spec.ts catalog count corrected 86 -> 89, verified against the live seeded database directly before the test itself confirmed it. turbo run typecheck lint clean across all 9 workspaces (23/23 tasks); turbo run build clean for every workspace except apps/web, which hit the pre-existing non-deterministic /_global-error//_not-found prerender bug on 5 consecutive attempts (typecheck/lint/compile all clean regardless -- see Status above and ADR-080's own Known issues). Full apps/api integration suite passed clean on its first run (29/29 files, 363/363 tests). A fresh-database migration replay on a disposable scratch database confirmed the full history -- including this slice's new migration -- applies cleanly from zero, with `prisma migrate diff` against the result showing only the same three pre-existing hand-added constraints every prior migration has excluded.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-080 (the mirror-with-flipped-sides posting design; the shared "available to pay" formula; the activated PaymentRequestsService.revoke() check; the vendor_payment permission matrix mirroring receipt's own). Fulfills the forward-flagged check named in ADR-078's own revoke() doc comment. Does not reopen ADR-075/076/079 (Vendor Bill) or ADR-078 (Payment Request) -- confirmed zero code changes required in either beyond the one revoke() check.
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice this session. apps/web's production build is intermittently red on the pre-existing upstream /_global-error//_not-found bug (ADR-043/052/053) -- not new to this slice, left open per the same standing user decision. This closes docs/01's own canonical procurement chain (Vendor Bill -> Payment Request -> Vendor Payment) end to end. Hold/Retention/Penalty-on-payment and a TDS section/rate master remain explicitly out of scope until a real policy need is confirmed.
Next task: Bank Transaction Import/Reconciliation remains deferred (ADR-073). With the procurement chain now complete end to end, the natural next slice is Direct Expense/Employee Claim/Advance/Statement (the remaining named Phase 6 vertical, no Vendor dependency) or Phase 7 (Inventory and costing) -- whichever the user prioritizes next.
```

## Phase 6, Slice 6.6: Employee Advance & Expense Claim

```text
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Implementation complete, UI verification pending -- backend fully built and tested (17 new tests in employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts, all passing on a clean first run), typecheck/lint clean across all 9 workspaces, production build clean for every workspace including apps/web (the pre-existing non-deterministic /_global-error//_not-found prerender bug, ADR-043/052/053, did not recur this pass), a fresh-database migration replay confirmed the full history -- 42 migrations, including this slice's new one -- applies cleanly from zero, and the full apps/api integration suite passed clean on its first run (30/30 files, 380/380 tests, confirming no regression from this slice's new back-relations on Employee/Project/LegalEntity/PaymentMode/BankAccount/JournalEntry/ExpenseCategory); the frontend has not yet had a real browser pass. Do not mark this slice's UI as done until a browser pass confirms: (1) the Give Advance page's wallet-impact preview and bank-account/payment-mode fields behave correctly; (2) the My Expenses Statement/Pending-Claims tabs render the running control-account balance correctly and the Add Expense form's Save-as-Draft/Submit two-button pattern works; (3) the Employee Reimbursements list's Approve/Reimburse modals and the claim detail page's Approve/Reject/Cancel/Reimburse/Reverse actions show and hide correctly per permission, self-approval state, and claim status.
Phase: Phase 6 -- Procurement and expense (Slice 6.6 -- the remaining named Phase 6 vertical with no Vendor dependency, following Vendor Payment/6.5).
Task: User asked for the next slice; AskUserQuestion offered Direct Expense/Employee Claim (chosen) alongside Subscription/Server cycles and Phase 7. The user then gave an explicit follow-up naming the four reference prototypes to study (expense-employee-statement.html, expense-add.html, expense-employee.html, expense-give-advance.html) before planning. Four scope questions were confirmed via AskUserQuestion before planning: Approve posts the expense-recognition journal immediately and Reimburse posts a separate cash-settlement journal later (a deliberate departure from this phase's usual "approval posts nothing" pattern, since docs/06 names this posting rule explicitly); GST is one flat, user-entered credit amount with no CGST/SGST/IGST split; Advance + Claim + Statement ship together this slice, with Direct Expense explicitly split into its own later slice (the user did not choose the "all four together" recommended option); a claim/advance links to a Project or nothing (company overhead), with no Lead link target this slice. The plan was approved with no revisions. Full rationale in docs/12 ADR-081.
Backend: EMPLOYEE_ADVANCE_RECEIVABLE is a single bidirectional control account per employee -- derived by working through the Employee Statement prototype's own worked numeric example (R. Shah: Rs.10,000 advance, then two claims of Rs.2,400 and Rs.4,000, closing wallet Rs.3,600), confirming issueAdvance and reimburseClaim share one posting shape (Dr control account / Cr Bank) reused via one private helper, while approveClaim posts the opposite side (Dr Expense + flat Input GST / Cr control account) and can legitimately drive the account negative (the company then owes the employee) with no second account needed. EmployeeExpenseClaim's state machine and maker-checker mirror VendorBill's own shape verbatim (DRAFT -> SUBMITTED -> APPROVED/REJECTED, -> CANCELLED creator-only with mandatory reason, self-approval gated by employee_claim.self_approve + a mandatory note), plus a fifth REIMBURSED state. reverseReimbursement moves REIMBURSED back to APPROVED (not a new terminal state), enabling a corrected re-run of reimburseClaim. No project-based scoping anywhere in this module -- a deliberate simplification versus Vendor Bill/Payment Request, since a claim is about the claimant, not a project team; self-service views (getMyEmployeeStatus, getMyWalletStatement, getMyClaims) resolve the acting employee from the session, and getClaimDetail blends admin/self-service access via a hasReadPermission param mirroring this codebase's own canSeeAll pattern. Ownership (not just permission) is checked server-side on updateClaimDraft/submitClaim/cancelClaim, since employee_claim.create is broadly granted to every Employee. Employee.isReimbursable (unused since Phase 2) is activated as the sole eligibility gate for both issueAdvance and createClaimDraft. Nine new permissions extend this codebase's one-permission-per-verb convention (employee_claim.{read,create,approve,self_approve,reimburse,reverse}, employee_advance.{read,create,reverse}) -- Accountant gets all non-self_approve grants, PM/Admin Staff/CEO get read only on both, Employee gets employee_claim.create only.
Frontend: New /employee-expenses (Accountant/Admin list with Approve/Reimburse modals), /employee-expenses/give-advance (Give Advance form with a calculateAdvanceWalletImpact-powered preview panel, per CLAUDE.md's "never calculate accounting totals independently in React"), /employee-expenses/my (self-service Statement/Pending-Claims tabs), /employee-expenses/my/new (Add Expense claim form, Save-as-Draft/Submit two-button pattern), /employee-expenses/claims/[id] (DraftEditor/DecidedView split mirroring Vendor Bill's own detail-page shape, full Approve/Reject/Cancel/Reimburse/Reverse action set with modals and decision history). documents-tab.tsx gains the EMPLOYEE_EXPENSE_CLAIM entity type (EmployeeAdvance gets no documents tab, matching ClientReceipt/VendorPayment's own no-attachment precedent). packages/ui gains a new EmployeeWalletIcon and two nav items ("My Expenses", always visible; "Employee Reimbursements", gated by employee_claim.read) under a new "Employee Expenses" group.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma, new migration packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260824090000_phase6_06_employee_advance_expense_claim, packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (9 permissions, role grants, 2 ledger accounts, 1 entity type). apps/api/src/employee-expenses/{employee-expenses.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts} (new), apps/api/src/{app.module.ts, main.ts}, apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (catalog count 89 -> 98). packages/contracts/src/{ports/employee-expense.port.ts, routers/employee-expenses.ts} (new), wired into context.ts/router.ts/ports/index.ts/index.ts. apps/web/src/app/(platform)/employee-expenses/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, give-advance/page.tsx, my/page.tsx, my/new/page.tsx, claims/[id]/page.tsx} (new), apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx, packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}.
Migrations: phase6_06_employee_advance_expense_claim -- hand-curated from `prisma migrate diff` (the same three unrelated pre-existing constraints/indexes always excluded), purely additive (three enums, three tables, indexes, FKs, no data migration), applied via prisma migrate deploy + prisma generate.
Tests: 17 new tests in employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts -- advance-issue journal shape and its reversal, plus a stale-version-conflict test on reverse; claim create/submit/reject/cancel state transitions, including rejection of a non-reimbursable employee and of editing/submitting someone else's own draft; approve's journal shape (plain claim, and one with a non-zero flat GST credit split into its own line), self-approval permission + mandatory-note gating, idempotent replay; reimburse's journal shape, its reversal back to APPROVED, a successful re-reimbursement after that reversal, and a stale-version-conflict test; the full R.-Shah-style multi-event sequence (advance -> two claims approved) reproducing the exact running control-account balances from the prototype (Rs.10,000 -> Rs.7,600 -> Rs.3,600) via getMyWalletStatement; getClaimDetail scoping (a claim's own employee can view it without employee_claim.read, a different employee cannot, an Accountant with the permission can); cross-org isolation -- all 17 passing on a clean first run. A real bug was caught by this suite, not found separately: reimburseClaim originally reused the claim's own id as the journal's sourceId, which collided with JournalEntry's own uq_journal_entries_org_source constraint on a second reimbursement attempt after a reverseReimbursement (approveClaim safely reuses claim.id since a claim is approved at most once ever) -- fixed by generating a fresh id per reimbursement attempt. A separate lint-only defect (an unused gujaratStateId variable left over from copy-paste in the spec's beforeAll fixture) was caught by the full-workspace lint sweep and removed. turbo run typecheck lint clean across all 9 workspaces (23/23 tasks); turbo run build clean across all 9 workspaces including apps/web, whose own previously-disclosed non-deterministic /_global-error//_not-found prerender bug (ADR-043/052/053) did not recur this pass. A fresh-database migration replay on a disposable scratch database (zitaware_crm_scratch_verify, dropped after use) confirmed the full migration history -- 42 migrations, including this slice's new one -- applies cleanly from zero, with `prisma migrate diff` against the result showing only the same three pre-existing hand-added constraints every prior migration has excluded. The full apps/api integration suite passed clean on its first, unmodified run: 30/30 files, 380/380 tests, confirming no regression from this slice's new back-relations on Employee/Project/LegalEntity/PaymentMode/BankAccount/JournalEntry/ExpenseCategory.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-081 (the single-bidirectional-control-account accounting model derivation; the approve-posts/reimburse-settles departure from this phase's usual pattern; the no-project-scoping simplification and the ownership-vs-permission security boundary for self-service mutation endpoints; the flat-GST-credit design; the nine-permission matrix; the reimburseClaim sourceId bug found and fixed).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session), the same disclosed gap as every other slice this session. Direct Expense, a Lead link target, pre-reimbursement (APPROVED-but-not-yet-paid) reversal, partial/batched reimbursement, a sub-category master, and a computed CGST/SGST/IGST split are all explicitly deferred, not silently dropped.
Next task: Direct Expense (the deferred fifth prototype branch, "Direct Company Expense" against expense-add.html) is the natural immediate follow-up, since it shares this slice's expense-category/ledger-account plumbing but has zero dependency on the employee-wallet concept just built. Subscription/Server cycles and Phase 7 (Inventory and costing) remain the other two named options once Direct Expense ships.
```

## Phase 6, Slice 6.6 follow-up: raise an expense claim on behalf of a no-login employee

```text
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Implementation complete AND fully verified — 2 new tests in employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts (19/19 in the file), typecheck/lint clean across all 9 workspaces, build clean across all 9 workspaces including apps/web, and the full apps/api integration suite passed clean on a re-run (30/30 files, 382/382 tests) after an initial run's one unrelated failure was confirmed as the pre-existing cross-file GSTIN test-contention flake (ADR-076), not a regression from this change. No live browser pass performed yet, same disclosed gap as every prior slice.
Phase: Phase 6, Slice 6.6 follow-up (ADR-082) — a real gap surfaced immediately after 6.6 shipped, not a new numbered slice.
Task: After resolving an unrelated support question (a user's own login needed to be linked to a newly-added Employee record via the Users screen's existing "Link Employee" flow, not a bug), the user asked the real underlying question: how to add an expense for an employee who does not need a CRM login at all. Investigation confirmed createClaimDraft (ADR-081) was self-service only by design, always resolving the claiming employee from the acting user's own account, with no admin-side path — unlike Give Advance, which already lets an Accountant target any employee directly. Confirmed via AskUserQuestion that the permission gate for this new "create on behalf of" capability should reuse the existing employee_claim.approve permission rather than introduce a new one.
Backend: createClaimDraft gains an optional employeeId input; when supplied, the actor must hold employee_claim.approve or the call is rejected FORBIDDEN, and the target employee is validated via the existing private assertEmployeeReimbursable helper (already used by issueAdvance) rather than duplicating its checks. employeeId is included in the idempotency requestHash so a replayed key targeting a different employee correctly conflicts. updateClaimDraft/submitClaim/cancelClaim each gain a hasApprovePermission input (computed the same way approveClaim's own hasSelfApprovePermission already is) and skip their ownership check entirely when true, letting an Accountant manage any claim including one raised on someone else's behalf. No change was needed to the claim detail page's existing isOwnClaim/canCancel gating, since it keys off claim.createdBy (the actor), which is already correct for an Accountant-created on-behalf-of claim.
Frontend: New /employee-expenses/new page mirrors my/new/page.tsx's form with one addition — an Employee picker at the top (reimbursable employees only, same pattern as Give Advance) — gated client-side on employee_claim.approve (the server enforces the real boundary). An "Add Expense" button was added next to "Give Advance" on the admin list page, both gated the same way. This screen has no prototype counterpart (expense-add.html never anticipated an admin acting on someone else's behalf) and is treated as NEW_REQUIRED per docs/16.
Files changed: packages/contracts/src/ports/employee-expense.port.ts, packages/contracts/src/routers/employee-expenses.ts (new withClaimApprovePermission helper), apps/api/src/employee-expenses/{employee-expenses.service.ts, .integration-spec.ts} (new noLoginEmployeeId fixture + 2 tests), apps/web/src/app/(platform)/employee-expenses/{new/page.tsx (new), page.tsx}.
Migrations: none — no schema change, permission-reuse only.
Tests: 2 new tests in employee-expenses.integration-spec.ts — an Accountant creates, edits, submits, and cancels a claim on behalf of a no-login employee end to end; a plain reimbursable employee (holding only employee_claim.create) is rejected FORBIDDEN both creating a claim for someone else and editing/submitting a claim the Accountant created on someone else's behalf. 19/19 tests passing in the file. turbo run typecheck lint clean across all 9 workspaces; turbo run build clean across all 9 workspaces including apps/web (the new /employee-expenses/new route registered). Full apps/api integration suite: one unrelated failure on the first run (vendor-bills.integration-spec.ts, a GSTIN uniqueness collision under parallel execution — the same pre-existing cross-file contention class from ADR-076); re-running that file alone passed 38/38, and re-running the full suite passed clean at 30/30 files, 382/382 tests, confirming this change touches nothing related to GstRegistration or vendor-bill fixtures.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-082 (reusing employee_claim.approve rather than a new permission; the ownership-check bypass shape; the employeeId-in-requestHash idempotency fix; the NEW_REQUIRED /employee-expenses/new screen with no prototype counterpart).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet. The pre-existing cross-file GSTIN-literal test contention (ADR-076) remains an unresolved latent full-suite flake risk under parallel execution — disclosed again since it surfaced during this change's own verification, not caused by it.
Next task: Direct Expense remains the next named Phase 6 vertical, unchanged from the prior entry's own Next task.
```

## Cross-cutting: show uploader and full date/time on every Documents tab

```text
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Implementation complete AND fully verified — 1 new test in documents.integration-spec.ts (13/13 in the file), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 9 workspaces, full apps/api integration suite passed clean on a first run (30/30 files, 383/383 tests). No live browser pass performed yet, same disclosed gap as every prior slice.
Phase: Cross-cutting (not phase-numbered) — a direct user request against the shared DocumentsTab component used by every module that attaches documents.
Task: The user asked that every uploaded document show who uploaded it and the date with time, not just a bare date. DocumentSummary.uploadedBy already existed but only held a raw user id, never a resolvable name; the list row showed formatDate(link.createdAt) — date only, and technically the link's own timestamp rather than the document's own uploadedAt.
Backend: DocumentSummary gains uploadedByName: string | null, resolved only in listLinksForEntity via one batched User.findMany keyed on the distinct uploadedBy ids on the page (not a per-row query), falling back to null for a missing/deleted uploader. initiateUpload/confirmUpload pass null (their own returned DocumentSummary is never rendered — the frontend re-fetches listLinksForEntity right after linking). No schema change — a raw-id column with an application-level join, not a new FK/relation, judged sufficient for this read-only display need.
Frontend: New shared formatDateTime helper in apps/web/src/lib/format.ts (en-IN, day/month/year + hour:minute). documents-tab.tsx's per-document row now reads "Uploaded by {name} · {date, time}", sourced from document.uploadedAt (falling back to link.createdAt only if somehow null) instead of the old link.createdAt-only display. Since DocumentsTab is one shared component, this applies identically everywhere it's embedded (Lead, Tender, Project, Invoice, Receipt, Security Instrument, Vendor Bill, Payment Request, Employee Expense Claim) with no per-module changes.
Files changed: packages/contracts/src/ports/document.port.ts, apps/api/src/documents/{documents.service.ts, documents.integration-spec.ts}, apps/web/src/lib/format.ts, apps/web/src/components/documents-tab.tsx.

Full apps/api integration suite passed clean on a first run (30/30 files, 383/383 tests).

## Phase 6, Slice 6.7: Direct Expense (the other branch of expense-add.html)

```text
Date: 2026-08-24
Status: Implementation complete AND fully verified — 11 new tests in direct-expenses.integration-spec.ts (11/11 in the file), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 9 workspaces, a fresh-database migration replay confirmed the full history (43 migrations) applies cleanly from zero, and the full apps/api integration suite passed clean on its first run (31/31 files, 401/401 tests). No live browser pass performed yet, same disclosed gap as every prior slice.
Phase: Phase 6 — Procurement and expense (Slice 6.7 — the last named Phase 6 vertical besides Subscription/Server cycles).
Task: The user asked what the next task was per the plan; the two remaining Phase 6 deliverables (Direct Expense, deferred from 6.6; Subscription/Server cycles, not started) were presented via AskUserQuestion and Direct Expense was chosen. Two scope forks were confirmed via AskUserQuestion before planning: no maker-checker (a single direct_expense.post holder creates and posts, matching docs/04's own create/post command pair and docs/06's "paid immediately" language — a genuine first for a slice moving real cash with no upstream approval gate at all); no Lead as a link target (Project or company overhead only, mirroring ADR-081's own Employee Expense Claim simplification). The plan was approved with no revisions.
Backend: New DirectExpense model (DRAFT/POSTED/CANCELLED/REVERSED) combines EmployeeExpenseClaim's own DRAFT/CANCELLED-with-creator-only-ownership shape with EmployeeAdvance's own paymentModeId/bankAccountId/journalEntryId/reversal shape — no decision table, since there's no maker-checker. post() is the sole authoritative action and is deliberately not creator-restricted (any direct_expense.post holder may post any colleague's draft); updateDraft/cancelDraft are creator-only, mirroring VendorBillsService's own DRAFT-stage scoping. The posting journal (Dr expense category account + Dr Input GST Receivable [flat, if any] / Cr bank account) reuses the same INPUT_GST_RECEIVABLE_EXPENSE account ADR-081 already created for Employee Expense Claim, rather than seeding a duplicate. No project-scoping anywhere in this module, same precedent as Employee Expense Claim. Four new permissions (direct_expense.{read,create,post,reverse}) — Accountant gets all four, PM/Admin Staff/CEO get read only, Employee gets none (a stricter boundary than Employee Claim's own broad create grant, since this records a company-paid bill, not a personal reimbursement).
Frontend: New /direct-expenses (Accountant/Admin list with Category/Project/Status/date filters and a row-level Reverse action), /direct-expenses/new (Category/Title/Link-to-Project-or-overhead/Amount/Date/GST + Payment Mode/Bank Account fields, Save as Draft / Post two-button pattern that chains createDraft then post in one click), /direct-expenses/[id] (DraftEditor/PostedView/CancelledView split with Documents on both draft and posted views, matching Vendor Bill's own fuller pattern rather than Employee Claim's simpler one). The "Employee Expenses" nav group is renamed to "Expenses" and gains a "Direct Expenses" item.

Tests: 11 new tests in direct-expenses.integration-spec.ts -- draft create/update/cancel with the mandatory-reason check; creator-only ownership rejecting a different actor from updating/cancelling someone else's draft; three GST-validation rejections (negative, exceeds amount, credit without an invoice); an inactive expense category and an inactive bank account both rejected; post's journal shape for a plain expense (2 lines) and one with a non-zero flat GST credit (3 lines); confirming post is genuinely not creator-restricted; idempotent replay on post plus a reused-key-different-request rejection; rejecting a re-post of an already-posted expense; reverse's journal shape and a stale-version-conflict rejection; list/getDetail filtering and cross-org isolation. ## Phase 6, Slice 6.8: Subscription & Server recurring cycles (closes Phase 6)

```text
Date: 2026-08-25
Status: Implementation complete AND fully verified — 80 new tests across six new/rewritten spec files, typecheck/lint/build clean across all 9 workspaces (checked twice, once per part), two independent fresh-database migration replays confirmed the full history (45 migrations) applies cleanly from zero, and the full apps/api integration suite (445/445 across 33 files) plus the full apps/worker integration suite (19/19 across 3 files) both passed clean. No live browser pass performed yet, same disclosed gap as every prior slice.
Phase: Phase 6 — Procurement and expense (Slice 6.8, the last named Phase 6 vertical — this closes Phase 6 in full).
Task: A first plan was rejected in a 23-point architectural review before any code was written (full text preserved verbatim in this session's own record) — it kept the confirmed scope (Subscription + Server ship together, both reuse Vendor/Party, one shared cycle engine, Record Payment always creates+posts a Direct Expense) but demanded a materially safer design across FK integrity, period boundaries, calendar-safe stepping, automatic generation, full lifecycle state machines, atomic payment recording, reversal-safe history, GST, TDS, Server cost allocation, and Debit/Credit Card posting. Two follow-up questions were then asked and answered directly: extend Direct Expense with a full CGST/SGST/IGST engine (not the flat field ADR-087 shipped with), and extend Direct Expense's own posting with optional TDS (not leave it out of scope) — both accepted, making a Direct Expense upgrade a genuine prerequisite (Part A) before the recurring-cycle engine itself (Part B). The revised plan was approved as written.
Part A backend: Rewrote DirectExpensesService's GST model around taxableAmount/gstRatePercent/supplyType (manual pick) with computed cgst/sgst/igstAmount frozen at draft time (same split math as Vendor Bill's computeLines), gated by a new itcEligible flag; added flat tdsAmount/tdsRatePercent posting to the existing TDS_PAYABLE account; added a new CardAccount master (Debit Card posts through its linked BankAccount unchanged, Credit Card posts to its own payable ledger instead, settled later via a new small CardSettlementsService capped at the card's live outstanding balance) and a new optional vendorId. New createAndPostDraft fuses create+post into one atomic call for RecurringCyclesService's own use.
Part B backend: New RecurringCyclesService (apps/api/src/subscriptions/) is the shared engine behind both SubscriptionsService and ServersService — real subscriptionId/serverId FKs with a DB CHECK (not a polymorphic sourceId), real periodStart/periodEnd/dueDate columns, calendar-safe stepping reusing addMonthsClamped from the existing OPEX engine, a rolling 12-month ensureHorizon anchored to the last cycle's actual dueDate (not sequenceIndex, so it survives a recurrence change's renumbering), explicit pause/resume/cancel/skipCycle lifecycle commands, a preview-then-apply recurrence-change flow that only ever supersedes future-pending cycles, and an atomic-in-effect recordCyclePayment/reverseCyclePayment pair backed by an append-only RecurringCyclePayment link table. A new worker job (apps/worker/src/recurring-cycle-horizon.ts) tops up every active parent's horizon every 6 hours plus once on boot, mirroring the existing payment-alert-opener job's exact JobRun-wrapped shape.
Frontend: New /card-accounts admin page (Debit/Credit Card CRUD + Settle action); new /subscriptions and /servers trees (list with forecast tiles, new/page.tsx creation forms, [id]/page.tsx with a cycle table, inline Record Payment modal, and Pause/Resume/Cancel actions) sharing a new RecordCyclePaymentFields component; Server's detail page adds an associated-projects manager. The "Direct Expenses" form gained GST/TDS/Card fields via a new shared form-fields.tsx.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (two migrations: 20260824130000_phase6_08a_direct_expense_gst_tds_card, 20260825090000_phase6_08b_subscription_server_recurring_cycles), packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (15 new permissions, DEBIT_CARD/CREDIT_CARD payment modes, CREDIT_CARD_PAYABLE account group, SUBSCRIPTION/SERVER entity types; catalog count 102 -> 117). apps/api/src/direct-expenses/direct-expenses.service.ts (rewritten), new apps/api/src/card-accounts/card-settlements.service.ts, new apps/api/src/subscriptions/{recurring-cycle-schedule.util.ts, recurring-cycles.service.ts, subscriptions.service.ts, servers.service.ts}, apps/api/src/organization/legal-entity.service.ts (CardAccount CRUD), apps/api/src/milestones/milestone-schedule.util.ts (exported addDaysUTC), apps/worker/src/{main.ts, recurring-cycle-horizon.ts}. packages/contracts/src/ports/{direct-expense.port.ts (rewritten), legal-entity.port.ts, card-settlement.port.ts (new), subscription.port.ts (new)} and matching routers, wired into context.ts/router.ts/ports/index.ts/index.ts. apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{direct-expenses,card-accounts,subscriptions,servers}/**, apps/web/src/components/record-cycle-payment-fields.tsx (new), documents-tab.tsx, packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}.
Migrations: both hand-curated from prisma migrate diff (the same three unrelated pre-existing constraints/indexes always excluded), applied via prisma migrate deploy + prisma generate, plus two small mid-stream column additions (requires_card_source on payment_modes, sequence_index on recurring_cycles) applied directly since neither migration had shipped yet.
Tests: 23 rewritten direct-expenses.integration-spec.ts tests, 16 new organization.integration-spec.ts Card Account tests, 9 new card-settlements.integration-spec.ts tests, 10 new recurring-cycle-schedule.util.spec.ts unit tests (every calendar case the review named: 17-Aug Monthly, 24-Nov Yearly, 31-Jan Monthly anchor restoration through Feb, 29-Feb-2028 leap Yearly), 18 new subscriptions.integration-spec.ts tests, 4 new worker recurring-cycle-horizon.integration-spec.ts tests. One real bug caught by this suite and fixed before shipping: recordCyclePayment's idempotent-replay path was rejecting a retry once the cycle had already flipped to PAID — fixed with its own top-level idempotency-key guard, checked before the state check, mirroring every other mutating command in this codebase. roles.integration-spec.ts catalog count corrected 102 -> 117.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-088 (the full 23-point review's resolution; reopening ADR-087's flat GST/no-TDS simplifications by direct user decision; the FK-pair+CHECK cycle design; the date-anchored-not-sequenceIndex-anchored horizon continuation; the append-only payment-link design; the Debit-vs-Credit-Card posting split; the bounded Server charge-to-project design; the project.read_all-derived PM scoping reuse).
Known issues: no live browser pass performed yet (no browser-automation tool available this session). Explicitly deferred and disclosed: automated credit-card statement import/reconciliation and due-date reminders, a spend-by-company dashboard, new provider masters, implicit project-cost splitting, bulk historical cycle generation.
Next task: This closes docs/11's own Phase 6 deliverable list in full. Phase 7 (Inventory and costing) is next.
```

## Project Financial Stats (cross-cutting slice, project-scoped)

```text
Date: 2026-08-25
Status: Implementation complete AND fully verified — 7 new tests in a new project-financials.integration-spec.ts, getExpenseLedger's existing suite extended to 5 tests, typecheck/lint/build clean across all 9 workspaces, no migration needed (no schema change), the full apps/api integration suite passed clean on rerun (452/452 across 34 files), the full apps/worker integration suite passed 19/19 (untouched by this slice), and all cross-package unit tests passed 14/14 tasks.
Phase: Cross-cutting (not tied to a numbered roadmap phase) — CEO/Admin financial visibility on the Projects module, requested directly rather than drawn from docs/11's own sequence.
Task: User asked for project-wise and client-wise financial stats, colored, server-computed, visible on the Projects list (per row) and a Project Detail tab. Four decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion: extend the expense ledger to a 3rd category (Direct Expense); omit Profit/Margin (ADR-086 precedent); a new "Financials" tab additive to "Expense"; a new dedicated project.read_financials permission. The first plan was rejected in a detailed 7-section correctness review (the third such review this codebase has seen) that caught a GST-basis mismatch (Project.value is taxable-value, invoice totals are GST-inclusive), a "Settled" vs. "Received" conflation, gross-vs-recognized-expense confusion (recoverable Input GST must be excluded from cost), an unreconciled Expense-tab-vs-new-tab numbers risk, and a too-loosely-exposed bulk aggregation method. The revised plan closed every point and was approved as written. Client-wide aggregation (the Client detail page's own disclosed "Receivable" gap) was explicitly deferred to an immediate follow-up, not built in this slice.
Backend: New, bounded ProjectFinancialsService (apps/api/src/projects/) computes five figure groups per project — Contract Value (Excl. GST), Billing (grossInvoiceValue/debitNoteAdjustments/creditNoteAdjustments/netBilled), Collections (cashReceived plus one bucket per AllocationDeductionType value), Receivable (invoiceOutstanding/unreleasedRetentionAndHold/clientReceivable, all deliberately unclamped — a negative value is a real credit balance), and Recognized Expense (purchaseExpense/employeeClaimExpense/directExpense, each excluding recoverable Input GST via one shared recognizedExpenseAmount function reused across VendorBillLine/DirectExpense/EmployeeExpenseClaim). It depends on ProjectsService one-way (reusing getDetail/list for authorization, never reimplementing project-visibility). listFinancialSummaries takes a required, caller-supplied projectIds batch and independently re-derives+intersects the actor's own permitted set rather than trusting the frontend. getExpenseLedger gained a 3rd category (Direct Expense, POSTED rows) alongside Purchase/Reimbursement.
Frontend: Projects list gains 3 new columns (Net Billed, Client Receivable, Recognized Expense) fetched for only the currently-visible page's rows. Project Detail gains a new "Financials" tab (5 KPI cards + Billing/Collections and Project Expenses breakdown cards, every deduction type shown as its own row rather than combined client-side). The existing Expense tab is relabeled "Spend" (kept at its existing gross, per-transaction totals) so it's never confused with the new tab's ITC-excluded Recognized Expense figure under a similar label. New MoneyText component (packages/ui) — a thin tone-aware (positive/negative/warning/neutral) wrapper around an already-formatted amount, the one component genuinely missing from docs/15 section 21's catalogue (StatTile is already fulfilled by the existing KpiCard).
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/seed.ts (project.read_financials permission + role grants; catalog count 117 -> 118). apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (catalog count corrected). ## Phase 7, Slice 7.2: Stock ledger, weighted-average engine, Vendor Bill inventory variant

```text
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: COMPLETE and verified against a live database (verification closed 2026-08-31, later the same day). The migration is applied (46 of 46, `prisma migrate status` clean), the seed is re-run and verified (122 permissions, INVENTORY ledger account present), 12 unit tests pass, and all 15 inventory integration tests pass -- the first time any of them had ever executed. A full apps/api integration regression run also passes: 36 spec files, 485 tests, zero failures, which independently clears the five specs this slice had edited only mechanically for the new constructor arity and line field. `turbo run typecheck` clean, 14/14.
Phase: Phase 7 -- Inventory and costing (Slice 7.2). Promotes TDR-007 (inventory costing = weighted average) from the pending technical-decision table to Accepted, as docs/12 requires when the phase begins.
Task: Three deliverables in one slice because they are one mechanism: the append-only stock ledger, the weighted-average valuation engine, and the Vendor Bill inventory variant docs/06 has specified since Phase 0 but ADR-075 shipped without ("no inventory variant", explicitly disclosed).
Backend: New pure inventory-valuation.util.ts is the whole of the arithmetic -- applyReceipt blends a purchase cost into the running average, applyConsumption issues at the ruling average -- shared by every caller and the tests, same discipline as the OPEX generator. Full depletion is special-cased to carry out the ENTIRE remaining value rather than quantity x rate; without it an uneven rate strands paise against zero quantity forever and stock value permanently stops reconciling to the INVENTORY account, failing Phase 7's own exit criterion silently. InventoryMovement is append-only and stores the balance it produced, so a past consumption's cost stays reproducible and the sum of every item's latest balanceValueAmount IS the INVENTORY account balance -- the exit criterion holds by construction. Backdated movements are refused outright (they would invalidate every later stamped rate); the correction path is reversal then re-entry, same as a posted journal. InventoryLedgerService.recordMovement is the single writer, always taking SELECT ... FOR UPDATE on the item row first, and always taking the caller's own tx so a movement commits with the event that caused it. New InventoryReceipt covers only what the bill path cannot: OPENING (Dr INVENTORY / Cr OPENING_BALANCE_EQUITY, reusing ADR-071) and DIRECT (Dr INVENTORY / Cr ACCOUNTS_PAYABLE_VENDOR).
Vendor Bill change: VendorBillLine gains a nullable inventoryItemId and expenseCategoryId drops NOT NULL, with a CHECK enforcing exactly one. approve() branches the debit account -- but the costDebit formula is shared, not duplicated, because docs/06's two tables debit the identical figure (taxable value plus ineligible tax) and differ only in the account. The per-unit cost handed to the ledger is that same costDebit divided by quantity, so the movement can never disagree with the journal line. No second journal is posted (docs/04:464); the movement records quantity and running cost only, inside the same transaction as the status flip.
Frontend: New /inventory (Stock + Stock Receipts tabs, stock-value KPI summed across the whole filtered set not the visible page), /inventory/[itemId] (separate stock-in and stock-out ledgers with running balance; a date filter narrows rows but never changes the reported on-hand figure, per docs/04:463), /inventory/receipts/new. The Vendor Bill line editor gains an Expense/Stock line-type switch. New nav item and InventoryIcon.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (InventoryMovement, InventoryReceipt, InventoryReceiptLine, three enums, VendorBillLine alter), seed.ts (INVENTORY account + INVENTORY_ASSETS group, 2 permissions, INVENTORY_RECEIPT entity type). New apps/api/src/inventory/{inventory-valuation.util.ts, inventory-valuation.util.spec.ts, inventory-ledger.service.ts, inventory.service.ts, inventory.module.ts, inventory.integration-spec.ts}. apps/api/src/vendor-bills/{vendor-bills.service.ts, vendor-bills.module.ts}. New packages/contracts/src/ports/inventory.port.ts and routers/inventory.ts plus the five wiring touchpoints; ports/vendor-bill.port.ts and routers/vendor-bills.ts for the either/or line. apps/web/src/app/(platform)/inventory/** (new), vendor-bills/{shared.tsx, new/page.tsx, [id]/page.tsx}. packages/ui/src/components/{icons.tsx, nav-items.ts}. Five existing integration specs updated mechanically for the new constructor arity and line field. roles.integration-spec.ts catalog 120 -> 122.
Migrations: one -- 20260831110000_phase7_02_inventory_movements_receipts (46 total). NOT APPLIED. Produced by an offline `prisma migrate diff --from-schema-datamodel <pre-change snapshot> --to-schema-datamodel <schema>` file-to-file diff because both the primary and shadow databases were unreachable; a side effect is that it required none of the three phantom-line exclusions every prior migration carries. Five hand-added CHECK constraints: exactly-one expense/inventory line target, one-direction movements, non-negative balances, DIRECT-needs-vendor/OPENING-forbids-vendor, positive receipt line quantity.
Tests: 12 unit tests passing (true weighted blending asserting 175 rather than the 150 a naive mean of rates gives; the full-depletion residue case proving three issues of 333.3333 post back exactly 1000.00; a long mixed receipt/issue sequence asserting value-on-hand always equals received minus issued with zero rounding drift; over-issue refusal; every guard). 15 integration tests, ALL PASSING against the live database (the earlier note said 17; the file actually contains 15 -- corrected by direct count), covering both posting shapes, the CHECK-backed vendor rules, non-stock/inactive/backdate rejection, idempotent replay not doubling stock, reversal including the consumed-stock refusal, ledger reads, the date-filter rule, cross-org isolation, and an explicit INVENTORY-account-equals-stock-value assertion.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-092; TDR-007 promoted to Accepted with rationale (weighted average is the only candidate needing no per-unit or per-lot identity -- FIFO/LIFO need lot tracking docs/00 excludes, specific identification is meaningless for consumables and cabling).
Known issues: (1) RESOLVED -- the database-verification gap is closed. The .env host settled at 192.168.1.5 and is reachable; both Phase 7 migrations are applied there, the seed is re-run, and the full integration suite passes. (2) RESOLVED -- Slice 7.1's migration and seed are confirmed applied to the current host alongside 7.2's. (3) OPEN -- references/ui/ is still absent, so /inventory and its ledger were built from docs/04 section 14's contract text, not from inventory-list.html / inventory-detail.html / inventory-add-stock.html. (4) OPEN -- no live browser pass. (5) OPEN -- SHADOW_DATABASE_URL still cannot authenticate, so no fresh-database replay from zero has been performed for either Phase 7 migration; both were verified against the live database only.
Next task: Slice 7.3 -- inventory consumption (Dr Project Material Cost / Cr Inventory at weighted average), which closes Phase 7's first exit criterion. (The database-access prerequisite named here originally is now done.)
```

## Phase 7, Slice 7.3: Inventory Consumption (project material cost)

```text
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Complete and verified against a live database. Migration applied (47 total), seed re-run and verified, 10 new integration tests passing (25 in the file, up from 15), full apps/api integration regression clean at 36 files / 495 tests, typecheck and lint clean 14/14. UI verification still BLOCKED by the absent references/ui/ bundle (see Known issues) -- the same standing Phase 7 blocker, not a new one.
Phase: Phase 7 -- Inventory and costing (Slice 7.3). Closes ADR-092's forward reference and makes the CONSUMPTION movement type reachable by a real document for the first time.
Task: Session opened by checking Phase 7's actual state rather than assuming it. Found 7.1 complete, 7.2 implementation-complete but never executed against a database. The database host had settled at 192.168.1.5 and was reachable, so the FIRST work of this session was closing 7.2's verification gap, not writing new code: prisma migrate status clean at 46/46, seed re-run (122 permissions, INVENTORY account present), the 15 inventory integration tests run for the first time ever -- all passing -- plus a full apps/api regression (36 files, 485 tests, zero failures) that independently cleared the five specs 7.2 had edited only mechanically. docs/11, docs/12 (ADR-092) and docs/13's own 7.2 entry were corrected in place: status, the "17 tests" figure (the file has 15, corrected by direct count), and the resolved/still-open Known issues. Only then did 7.3 begin.
Backend: The slice forced a change to the posting engine itself, and that is the most consequential thing in it. Every earlier posting caller computes its amount from user input, posts the journal on its own connection, then opens a transaction for its business rows. A consumption's amount IS the ruling weighted average read under the item's FOR UPDATE lock -- and full depletion carries out the entire residual balance rather than quantity x rate -- so posting first would have to guess it, and a concurrent receipt landing in between would leave an Inventory credit the movement disagrees with, silently breaking Phase 7's own exit criterion. PostingService.postJournal is therefore refactored into a thin wrapper over a new postJournalWithin(tx, input) holding the whole engine; all ten existing callers are byte-for-byte unaffected (the 25 posting integration tests were run against the refactor before anything was built on it). InventoryLedgerService gained lockAndGetBalance, extracted from recordMovement which now calls it, so the consumption can read the figure it is about to post under exactly the lock the movement will be written under. New InventoryConsumption/InventoryConsumptionLine shaped exactly like the receipt pair. A project is REQUIRED (docs/06 names the debit "Project Material Cost"; a consumption with no project has no posting rule), a terminal-status project is refused reusing ProjectsService's own TERMINAL_STATUSES, and the consumption must post in the project's own legal entity. Both journal legs carry the project dimension. Lines naming the same item are chained through one shared computeConsumptionLines helper, not valued independently -- independently, two lines for one item could each "succeed" into a negative balance and neither would be recognised as the depleting one. Items are locked in sorted id order so concurrent multi-item consumptions cannot deadlock. After writing the movements the service asserts their total equals the total it posted. Reversal returns each line at its OWN stored unit cost, not today's average -- a later receipt legitimately moves the average, and returning at the new one would silently change stock value.
Frontend: New "Project Issues" tab on /inventory (project + status filters), an "Issue to Project" header action, and a new /inventory/consumptions/new entry screen. Every figure on that screen -- per-line value, on-hand, ruling rate, document total -- comes from a new server-side previewConsumption query; the browser does no valuation arithmetic at all, because it genuinely cannot reproduce the depletion rule. The preview returns every line even when one is short, with a per-line shortfall message, so all problems show at once; Post is disabled while any line is short. Legal entity is derived from the chosen project and shown read-only.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (InventoryConsumption, InventoryConsumptionLine, inventory_consumption_id on InventoryMovement, four back-relations), seed.ts (inventory.consume permission, PROJECT_COSTS group, PROJECT_MATERIAL_COST account, INVENTORY_CONSUMPTION entity type). apps/api/src/accounting/posting.service.ts (postJournalWithin extraction, PostJournalInput, resolveAccountingPeriod takes a client). apps/api/src/inventory/{inventory-ledger.service.ts, inventory.service.ts, inventory.integration-spec.ts}. packages/contracts/src/ports/inventory.port.ts, routers/inventory.ts, ports/index.ts, index.ts. apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts (catalog 122 -> 123). apps/web/src/app/(platform)/inventory/{shared.tsx, page.tsx} and new consumptions/new/page.tsx.
Migrations: one -- 20260831130000_phase7_03_inventory_consumption (47 total). Two tables, one nullable column, six indexes, four composite org-consistency FKs, two hand-added CHECKs (positive line quantity mirroring the receipt side; reversal fields settable only together with REVERSED status). Hand-curated from prisma migrate diff --from-url, excluding the same three known phantom lines every prior migration excludes. Applied via prisma migrate deploy + prisma generate; post-apply diff confirmed to contain only those three phantom lines.
Tests: 10 new integration tests, all passing -- the blended 175 rate with both journal legs carrying the project dimension; the full-depletion residue proving 1000.00 in comes back out as exactly 1000.00 across two documents; two lines for one item chaining into a single running balance and the follow-up being refused; over-issue refused with nothing partial written; closed project, mismatched legal entity, non-stock item, inactive item, backdated date and zero quantity all refused; idempotent replay not issuing stock twice plus IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT; reversal returning stock at its original 40.00 after a later receipt moved the average, with double-reversal and empty-reason refused; preview matching the posted figure and flagging a shortfall without throwing; the item ledger naming the consumption and its project; cross-org isolation. The file's existing INVENTORY-account-equals-stock-value assertion now runs over real posted consumptions -- its exclusion was narrowed to the document-less raw ledger movements the suite writes by hand.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-093. ADR-092 and its docs/11 and docs/13 entries corrected from UNVERIFIED to verified.
Known issues: (1) references/ui/ is still absent (the standing Phase 7 blocker), so the new tab and entry screen were built from docs/04 section 14's contract text and the existing receipts/new structure, NOT from inventory-add-consumption.html. (2) No live browser pass. (3) SHADOW_DATABASE_URL still cannot authenticate, so no fresh-database replay from zero. (4) inventory.consume is granted to Accountant only, matching inventory.receive's posture since it posts to the ledger -- Project Manager can see issues but not raise them. Disclosed as a deliberate boundary, and the natural thing to revisit if site teams are to issue stock themselves.
Next task: Slice 7.4 -- salary import (Dr Salaries & Wages Expense / Cr Salary Payable), which begins Phase 7's employee-costing vertical.
```

## Phase 7, Slice 7.4: Salary Import (opens the employee-costing vertical)

```text
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Complete and verified against a live database. Migration applied (48 total), seed re-run and verified (126 permissions, SALARIES_WAGES_EXPENSE and SALARY_PAYABLE present under two new groups), 12 new integration tests passing, typecheck and lint clean 14/14.
Phase: Phase 7 -- Inventory and costing (Slice 7.4). Opens the second of the phase's three verticals.
Task: docs/04 section 13's employee-salary-upload.html: "import monthly gross salary only. Validate month uniqueness, duplicates, unmatched employees and totals; preview before atomic commit. Preserve source file/checksum and audit batch." docs/06's "Salary project costing" gives the posting. docs/16 section 7.6 settles the authority question -- "imported monthly gross salary is the historical costing source" -- so Employee.displayedSalaryAmount is explicitly NOT what project cost multiplies.
Backend: preview and commit share ONE resolveRows method. That is the whole point of "preview before atomic commit": the preview is only trustworthy if it is literally the commit's own validation run without writing, otherwise the two can disagree. Five row outcomes -- MATCHED / UNMATCHED / DUPLICATE / INACTIVE / INVALID_AMOUNT -- all classified in one pass rather than throwing on the first, so a whole sheet is fixed in one go. The whole month posts or none of it does: one unresolved row blocks the batch, because a partial import would understate a month of cost and afterwards be indistinguishable from a month where those people earned nothing. One POSTED batch per legal entity per month, enforced by a PARTIAL unique index (WHERE status = 'POSTED') -- a plain composite unique would block a corrected re-import forever once a batch was reversed. The month is normalised to the first of the month on write. The journal carries one debit and one credit line PER EMPLOYEE, both stamped with the employeeId dimension JournalLine has had since ADR-065 with nothing ever populating it; a single summed pair would balance equally well but leave "what is still owed to this person" unanswerable. sourceChecksum hashes the parsed rows, not the file bytes -- the same sheet re-saved has different bytes and identical content. Gross salary only; a zero gross is allowed (unpaid leave is real), negative is refused by a CHECK.
Frontend: New /salary-imports list with a Reverse action, and /salary-imports/new -- legal entity, month, accounting date, CSV upload or paste, a server-validated preview grid with per-row status and problem, four KPI tiles, an already-imported alert, a non-blocking note naming active employees absent from the sheet, and a Commit action disabled until every row matches. CSV is parsed in the browser and only parsed rows are sent, so there is no file endpoint and nothing to clean up if the commit fails; .xlsx is deliberately not parsed (it would need a parser dependency this project has not taken) and the screen says to export to CSV first. New "Employee Costing" nav group.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (SalaryImport, SalaryImportLine, SalaryImportStatus, two back-relations), seed.ts (3 permissions, 2 account groups, 2 ledger accounts, SALARY_IMPORT entity type). New apps/api/src/salary-imports/{salary-imports.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}; app.module.ts/main.ts wiring.
