## Phase 7, Slice 7.5: Monthly Employee Allocation and Project Manpower Cost

```text
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Complete and verified against a live database. Migration applied (49 total), seed re-run and verified (129 permissions, 6 allocation targets), 10 new unit tests and 10 new integration tests passing, typecheck and lint clean.
Phase: Phase 7 -- Inventory and costing (Slice 7.5). Closes two of Phase 7's three exit criteria: "submitted employee allocations total 100%" and "project cost uses imported gross salary".
Task: Two roadmap deliverables in one slice, because the second has no schema and no rules of its own -- project manpower cost is a read over the first times the salary import. docs/04 section 13 MERGEs employee-effort-entry.html and employee-contribution-entry.html into one canonical screen and RETIREs the second ("no independent percentage truth").
Backend: The design is decided by two sentences of docs/06. Allocation "moves cost analytically ... without changing total salary expense", so it posts NO journal and every amount is computed on read -- which means a corrected salary import instantly corrects every project cost derived from it, with nothing to re-run and no second figure that can drift. And "rounding remainder is assigned deterministically so allocations equal gross salary", which is why one pure allocation-cost.util.ts exists: three equal shares of 100.00 each round to 33.33 and the month is a paisa short, so left alone every project cost report is quietly light and the shortfall grows with head count. The remainder goes to one deterministically chosen line (largest share, ties by earliest sequence) rather than being spread -- one line carrying one extra paisa is explainable, a distribution that shifts paise as unrelated lines change is not. Exactly 100% to submit, meant literally (99.9999 is refused). A line targets either a project or a non-project AllocationTarget, never both and never neither (DB CHECK plus an explicit message), and the same target may not appear twice. AllocationTarget is the 16th generic MasterTypeKey and deliberately carries no ledger account, since allocation posts nothing. Manpower cost splits the WHOLE month first and keeps only the project lines -- splitting just the project lines would decide the remainder against a different denominator, so two projects' figures would depend on which report ran. Only SUBMITTED months count, with the excluded-draft count returned so a low total is explainable. Project scoping reuses ProjectsService.list verbatim (ADR-058).
Frontend: /employee-allocations -- month picker, four KPI tiles, a summary grid listing every active employee INCLUDING the ones who have not started (the people the screen exists to find), and a drawer holding the entry grid with per-line server-computed amounts, a rounding-remainder note, Save Draft / Submit, and a reason-required Reopen. /project-manpower-cost -- month range and project filters, three tiles, a per-project-per-month grid, and a warning naming the draft employee-months excluded.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (AllocationTarget, EmployeeMonthlyAllocation, EmployeeAllocationLine, EmployeeAllocationStatus, three back-relations), seed.ts (3 permissions, 6 allocation targets, EMPLOYEE_ALLOCATION entity type). New apps/api/src/employee-allocations/{allocation-cost.util.ts, .util.spec.ts, employee-allocations.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}; app.module.ts/main.ts wiring. New packages/contracts/src/ports/employee-allocation.port.ts and routers/employee-allocations.ts; allocation_targets added to the four generic-master touchpoints. masters.integration-spec.ts master-type count 15 -> 16; roles.integration-spec.ts catalog 126 -> 129. New apps/web/src/app/(platform)/{employee-allocations,project-manpower-cost}/page.tsx; packages/ui AllocationIcon/ManpowerCostIcon and two nav items.
Migrations: one -- 20260831170000_phase7_05_employee_allocation (49 total). Three tables, one enum, three hand-added CHECKs. Nothing existing altered.
Tests: 10 unit tests on the pure util (exact 100 across awkward fractions; one ten-thousandth short refused both ways; a clean split producing no remainder line; the three-way 100.00 split where the remainder genuinely fires; a tie broken identically regardless of array order; a seven-way 14.2857 split losing nothing across four gross figures; a zero gross allocating to zero; a part-filled draft previewing at what it says). 10 integration tests (unstarted month already carrying its imported gross; any day collapsing to one record; server-computed per-line shares; both directions of not-exactly-100 refused; submit locking editing with the reopen reason audited; every invalid-line shape refused; the summary listing NOT_STARTED employees; manpower cost counting only submitted months with excluded drafts reported; an explicit reconciliation that project cost plus bench equals the imported total exactly; inverted range refused; cross-org isolation).
Decisions added/changed: ADR-095.
Known issues: references/ui/ still absent. No live browser pass. contribution-dashboard.html's MERGE (a KPI/trend surface over the same records) deliberately not built -- the Allocation Summary already carries the data, and a second visual surface with no new facts is deferred rather than invented. No export action, so employee_allocation.export / project_cost.export are not in the catalogue.
Next task: Slice 7.6 -- introducer agreement, accrual, payment and allocation, the last named Phase 7 deliverable.
```

## Phase 7, Slice 7.6: Introducer Agreement, Accrual, Payment and Allocation (closes Phase 7)

```text
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Complete and verified against a live database. Migration applied (50 total), seed re-run and verified (132 permissions, three new ledger accounts), 14 new integration tests passing, typecheck and lint clean.
Phase: Phase 7 -- Inventory and costing (Slice 7.6). The last named deliverable in the phase.
Task: Gives Introducer (Phase 3.2, deliberately minimal) and Project.introducerId (Phase 3.4) their first financial meaning. docs/04 section 15 names three screens; docs/06 gives exactly two rules -- accrual is Dr Introducer Commission Expense / Cr Introducer Payable, and payment "follows vendor-style payable settlement with TDS where applicable. Excess payment posts to Introducer Advance Receivable."
Backend: Three documents in one aggregate and one service, because they are one obligation seen at three moments -- what is owed, when it became a liability, how it was settled; splitting them would put the payable balance, which needs all three, in no service at all. The agreement posts nothing (an entitlement is not an event). Accrual is a deliberately manual act: the moment commission is earned is a business judgement docs/00 does not fix, and a trigger here would invent an accounting policy. An unknown entitlement stays NULL rather than zero -- a percentage agreement on a project with no value has no derivable ceiling, and returning 0.00 would silently cap every accrual at nothing; cumulative accrual is capped whenever the entitlement IS derivable. The over-payment rule is the load-bearing one: the excess debits Introducer Advance Receivable, never a negative payable, so a payment with no allocations at all is valid and posts a pure advance. Journal legs with a zero amount are omitted rather than posted (the posting engine rejects a both-or-neither line). An allocation may only touch an accrual of the same introducer and legal entity, may not exceed its outstanding balance, and may not name it twice. Allocations are read through their payment's status and never deleted, so reversing a payment restores its accruals with the allocation history intact; reversing an accrual is refused once any of it is paid, since the payment settled a liability that would no longer exist.
Frontend: One /introducer-fees page with four tabs -- Balances (five separate figures plus payable, four KPI tiles), Agreements (with an Add drawer whose rate/amount field follows the chosen basis, and a per-row Accrue action), Accruals (with Reverse), Payments (with Reverse) -- plus a dedicated /introducer-fees/payments/new screen showing live Gross / Allocated / Advance / Net tiles and a per-accrual allocation grid capped at each accrual's outstanding balance. New "Introducer" nav group.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (IntroducerAgreement, IntroducerAccrual, IntroducerPayment, IntroducerPaymentAllocation, two enums, four back-relations), seed.ts (3 permissions, 3 ledger accounts, 3 entity types). New apps/api/src/introducer-fees/{introducer-fees.service.ts, .module.ts, .integration-spec.ts}; app.module.ts/main.ts wiring. New packages/contracts/src/ports/introducer-fee.port.ts and routers/introducer-fees.ts plus wiring. roles.integration-spec.ts catalog 129 -> 132. New apps/web/src/app/(platform)/introducer-fees/{shared.tsx, page.tsx, payments/new/page.tsx}; packages/ui IntroducerFeeIcon and nav item.
Migrations: one -- 20260831190000_phase7_06_introducer_fee (50 total). Four tables, two enums, six hand-added CHECKs including the payment's own arithmetic (gross = tds + net, advance never exceeding gross). Nothing existing altered.
Tests: 14 new integration tests, all passing -- entitlement from both bases with the duplicate-pair refusal and a second introducer on the same project allowed; a valueless percentage project leaving the entitlement null; every basis/figure mismatch, inactive introducer and out-of-range rate refused; the accrual journal with the project dimension; cumulative accrual capped when derivable and uncapped when not; idempotent accrual replay plus IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT; a TDS settlement with all four legs asserted by account and the payable moving by exactly the allocated amount; an 8,000 payment against a 5,000 accrual splitting into payable and advance; a pure advance posting only two lines; over-allocation, over-payment of an accrual, a duplicate allocation line, a foreign introducer's accrual and TDS above gross all refused; payment reversal restoring the accrual while keeping the allocation row; accrual reversal refused while paid and permitted once the payment is reversed; five separate balance figures reconciling; cross-org isolation.
Also fixed here, found by this slice's own full-suite regression rather than by its code: PostingService.getLedgerActivity had no total order -- it sorted only by accountingDate, and several postings share a date routinely, so Postgres could return them in any order and the running balance the Chart of Accounts "View Ledger" drawer shows could differ between two reads of the same ledger. The posting spec's running-balance test passed in isolation and failed under load, which is what surfaced it. Now ordered by accountingDate, then journalEntry.createdAt, then id (UUID v7, so the tiebreak is chronological). Read-path only: no schema, posting or API change.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-096.
Known issues: references/ui/ still absent (standing Phase 7 blocker) -- all three screens were built from docs/04 section 15's contract text, not from introducer-all.html / introducer-fee-add.html / introducer-fee-payment.html. No live browser pass. An agreement has no deactivate or edit action yet (isActive exists and is honoured but nothing flips it) -- disclosed as deferred rather than half-built. No GST on introducer commission: docs/06 gives it no tax treatment, so none is invented.
Next task: Phase 7's named deliverables are all built. Remaining before the phase can be declared closed to this project's own standard: restore references/ui/ and compare every Phase 7 screen against its paired 1440x900 screenshot (docs/16 section 5), and a live browser pass.
```

## Phase 7 integration pass — connecting the modules to the app

```text
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: Complete and verified against a live database. Two migrations applied (53 total), seed re-run with a compensation backfill, 14 new/rewritten integration tests plus 5 new worker tests, full apps/api regression at 540 tests, typecheck and lint clean 14/14.
Phase: Cross-cutting follow-up to Phase 7, requested directly by the user after reviewing the finished phase. Recorded here rather than as a numbered slice, like the Project Financial Stats work before it.
Task: The user's criticism was correct and specific: the eight Phase 7 features are siloes that connect neither to existing entities nor to each other, and salary in particular should derive from a maintained history rather than a monthly upload. Exploration quantified it -- EVERY Phase 7 route had zero inbound links from any non-Phase-7 page (/products, /inventory, /salary-imports, /employee-allocations, /project-manpower-cost, /introducer-fees: 0 each, against 8 for /projects). And one number was actually wrong: docs/04:229 requires project cost to roll up salary allocation, inventory consumption and introducer accrual; Phase 7 built all three and none reached the Spend or Financials tabs, so a project could carry material, manpower and commission cost on its journal lines and report none of it. ADR-086 had deferred exactly those categories "until their own source modules exist" -- so this was a scheduled follow-up coming due, not new scope. Planned in plan mode with three forks confirmed by the user: keep /products and /inventory separate with cross-links; have the worker auto-generate the monthly salary draft; do all five workstreams in one pass, ordered A -> B -> E -> D -> C.
Backend (A -- the wrong-number fix): getExpenseLedger gains MATERIAL / MANPOWER / INTRODUCER and ProjectFinancialsService gains the same three in recognized expense. Server and Subscription deliberately do NOT become categories -- their cycle payments post through DirectExpensesService and already arrive as DIRECT_EXPENSE, so labelling them again would split one source or double-count. All three enter at face value, passing ZERO to recognizedExpenseAmount rather than bypassing it so the "one shared formula" invariant holds: consumption is already net of recoverable ITC, manpower and commission carry no GST. Queried directly through Prisma, never by injecting the services -- EmployeeAllocationsService already injects ProjectsService, so the reverse edge would be circular (exactly ADR-086's own reasoning for VendorBillsService, now load-bearing a second time). Manpower reuses allocateSalary and splits the WHOLE month before keeping project lines, so the ledger, the Financials tab and the Manpower report cannot disagree.
Backend (E -- two real defects found while planning): introducer_fee.read/create/pay had all landed on PROJECT_MANAGER instead of ACCOUNTANT (a seed-edit anchor matched the wrong block in ADR-096), so a Project Manager could DISBURSE MONEY and the Accountant could not see introducer fees at all. Deeper: the seed's role-permission loop was additive only, so re-running it after correcting a definition changed nothing and a mis-grant was permanent in any database that had run the buggy seed. The loop now reconciles, scoped to system roles only. roles.integration-spec.ts had asserted only the SIZE of the permission catalog and never which role holds which key -- a count cannot catch a misplaced grant. New per-role assertions were confirmed to fail against the pre-fix seed before passing after it.
Backend (C -- salary, discharging ADR-047's deferral): new EmployeeSalaryRevision with effectiveFrom/effectiveTo, one open revision per employee enforced by a hand-added PARTIAL unique index. Revising closes the open row the day before the new period starts and inserts a new one -- the two abut without overlapping, so a past month's figure is never rewritten. Employee.displayedSalaryAmount survives as a read-cache with the revision service as its ONLY writer, and leaves the edit form. SalaryImport gains DRAFT with a nullable journalEntryId; the enum change ships in its own migration because Postgres will not let a new enum value be used in the transaction that adds it, and the new CHECKs reference 'DRAFT' directly. commit now PROMOTES a draft rather than creating from transient input, which makes "the rows reviewed are the rows posted" true by construction rather than by running the same validation twice. A new hourly worker job drafts the previous month automatically -- only the month that has ENDED, skipping any month that already has a batch, and refusing to create an empty one.
Frontend (B and D): three new project tabs (Material, Manpower, Introducer), each a thin project-scoped mount of a procedure that already accepted a projectId filter, following security-instrument-tab.tsx. The Introducer tab finally reads Project.introducerId -- populated since Phase 3.4, previously used by nothing -- pre-filling the agreement form and warning on a mismatch. /project-manpower-cost removed (163 lines, one query on the same router as allocations, no useRouter, faked pagination, zero links) into a tab on /employee-allocations. The one-item "Introducer" nav group folded into CRM beside the Introducers master. introducer-fees/page.tsx (571 lines holding four tab components) split into sibling *-tab.tsx files. expense-tab.tsx's three hand-written category chips refactored to a descriptor map before adding three more. The app's only hardcoded KpiCard ("Central Store") replaced with a real count. A salary-import detail drawer added -- committed rows were unviewable and journalEntryId was carried but rendered nowhere. Six cross-links added.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (EmployeeSalaryRevision, DRAFT status, nullable journalEntryId), seed.ts (permission fix, reconciling role sync, compensation backfill). apps/api/src/projects/{projects.service.ts, project-financials.service.ts, projects.integration-spec.ts, project-financials.integration-spec.ts}; new apps/api/src/employees/employee-salary-revisions.{service,integration-spec}.ts; apps/api/src/salary-imports/salary-imports.{service,integration-spec}.ts; apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts; new apps/worker/src/salary-draft-generator.{ts,integration-spec.ts} plus main.ts registration. packages/contracts ports and routers for projects, project-financials, employees, salary-imports. apps/web: three new project tabs, the introducer split, the allocations hub, the rewritten salary-import flow, the employee Salary section, nav changes, one deleted route.
Migrations: two -- 20260831210000_phase7_int_salary_import_draft_enum (the enum alone, deliberately) and 20260831210001_phase7_int_salary_revisions (table, nullable column, one partial unique for the open revision, one for the DRAFT month, a period CHECK, a journal-by-status CHECK, and a REPLACED reversal-fields CHECK that would otherwise reject every draft). 53 total. Section A needed no migration at all -- every figure it surfaces was already on the journal, merely unread.
Tests: 2 new ledger tests (35/35 in projects.integration-spec.ts) covering the three streams' shapes and link types, the null manpower link, and REVERSED/DRAFT exclusion; extended financials assertions (11/11) with recognizedProjectExpense rising 198,720 -> 229,720; 2 new role-grant tests (9/9); 5 new salary-revision tests (period abutment, every refusal, month-boundary resolution, isolation); 9 rewritten salary-import tests for the draft lifecycle; 5 new worker tests. Full apps/api regression: 540 tests, 528 passed, 11 skipped, 1 contention-flake failure that passes clean on rerun (ADR-077's documented class, confirmed twice).
Decisions added/changed: ADR-097 (integration + the two defects), ADR-098 (salary revisions, DRAFT batches, the worker). ADR-047's deferral is discharged; ADR-094's closing line about displayedSalaryAmount never being a costing input is reversed; ADR-086's four deferred categories are closed; ADR-096's permission grant is corrected.
Known issues: references/ui/ is still absent, so all new screens and every consolidation decision were made from docs/04's contract text rather than the prototypes -- the standing Phase 7 UI blocker applies here too. No live browser pass. No Profit/Margin tile: cost is complete now, but docs/04's report rules forbid a blended profit without a named accounting basis, which belongs to Phase 8. A salary revision's own effectiveFrom cannot be corrected after the fact -- a different operation from a raise, needing rules about already-posted months, disclosed as deferred. The / dashboard remains a 34-line stub from Phase 2B, out of scope here.
Next task: Phase 8 (Reports and controls) can now consume a correct project cost. Before Phase 7 is formally closed, the references/ui/ bundle still needs restoring for the prototype comparison, plus a live browser pass and a fresh-database migration replay.
```

## Phase 7 flow pass — one Costs tab, guided empty states, and paying the salary payable

```text
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: Implementation complete. One migration applied (54 total), seed re-run, 6 new integration tests, full apps/api regression at 546/546 across 40 files, typecheck and lint clean 14/14. The production next build could NOT be completed -- see Known issues; it is not this work's doing.
Phase: Second cross-cutting follow-up to Phase 7, requested directly by the user after using the previous pass.
Task: The user's verdict on the integration pass was "everything is unambiguous and separated -- rethink again and connect all and simple flow", plus two questions: manpower is salary-based so the project should not ask for it at all, and how do these costs actually get paid. Both were right, and the second exposed a genuine hole rather than a missing screen.
What was actually wrong: the project detail screen had 14 tabs, three of them cost, and each cost stream had an invisible chain of prerequisites the app never narrated. The live database showed it exactly -- July 2026 salary POSTED at 60,000, no allocation ever started, so the Manpower tab read 0.00 with no link and no explanation, indistinguishable from "this project has no manpower cost". Material was worse: its Issue Stock button threw away the project the user had come from. The governing principle adopted, and worth reusing: a zero meaning "nothing happened" and a zero meaning "you have not finished setting up" must never look the same.
The hole: SALARY_PAYABLE was only ever CREDITED, by the salary import, and nothing debited it. "What we owe staff" grew every month with no way to record paying it. docs/00 excludes payroll processing (attendance, leave, computing pay) and docs/06 is silent on settlement -- on review an oversight, not an exclusion, since settling an already-recorded liability is a bank disbursement of the same shape as the vendor and introducer payments that exist. Confirmed with the user before building.
Frontend (ADR-099): three cost tabs become one Costs tab with Material and Introducer sections, each gating on its own read key. manpower-tab.tsx is DELETED per the user's call -- the server rollup stays because it is correct and free when empty, but the UI stops presenting an unfilled step as pending work, so the Financials tab hides a zero Manpower row and the Spend ledger's Manpower chip appears only if allocations exist. material/manpower/introducer survive as ?tab= aliases resolving to costs, because a dead ?tab= was one of the bugs being fixed. A new shared components/next-step.tsx names the missing step in every empty state, detecting it through procedures that already existed (inventoryItems.getCount, unused by the web app until now, and inventory.listStock with inStockOnly) -- no backend surface added. /inventory/consumptions/new now accepts ?projectId= and REPLACES the picker with read-only text plus a hidden Form.Item when seeded; a disabled AntD Select would not submit its value.
Frontend (the reported bug): /journal-entries/null was caused by salary-imports/shared.tsx declaring journalEntryId: string while the contract says string | null since ADR-098, so both Journal buttons navigated unconditionally; the same file's two-state ternary rendered a DRAFT batch as "Reversed" in red. Fixed by ALIASING the local row types to the contract types in the three Phase 7 shared files, which turns the next such drift into a compile error. 43 web files still hand-redeclare row types and only 6 import them from @zitaware/contracts; the other 40 are deliberately untouched -- none is broken and converting them is a separate change.
Backend (ADR-100): new SalaryPayment mirroring IntroducerPayment. Dr Salary Payable (gross) / Cr TDS Payable (tds) / Cr Bank (net), omitting any zero leg -- TDS is withheld rather than paid, moving between liabilities without leaving the bank. Cumulative payments may not exceed the month's gross; only a POSTED import can be paid, and the bank account must belong to the entity that recognised the cost. Outstanding is DERIVED (gross minus POSTED payments), never stored, so reversing a payment restores the balance without editing a figure. Deliberately per-month, not per-employee: the import already posts one payable line per employee stamped with employeeId, so per-employee settlement stays possible, but salary leaves the bank as one transfer. New permission salary_import.pay, Accountant only, catalog 132 -> 133.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (SalaryPayment, SalaryPaymentStatus, three back-relations), seed.ts (the new key in the catalogue and in ACCOUNTANT_PERMISSIONS). apps/api/src/salary-imports/salary-imports.{service,integration-spec}.ts; apps/api/src/rbac/roles.integration-spec.ts. packages/contracts: salary-import.port.ts (SalaryPaymentSummary, paidAmount/outstandingAmount on the summary, payments on the detail), routers/salary-imports.ts (pay, reversePayment, listPayments), both index files. apps/web: new projects/[id]/costs-tab.tsx and components/next-step.tsx; deleted material-tab.tsx and manpower-tab.tsx; introducer-tab.tsx now exports IntroducerSection; projects/[id]/page.tsx (TAB_ALIASES, two fewer tab keys); expense-tab.tsx, financials-tab.tsx, inventory/consumptions/new/page.tsx, salary-imports/{page.tsx,shared.tsx}, inventory/shared.tsx, introducer-fees/shared.tsx.
Migrations: one -- 20260901090000_phase7_int_salary_payment (the enum, the table, four FKs, four indexes, and two hand-added CHECKs: gross = tds + net with all three non-negative and gross positive, plus the reversal-fields pairing). 54 total. ADR-099 needed no migration at all.
Tests: 6 new -- the three journal legs asserted BY ACCOUNT; a partial payment leaving exactly 55000.50 with the next rupee refused and the exact remainder accepted; a zero-TDS payment posting two lines and no empty third; idempotent replay returning the same payment with the paid total unmoved, and a reused key with different amounts refused; reversal restoring the outstanding balance with the payment/reversal pair netting to zero on the bank; and refusals for a DRAFT, a reversed month, another entity's bank account, a date before the month, and TDS above gross. Role assertions updated to 133 with salary_import.pay required on Accountant and forbidden on Project Manager. Whole apps/api suite 546/546, clean.
Also fixed here: a stray untracked packages/database/_check.ts scratch file was failing the database package's no-console lint rule; removed.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-099 (the flow pass), ADR-100 (salary payment). docs/14 gained a dated update on the build blocker below.
Known issues: THE PRODUCTION next build CANNOT CURRENTLY BE COMPLETED ON THIS MACHINE. It fails at "Error occurred prerendering page /_global-error" with "Cannot read properties of null" -- the upstream Next.js 16.x bug docs/14 already documents, but the documented retry mitigation no longer works: 11 consecutive failures. This was bisected rather than assumed: with the ENTIRE working tree stashed, a clean HEAD checkout with a deleted .next fails identically 3/3, so the application is not the variable. A stale .next cache and memory pressure were both ruled out. typecheck, lint and the test suites are therefore the gate on this machine until the upstream issue closes or the artifact is built elsewhere. Separately: references/ui/ is still absent, so the Costs tab and the pay modal were built from docs/04 and docs/15 text with no paired screenshot (there is no prototype screen for salary payment at all). No live browser pass. The three runtime bugs reported this week were all invisible to typecheck, lint, build and the backend suite; aliasing the row types closes the type-drift class only -- the allowlist and schema-max classes still need a browser test that loads a screen and asserts its controls populate. Playwright is already a dependency with only its browser binary missing.
Next task: restore references/ui/ for the prototype comparison, add the browser smoke test, and resolve the build blocker (or build the artifact on a machine where it does not reproduce) before Phase 7 is formally closed. Phase 8 remains unblocked -- project cost is complete and correct.
```

## Salary payment becomes per-employee (ADR-101)

```text
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: Complete and verified against a live database. One migration applied (55 total), 6 payment tests rewritten, full apps/api regression at 546/546, workspace typecheck and lint clean 23/23.
Phase: Correction to the same day's ADR-100, found by the user opening the screen.
Task: The user asked "here may be coming whole staff, so why one reference?" -- and was right. ADR-100 settled a month as one anonymous figure with one reference, defending per-month settlement on the premise that salary leaves the bank as a single transfer. A payroll run is several credits with several UTRs, so there was nowhere to record them, and the modal could not even show WHICH people the amount covered.
What changed: new SalaryPaymentLine (employee, amount, TDS, per-line reference). The journal now posts Dr Salary Payable / Cr TDS Payable / Cr Bank ONCE PER EMPLOYEE, each leg stamped with employeeId -- the mirror of the per-employee credit the import already posted, so what is owed to one person is answerable from both sides of the ledger. Each employee's own outstanding is enforced rather than only the month's total: if A is owed 40,000 of a 105,000 month, paying A 40,000.01 is refused even though the month has 85,000 of headroom, which a month-level cap would have allowed. Header totals are derived server-side from the lines, never sent, so a client total can never disagree with its own rows. A zeroed row means "not paying this person now" and is filtered, not rejected.
UI: the pay modal is now the month's roster -- every employee with something outstanding, pre-filled at their balance, with their own Paying / TDS / Reference columns and a running "leaving the bank" figure. The drawer's payments table expands to show who was paid under which reference, and the month's employee rows gain a per-person "Still Owed" column.
Files changed: packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma (SalaryPaymentLine, two back-relations). packages/contracts/src/ports/salary-import.port.ts (SalaryPaymentLineRow/Input, paidAmount+outstandingAmount per import line, lines on the payment summary, reshaped pay signature), routers/salary-imports.ts (lines input, shared amountSchema), both index files. apps/api/src/salary-imports/salary-imports.{service,integration-spec}.ts. apps/web/src/app/(platform)/salary-imports/page.tsx.
Migrations: one -- 20260901120000_phase7_int_salary_payment_lines (table, two FKs, three indexes, one CHECK enforcing that TDS is withheld FROM the amount rather than added to it). 55 total.
Tests: the 6 payment tests rewritten around lines. Six journal legs for a two-employee payment, every line carrying its employee, each person's reference on their own row; the per-employee cap asserted against exactly the case a month-level cap would miss; zero-TDS still omitting the empty leg; idempotent replay and key reuse; reversal restoring both the month and each employee with the payment/reversal pair netting to zero on the bank; refusals for a DRAFT, a reversed month, an employee not in the month, the same employee twice in one transfer, a wrong-entity bank account, a date before the month, and TDS above the amount settled.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-101, which REVERSES ADR-100's decision (6). That boundary was defended as "per-employee entry would be data entry with no decision in it" -- wrong on both counts: each row carries a decision, and the per-month alternative could not represent a real payroll run.
Known issues: the production build blocker in docs/14's 2026-09-01 update is unchanged and unrelated. No live browser pass; references/ui/ still absent, and there is no prototype screen for salary payment at all. This is the fifth defect this week found by using a screen rather than by any automated check -- the argument for the Playwright smoke test is now quite strong.
Next task: unchanged -- restore references/ui/, add the browser smoke test, fix the shadow database, and resolve the build blocker before Phase 7 can be closed.
```

## App-wide UI corrections (ADR-102)

```text
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: Complete. No migration, no backend change. Workspace typecheck and lint clean 23/23; dev server compiles and serves every touched route.
Phase: Cross-cutting UI fixes, all four reported by the user in one sitting while using the app.
Task: Four defects, each app-wide rather than local to the screen where it was noticed, and each invisible to typecheck, lint and the 546-test backend suite.
(1) React key spread: AntD Form.List hands each row a { key, name, ... } object, and eight files spread the whole object into Form.Item while also setting key={field.key} on the wrapper -- so React warned on every render and reported "two children with the same key, 0". Destructured the key out at the map in all eight files; dropped key from the three sub-component prop types that demanded it. Swept the app to confirm no other spread carries a key.
(2) Sidebar scroll: the aside and the nav inside it BOTH had overflow-y-auto, so the shell could show two scrollbars and scrolling the outer one dragged the brand header out of view. The nav now owns the scroll alone, and gets a thin low-contrast scrollbar -- the default OS scrollbar drew a light track against the dark --ui-ink panel. Scoped to that element so everything else keeps the platform scrollbar.
(3) Form width: 28 pages lose their max-w-* container caps. This OVERRIDES docs/15 section 9.2, on the product owner's explicit call after being shown the trade-off. The caps had been applied without centring, so every form sat hard-left with several hundred pixels of dead space down one side; section 9.3's two-column grid already halves most fields. docs/15 was amended in the same change rather than left contradicting the code. Right-aligned totals panels keep their narrow width -- they are summaries, not input.
(4) Pointer cursor: Tailwind v4's Preflight stopped forcing cursor: pointer on buttons, so EVERY button in the app had lost it -- the hamburger and avatar were just where it was spotted. Only two places in the codebase had compensated with an explicit class. One global rule now covers button, [role=button], label[for] and summary, with not-allowed on disabled to match AntD. An audit found no clickable div/span/tr anywhere, so one rule genuinely covers the app.
Files changed: packages/ui/src/components/{AppShell,SidebarNavigation}.tsx; apps/web/src/app/globals.css; 28 form pages; 8 Form.List files; 3 sub-component prop types; docs/15-ui-design-system.md section 9.2.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-102, which amends docs/15 section 9.2 and removes its "Do not stretch narrow forms across the full data-page width" instruction.
Known issues: these are visual changes with no screenshot comparison, because references/ui/ is still absent. The full-width decision departs from the paired prototypes deliberately and is recorded in docs/15 so a later comparison does not silently revert it. Counting these four, NINE defects this week were found by using a screen rather than by any automated check; the Playwright smoke test remains unbuilt and remains the obvious answer.
Next task: unchanged -- restore references/ui/, add the browser smoke test, fix the shadow database, and resolve the production build blocker before Phase 7 can be closed.
```

## Money-flow scenario test and the stock double-count fix (ADR-103)

```text
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Complete and verified against the live dev database. No migration. Scenario spec 31/31, projects 35/35, project-financials 11/11; apps/api typecheck and lint clean.
Phase: Cross-phase verification of the whole project money flow (Phases 4-7).
Task: The user asked to test every figure "by digits", with multiple clients, multiple projects and every scenario, and chose both an automated test and demo data to click through.
What changed: a reusable scenario, apps/api/src/testing/money-flow-scenario/. build.ts drives the REAL services end to end inside a fresh organization: 3 clients (one intrastate, two interstate), 5 projects (one with no activity, one carrying a CAPEX build AND a 36-month OPEX O&M schedule whose pool does not divide evenly), CAPEX and OPEX invoices on both tax paths, one receipt settling an OPEX and a CAPEX invoice together, a voided invoice, a debit and a credit note, six receipts including multi-invoice receipts and every deduction type, partial hold/retention releases, a reversed receipt, three vendor bills (one never approved) with a stock line, a payment request and vendor payment with TDS, a direct stock receipt and two consumptions at weighted average, four direct expenses (one overhead, one draft), three claims (one reimbursed, one draft), two salary months with a mid-period revision and a paid July, allocations including a three-way split that leaves a paisa, and an introducer agreement, two accruals and a payment. expected.ts holds every expected figure WORKED OUT BY HAND with its arithmetic beside it -- never copied from a run -- including all 23 summary fields for five projects, three client rollups, the Spend tab, the Manpower report and every ledger balance (debits 27,55,084.55 = credits). purge.ts deletes an organization by reading its tables from information_schema and retrying foreign-key blocks, so no hand-kept delete list can go stale. scripts/load-money-flow-demo.ts (npm --workspace api run demo:money-flow) loads the same scenario into its own DEMO-MONEY-FLOW organization with a maker and a checker login, and writes the expected-numbers sheet from the same constants, so the sheet and the test cannot disagree.
Defect found and fixed: a vendor bill's stock lines were counted in project cost twice -- as purchase expense at approval and again as material when issued. See ADR-103.
Files changed: apps/api/src/projects/{project-financials,projects}.service.ts (the fix); new apps/api/src/testing/money-flow-scenario/{expected,build,purge,sheet}.ts and money-flow.integration-spec.ts; new apps/api/scripts/load-money-flow-demo.ts; apps/api/package.json (demo:money-flow script, tsx declared as a dev dependency it was already using through packages/database); package-lock.json; docs/10, docs/12 (ADR-103), docs/14.
Migrations: none.
Tests: 31 new -- every project's CAPEX and OPEX pool totals, both OPEX schedules (every installment but the last at the rounded-down share, the paisa remainder on the last, invoice dates clamped to month-end, e.g. 31 Jul -> 30 Sep -> 30 Jun 2029), the CAPEX+OPEX project's events and schedule side by side, every project's and client's Financials figures, the portfolio list agreeing with each project, each project's Spend categories and total, invoice and note GST splits (CGST+SGST and IGST never together), both salary batches and the payment, the Manpower report from submitted months only, the paisa remainder landing on the largest share, the trial balance, every ledger account's balance with nothing posted elsewhere, and the screens reconciling to the ledger (receivable = sum of outstanding, stock value = INVENTORY, salary batches = salary expense, manpower + bench + drafts = salary expense).
Decisions added/changed: ADR-103.
Known issues: integration specs run against the shared dev database (DATABASE_URL), not a separate test database -- an interrupted run can leave an MFLOW-* organization behind, which the next successful run does not remove. A credit/debit note always posts on today's date, so the scenario creates financial years up to the one containing today. The "all-stock bill gets no Spend row" branch is untested. No browser pass of the demo organization. Found but NOT fixed: a milestone's billing status is never derived -- MilestonesService.toMilestoneSummary returns the literal 'NOT_INVOICED' whatever has been invoiced, and no screen shows it, although docs/03 calls it derived, docs/05 lists PARTIALLY_INVOICED/FULLY_INVOICED and docs/04:262 requires it on screen. The scenario therefore does not assert it.
Next task: click through the demo organization against the generated sheet; unchanged beyond that -- restore references/ui/, add the browser smoke test, and resolve the production build blocker before Phase 7 can be closed.
```

## Milestone billing status, a separate integration test database, and test-date fixes (ADR-104, ADR-105)

```text
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Complete and verified. No migration. apps/api integration 41 files / 580 tests and apps/worker 4 / 24 (three consecutive runs) green on the new zitaware_crm_test database; apps/api unit tests green; api, worker and web typecheck and lint clean.
Phase: Fixes for the open issues found by the money-flow scenario, at the user's request (the deployment issues were excluded by the user).
Task and what changed:
(1) Milestone billing status is now derived from FINALIZED invoices instead of the literal NOT_INVOICED (ADR-104): per CAPEX event, per OPEX milestone with "n of m invoiced", and per installment, on the API and on the milestone screen.
(2) Integration tests run on TEST_DATABASE_URL (zitaware_crm_test), migrated and seeded automatically, never on the development database (ADR-105). Worker integration files run one at a time; the outbox-dispatcher spec parks other organizations' due events; the alert-opener and salary-draft specs delete notifications before outbox events.
(3) receipts, project-financials and credit-debit-notes specs no longer break on 1 January 2027: a helper adds financial years through today, since credit/debit notes post on today's date (ADR-070).
(4) The money-flow scenario adds an all-stock vendor bill on P2 and asserts it produces no Spend PURCHASE row and no purchase cost — the ADR-103 branch that was untested. Stock, Inventory, Input GST and Accounts Payable expectations updated by hand (trial balance 27,99,662.31).
(5) The Spend tab's note now says a vendor bill's stock lines appear under Material, not Purchase.
(6) The scenario purge deletes in foreign-key order, so it no longer logs constraint errors.
Not changed, by design: credit/debit notes posting on today's date is ADR-070's confirmed rule and matches GST (a note is reported in the period it is issued); the Spend tab's GST-inclusive document basis is deliberate and already explained on the tab. Both were raised in the previous review as possible issues; neither is a defect.
```

## Phase 7 close-out: build fixed, live browser pass, shadow database (ADR-106)

```text
Date: 2026-09-11
Status: Complete and verified, except the UI screenshot comparison, which waits on references/ui/ being restored. Phase 7 is therefore still not formally closed; Phase 8 has not started, by the user's instruction to start it only once everything before it is complete.
Phase: Phase 7 close-out.
What changed:
(1) Production build (ADR-106). The /_global-error prerender crash blamed on Next.js since ADR-043 was NODE_ENV=development exported into `next build` from a sourced .env. apps/web's build script now pins NODE_ENV=production; the next.config.ts workaround, the retry loop and every "upstream bug" claim are gone. Each package's and the worker's tsbuildinfo now lives in dist/, so turbo caches it with the output it describes and the stale-package-build trap of 2026-09-11 cannot recur.
(2) Release runbook: the seed step is added (it is the only thing that adds new permissions), and docs/14's Seeding section now says what the seed really changes.
(3) Live browser pass. New `npm --workspace web run test:e2e` (Playwright, Chromium at 1440x900) opens every page as a logged-in user and fails on the not-found page, a login bounce, "Could not load", a never-ending "Loading...", or an uncaught exception; console errors are recorded per page. It shares one page inventory (scripts/route-inventory.cjs) with check:routes, so both always cover the same pages.
(4) Shadow database. SHADOW_DATABASE_URL named a role (zitaware) that does not exist on the server; it now uses the same login as DATABASE_URL (local .env only). A replay of all migrations from zero into it produces the schema apart from the three documented hand-written objects Prisma always proposes dropping (chk_cities_state_matches_district, uq_districts_id_state_id, idx_invoices_payment_alert_id).
```

## Phase 8, Slice 8.7: Payables Aging, Vendor Payments, Expenses, Reimbursements, Employee Advances (ADR-116)

```text
Date: 2026-09-15
Status: Complete and verified against the live development database.
Phase: Phase 8 — Reports and controls.
What changed:
(1) Payables Aging (as of): vendor bills and direct stock receipts less vendor-payment settlements standing on the date, aged from the document date, by vendor, with the amount already approved for payment; reconciled to Accounts Payable — Vendor.
(2) Vendor Payments (period): payments and reversals with their bill allocations, cash, typed TDS/GST-TDS and settled amounts; reconciled to the payable, bank and tax postings the payments made.
(3) Expenses (period): non-stock vendor-bill lines, direct expenses and approved claims — gross, recoverable GST, recognized — by category and project; reconciled to the expense accounts those documents posted to.
(4) Reimbursements (period): claims with their status on the end date, approved/reimbursed/awaiting totals and by employee; reconciled to Employee Advance Receivable movements.
(5) Employee Advances (period): opening + advances − claims adjusted + reimbursements ± other = closing per employee; reconciled to Employee Advance Receivable.
(6) LedgerControlService.movementBySource — movement on accounts counting only the postings of given business events and their reversals.
(7) New permission report.payables.read (catalogue 147).
```

```text
Tests: 3 unit tests for the action grouping. 3 new scenario tests — audit activity totals equal to the audit table, three recorded sign-in failures reported as repeated, exports counted, reversals/voids/lock/unmatch among sensitive events and routine postings not, an empty future period; exceptions at the end of the scenario with hand-derived counts (all ten ledger checks agree; bank clear; 14 TDS exceptions = 13 + the 26AS credit the 8.5a CSV test leaves unmatched; 2 overdue deposits 8,000; AN-1002 awaiting approval 29,500; Installation milestone 6,00,000 past due; Karan's August 55,555.55 unallocated; 5 open); an early as-of date with nothing yet open for billing or salary, and the PDF export. Integration: scenario 125/125, rbac 18/18. Browser on the demo organization: both pages render, 10/10 ledger checks agree, no console errors. Workspace typecheck, lint and tests 32/32 (--concurrency=2). Dev database seeded (150); demo organization rebuilt.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-120.
Known issues: see ADR-120. references/ui/ still absent.
Phase 8 summary: 8.1–8.9b deliver 30 reports across receivables, profitability, GST, TDS, cash and bank, payables, operations and controls, TDS certificates and deposits, bank statement reconciliation with period locks, and the main Dashboard — each report naming its basis, reconciling to the ledger where its totals map to control accounts, and exporting to Excel/PDF with an audit event.
Next task: Phase 9 — Hardening and go-live (performance/load testing, backup and restore test, security review, production monitoring and alerts, historical data import tools).
```

## Session lifetimes: 1-hour access token, 1-day refresh token (ADR-122)

```text
Date: 2026-09-29
Status: Implemented. Unit tests cover expiry detection and a single refresh from the proxy. A full hour-long browser stay was not waited out.
What changed: ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_MINUTES=60 and REFRESH_TOKEN_TTL_DAYS=1. The access cookie expires with the JWT. An expired access token is renewed from the refresh cookie (once) by the Next.js proxy and by in-page tRPC calls, then the original request is retried. A failed refresh still redirects to login.
Files changed: .env, .env.example, packages/config/src/env.ts, packages/contracts/src/ports/auth.port.ts, packages/contracts/src/routers/auth.ts, apps/api/src/identity/identity.service.ts, apps/web/src/lib/session-tokens.ts, apps/web/src/proxy.ts, apps/web/src/server/provider.tsx, apps/web/src/server/session.ts, docs/12, docs/13.
Decisions added/changed: ADR-122.
```

## Dates show as DD/MM/YYYY everywhere

```text
Date: 2026-10-26
Status: Implemented.
What changed: Every date the user sees or types is DD/MM/YYYY, as docs/15 requires; API values stay ISO YYYY-MM-DD. formatDate shows DD/MM/YYYY and formatDateTime shows DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm; the audit log, audit activity report and user detail pages now use them instead of the browser's own format. The 58 browser date inputs became a shared DateInput (antd DatePicker showing DD/MM/YYYY and holding the ISO string), because a browser date input always follows the viewer's locale. All date pickers default to DD/MM/YYYY through the antd ConfigProvider locale; month pickers keep their month format. Picker handlers that sent the picker's display string to the API now send isoDate/isoRange of the picked date, so they still send YYYY-MM-DD. The invoice PDF, report PDF/Excel date columns and the report "As of / Period" line show DD/MM/YYYY.
```

## Production correction: Invoice 0001 posting reversed

```text
Date: 2026-10-26
Status: Done on production.
What happened: Invoice 0001 (Rs 59,65,759.68; IGST charged at 36% because 18 was typed into the per-component GST rate) was finalized at 08:12 UTC and then deleted directly in the database instead of being voided. Its journal stayed posted, so Receivable Aging, Pending Invoices and GST Net showed ledger differences of Rs 59,65,759.68 and Rs 15,79,171.68.
Fix: its journal entry (01a17744-9dd8-782c-8491-3ac63e60e6e3) was reversed with the app's own PostingService.reverseJournal, dated 2026-10-26, actor zitawaresolutions@gmail.com, reason recorded, idempotency key fix-orphan-invoice-0001-2026-10-26. Reversal entry 01a1784a-7134-7201-bfc4-b5c33eff57d5.
Verified: Accounts Receivable ledger Rs 1,39,131.44 equals finalized invoices outstanding; Output IGST ledger equals invoice IGST (0.00).
Follow-up: raise the invoice again from the project's open payment alert with GST rate 9 (18% IGST). Never delete invoices in the database; void them.
```

## Partial billing of events and installments (ADR-156, slice 2 of ADR-155) and the failing integration tests

```text
Date: 2026-10-27
Status: Implemented. One migration (20261027120000_invoice_partial_billing), not yet applied to the dev or production database.
What changed: A CAPEX event or OPEX installment can be billed across several finalized invoices; one invoice per source may be open at a time. Billing status is by amount (NOT / PARTIALLY / FULLY_INVOICED) on events, installments and milestones. Finalize and void keep each source's alert and installment status in step with what is invoiced: billed in part, the alert stays open for the rest; a void reopens it. The milestone's approved amount stays the only hard cap. The invoice form shows what is already invoiced and suggests what is left; the payment alert and Pending Invoices' billing-pending figures show what is left; the milestone tab gains billing by amount on installments and a Raise Invoice action wherever something is left. An installment billed in part cannot be skipped.
```

## Users logged out on their own (ADR-158)

```text
Date: 2026-10-27
Status: Implemented. No migration. New optional setting REFRESH_REUSE_GRACE_SECONDS (default 60).
What changed: When the access token expired, the web server and the browser (or two tabs) could renew the session at the same moment; the second renewal reused a just-rotated refresh token, which was treated as theft and revoked the whole login. A token rotated within the grace window now gets its own successor, and the previous access token still verifies for that window; logout ends the whole login. Also fixed: the web server shared one in-flight refresh across all users, so one user's renewal could answer another's request; it is now keyed by refresh token.
Files changed: packages/config/src/env.ts; apps/api/src/identity/{identity.service.ts, identity.integration-spec.ts}; apps/web/src/lib/{session-tokens.ts, session-tokens.test.ts}; .env.example; docs/12 (ADR-158), docs/13, docs/14.
Tests: identity integration 11/11 (3 new: concurrent refreshes both succeed; a just-rotated token and its access token are accepted without revoking; a logged-out login is not reopened; the reuse test now moves the rotation past the window); web unit 39/39 (new: two users' refreshes are never shared). Full API integration 707/707; workspace typecheck and lint 23/23.
Known issues: a user idle for a day (REFRESH_TOKEN_TTL_DAYS=1) is still logged out, by design. No browser pass.
```

## Session renewal never worked: hourly logout (ADR-158 follow-up)

```text
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Fixed. Web only, no API or database change.
What changed: The renewal request (auth.refresh) sent the body `null`. The API's JSON body parser is strict (objects and arrays only), so every renewal, from the browser and from the Next.js proxy, was answered 400 "null is not valid JSON". That 400 carried no CORS headers, so the browser reported it as a CORS block. No session was ever renewed: every user was sent to /login when the 60-minute access token expired (the dev database shows 0 renewals in 14 days). The request now sends `{}`.
How it was verified: a real headless-Chromium run (Playwright) against the test database with a 1-minute access token. Before the fix, every renewal was blocked; after it, renewals return 200 and the user stays signed in across expiry. A forced logout (sessions revoked) sends the user to /login?next=..., and signing in again lands on the page they were on, by full page load and by in-app navigation.
Files changed: apps/web/src/lib/{session-tokens.ts, session-tokens.test.ts}; docs/13.
Tests: web unit 39/39.
Note: on this machine `next dev` with Turbopack answered 404 for every page, while `next dev --webpack` worked; this did not affect the fix, and the user's own dev server serves pages normally.
```

## Bank account: the masked number is derived, not typed

```text
Date: 2026-10-28
Status: Implemented. No migration.
What changed: The Add/Edit Bank Account form no longer has an "Account number (masked)" field. ## Bank account and card dropdowns show the full number
```

```text
Date: 2026-11-04
Status: Done. Database, contracts, API and web typecheck clean; lint clean; API unit tests (141) and web unit tests (49) pass.

- Card accounts table and edit drawer display the full card number with groups of four when present.
```
